.
Then

(4.34)

\displaystyle x(t)-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)\cong O^{-}_{3}(1)(t-t_{p})^{\frac{1+\sigma_{-}}{\sigma_{-}}}\qquad{\rm as}\ t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}.

For
(x,t)\in\Omega_{-}
near
(x_{p},t_{p})
,

(
4.30
)
with
\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}

holds for
\delta>1-(Q_{3}^{-})^{-1}
;

For
(x,t)\in\Omega_{+}
near
(x_{p},t_{p})
,

(4.35)

\displaystyle\qquad u(x,t)-c\cong\begin{cases}C_{\gamma_{+}}(O^{+}_{4}(1))^{-\varrho}\,\big|x-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)\big|^{\varrho}&{\rm for}\ c=b,\\[5.69054pt]
-C_{\gamma_{-}}\big|x-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)\big|^{\gamma_{-}}&{\rm for}\ c<b,\end{cases}

where
\varrho=\max\{\gamma_{-},\gamma_{+}\}
and
b:=\underline{D}_{+}\Phi(x_{0})
.

In the above,

\mathcal{C}(x_{0})
,
\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(x_{0})
,
and
\underline{{\rm D}}_{+}\Phi(x_{0})

are given by
(
3.2
)
–
(
3.4
)
;

constants
O_{1}(1)
and
O_{2}(1)
are given by

(4.36)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}O_{1}(1):=\overline{C}_{\gamma}\Big(\frac{(\overline{C}_{\gamma})^{2}Q_{+}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\sigma}}|Q_{+}-1|=\overline{C}_{\gamma}\Big(\frac{(\overline{C}_{\gamma})^{2}Q_{-}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\sigma}}|Q_{-}-1|,\\[8.53581pt]
O_{2}(1):=\frac{(1+\gamma)(1-\gamma)+\sigma}{(1+\gamma)(1-\gamma)+\sigma(2+\sigma)}\frac{|\overline{C}_{\rho}|}{\overline{C}_{\gamma}}\Big(\frac{(\overline{C}_{\gamma})^{2}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma}}\Big)^{\frac{1+\sigma+\rho}{\sigma}},\end{cases}

where
Q_{\pm}
are given by

(4.37)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}Q_{+}=\frac{\gamma\sigma}{(1+\gamma)(1+\sigma)}\frac{(\lambda_{1})^{\gamma}(1+\lambda_{1})}{1+(\lambda_{1})^{\gamma}}+\frac{1+\gamma+\sigma}{(1+\gamma)(1+\sigma)},\\[8.53581pt]
Q_{-}=\frac{\gamma\sigma}{(1+\gamma)(1+\sigma)}\frac{1+\lambda_{1}}{\lambda_{1}(1+(\lambda_{1})^{\gamma})}+\frac{1+\gamma+\sigma}{(1+\gamma)(1+\sigma)}\end{cases}

for the unique real root
\lambda_{1}
of the following equation
:

\displaystyle\gamma\sigma(1+\lambda)(\lambda_{0}-\lambda^{1+\gamma+\sigma})=(1+\gamma+\sigma)\lambda(1+\lambda^{\gamma})(\lambda^{\sigma}-\lambda_{0})\qquad{\rm with}\ \lambda_{0}:=\textstyle\frac{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}};

and constants
O^{\pm}_{3}(1)

with
Q^{\pm}_{3}

and
O^{\pm}_{4}(1)
are given by

(4.38)

\displaystyle O^{\pm}_{3}(1):=\overline{C}_{\gamma_{\pm}}(1-Q^{\pm}_{3})(Q^{\pm}_{3})^{\frac{1}{\sigma_{\pm}}}\bigg(\frac{(\overline{C}_{\gamma_{\pm}})^{2}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{\pm}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{\sigma_{\pm}}},\qquad\,\,\,Q^{\pm}_{3}=\frac{1+\gamma_{\pm}+\sigma_{\pm}}{(1+\gamma_{\pm})(1+\sigma_{\pm})},

(4.39)

\displaystyle O^{\pm}_{4}(1):=\begin{cases}\frac{\overline{C}_{\gamma_{\pm}}}{\overline{C}_{\gamma_{\mp}}}\quad&{\rm if}\ \gamma_{\pm}(1+\alpha)<1,\\[2.84526pt]
1+{\rm sgn}(C_{\gamma_{\pm}})\frac{\overline{C}_{\gamma_{\pm}}}{\overline{C}_{\gamma_{\mp}}}\quad&{\rm if}\ \gamma_{\pm}(1+\alpha)=1,\\[2.84526pt]
1\quad&{\rm if}\ \gamma_{\pm}(1+\alpha)>1.\end{cases}

See also

Fig.

4.3
and
Fig.

2.8
.

Figure 4.3.
The shock curve
x(t)

for the case that
t_{p}^{+}=t_{p}^{-}\in(0,\infty)
.

Proof.

We complete the proof case by case accordingly.

Case I.
In this case,
t_{p}^{+}=t_{p}^{-}\in(0,\infty)
. By Theorem
4.1
,

c=\overline{D}_{-}\Phi(x_{0})=\underline{D}_{+}\Phi(x_{0})
.
Denote
u^{\pm}(t):=u(x(t)\pm,t)
for
t>t_{p}
.
By Corollary
2.6
,
u^{\pm}(t)\in\mathcal{U}(x(t),t;t_{p})
so that,
by
(
2.16
)
–
(
2.19
)
,

E(u^{+}(t);\,x(t),t;\,t_{p})=E(u^{-}(t);\,x(t),t;\,t_{p}),

which, similar to
(
3.5
)
, is equivalent to

(4.40)

\frac{1}{t-t_{p}}\int_{x_{p}+l_{p}^{-}}^{x_{p}+l_{p}^{+}}(u(\xi,t_{p})-c)\,{\rm d}\xi=\int_{u^{+}(t)}^{u^{-}(t)}(s-c)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s,

where
l_{p}^{\pm}:=l_{p}^{\pm}(t)
are given by

(4.41)

l_{p}^{\pm}=l_{p}^{\pm}(t):=x(t)-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t))\qquad{\rm for}\ t>t_{p}.

Denote
U_{\pm}:=U_{\pm}(t,t_{p})
by

U_{\pm}=U_{\pm}(t,t_{p}):=\frac{1}{l_{p}^{\pm}}\int_{0}^{l_{p}^{\pm}}(u(x_{p}+\varsigma,t_{p})-c)\,{\rm d}\varsigma.

Then we have

\displaystyle\int_{x_{p}+l_{p}^{-}}^{x_{p}+l_{p}^{+}}(u(\xi,t_{p})-c)\,{\rm d}\xi=\Big(\int_{0}^{l_{p}^{+}}+\int_{l_{p}^{-}}^{0}\Big)(u(x_{p}+\varsigma,t_{p})-c)\,{\rm d}\varsigma=U_{+}\,l_{p}^{+}-U_{-}\,l_{p}^{-},

which, by combining with
(
4.40
)
–
(
4.41
)
, implies

(4.42)

\displaystyle\frac{x(t)-x_{p}}{t-t_{p}}-f^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t))=\frac{{-}\int_{u^{+}(t)}^{u^{-}(t)}(s-c)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s+U_{\mp}(f^{\prime}(u^{-}(t))-f^{\prime}(u^{+}(t)))}{U_{-}-U_{+}},

(4.43)

\displaystyle\frac{x(t)-x_{p}}{t-t_{p}}-f^{\prime}(c)=\frac{{-}\int_{u^{+}(t)}^{u^{-}(t)}(s-c)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s+U_{-}(f^{\prime}(u^{-}(t))-f^{\prime}(c))-U_{+}(f^{\prime}(u^{+}(t))-f^{\prime}(c))}{U_{-}-U_{+}}.

The remaining proof of
Case I
is divided into four steps:

1.
We first prove that

(4.44)

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}:=\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}}\lambda(t)\begin{cases}>1\quad&{\rm if}\ \overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}>\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}},\\[2.84526pt]
=1\quad&{\rm if}\ \overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}=\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}},\\[2.84526pt]
<1\quad&{\rm if}\ \overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}<\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}},\end{cases}

where
A_{\pm}:=A_{\pm}(t)
and
\lambda:=\lambda(t)
are given by

(4.45)

\displaystyle A_{\pm}=A_{\pm}(t):=|u^{\pm}(t)-c|,\qquad\lambda=\lambda(t):=\Big(\frac{A_{-}(t)}{A_{+}(t)}\Big)^{1+\alpha}.

(a).
Denote
l^{\pm}:=l^{\pm}(t)
by

(4.46)

\displaystyle l^{\pm}=l^{\pm}(t):=x(t)-x_{0}-tf^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t)).

By
(
4.18
)
, it follows from
(
4.41
)
and
(
4.46
)
that

\displaystyle l_{p}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=l^{\pm}+t_{p}(f^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t))-f^{\prime}(c))=l^{\pm}+t_{p}(f^{\prime}(\varphi(x_{0}+l^{\pm}))-f^{\prime}(c))

\displaystyle=(1-t_{p}\overline{C}_{\gamma})l^{\pm}+t_{p}(\overline{C}_{\sigma_{\pm}}+o(1)){\rm sgn}(l^{\pm})|l^{\pm}|^{1+\sigma}

(4.47)

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\frac{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{\pm}}}{\overline{C}_{\gamma}}{\rm sgn}(l^{\pm})|l^{\pm}|^{1+\sigma}.

Then, by
(
4.17
)
, we obtain

\displaystyle u^{\pm}(t)-c

\displaystyle=u(x_{p}+l_{p}^{\pm},t_{p})-c=\varphi(x_{0}+l^{\pm})-c=(C_{\gamma}+o(1)){\rm sgn}(l^{\pm})|l^{\pm}|^{\gamma}

\displaystyle=-(1+o(1))|C_{\gamma}|\bigg(\frac{\overline{C}_{\gamma}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{\pm}}}\bigg)^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\sigma}}{\rm sgn}(l_{p}^{\pm})|l_{p}^{\pm}|^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\sigma}}

(4.48)

\displaystyle=:-(1+o(1))\overline{C}_{\pm}{\rm sgn}(l_{p}^{\pm})|l_{p}^{\pm}|^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\sigma}}.

Thus, by
(
4.48
)
, it follows from
(
4.41
)
and
(
4.45
)
that

(4.49)

\displaystyle\frac{x(t)-x_{p}}{t-t_{p}}-f^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t))=\frac{l_{p}^{\pm}}{t-t_{p}}={\rm sgn}(l_{p}^{\pm})\frac{1+o(1)}{t-t_{p}}\bigg(\frac{A_{\pm}}{\overline{C}_{\pm}}\bigg)^{\frac{1+\sigma}{\gamma}}.

On the other hand, from
(
4.48
)
,

\displaystyle U_{\pm}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{l_{p}^{\pm}}\int_{0}^{l_{p}^{\pm}}(1+o(1))\overline{C}_{\pm}{\rm sgn}(l_{p}^{\pm})|\varsigma|^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\sigma}}\,{\rm d}\varsigma

\displaystyle=-(1+o(1))\frac{1+\sigma}{1+\gamma+\sigma}\overline{C}_{\pm}{\rm sgn}(l_{p}^{\pm})\,|l_{p}^{\pm}|^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\sigma}}

(4.50)

\displaystyle=-(1+o(1))\frac{1+\sigma}{1+\gamma+\sigma}{\rm sgn}(l_{p}^{\pm})\,A_{\pm}.

Furthermore, by
(
4.45
)
, it follows from
(
A.5
)
–
(
A.6
)
that

(4.51)

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t))-f^{\prime}(c)=\mp\frac{N+o(1)}{1+\alpha}|u^{\pm}(t)-c|^{1+\alpha}=\mp\frac{N+o(1)}{1+\alpha}(A_{\pm})^{1+\alpha},

(4.52)

\displaystyle-\int_{u^{+}(t)}^{u^{-}(t)}(s-c)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s=-\frac{N+o(1)}{2+\alpha}(A_{-})^{2+\alpha}+\frac{N+o(1)}{2+\alpha}(A_{+})^{2+\alpha}.

Taking
(
4.49
)
–
(
4.52
)
into
(
4.42
)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\pm\frac{1}{t-t_{p}}\Big(\frac{A_{\pm}}{\overline{C}_{\pm}}\Big)^{\frac{1+\sigma}{\gamma}}=\frac{1+o(1)}{\frac{1+\sigma}{1+\gamma+\sigma}(A_{-}+A_{+})}\bigg(

\displaystyle\pm\frac{1+\sigma}{1+\gamma+\sigma}\frac{N+o(1)}{1+\alpha}A_{\mp}\big((A_{-})^{1+\alpha}+(A_{+})^{1+\alpha}\big)

(4.53)

\displaystyle\,+\frac{N+o(1)}{2+\alpha}\big((A_{+})^{2+\alpha}-(A_{-})^{2+\alpha}\big)\bigg).

Then, as
t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}}\Big(\frac{A_{+}}{A_{-}}\Big)^{\frac{1+\sigma}{\gamma}}\cong\frac{\gamma\sigma(A_{-})^{2+\alpha}+(1+\gamma)(1+\sigma)A_{-}(A_{+})^{1+\alpha}+(1+\gamma+\sigma)(A_{+})^{2+\alpha}}{\gamma\sigma(A_{+})^{2+\alpha}+(1+\gamma)(1+\sigma)A_{+}(A_{-})^{1+\alpha}+(1+\gamma+\sigma)(A_{-})^{2+\alpha}},

which, by
A_{-}=A_{+}\lambda^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}
inferred by
(
4.45
)
and
\gamma(1+\alpha)=1
in
(
4.17
)
, implies

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}=\frac{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}}\cong\frac{\gamma\sigma\lambda^{2+\gamma+\sigma}+(1+\gamma)(1+\sigma)\lambda^{1+\gamma+\sigma}+(1+\gamma+\sigma)\lambda^{1+\sigma}}{\gamma\sigma+(1+\gamma)(1+\sigma)\lambda+(1+\gamma+\sigma)\lambda^{1+\gamma}}.

By a simple calculation, we obtain

(4.54)

\displaystyle\gamma\sigma(1+\lambda)(\lambda_{0}-\lambda^{1+\gamma+\sigma})\cong(1+\gamma+\sigma)\lambda(1+\lambda^{\gamma})(\lambda^{\sigma}-\lambda_{0}).

(b).
We now solve equation
(
4.54
)
. First, it is direct to see that

\displaystyle 0<\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}}\lambda(t)<\mathop{\overline{\lim}}\limits_{t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}}\lambda(t)<\infty.

Due to the magnitude of
\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}
and
\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}
,
there are three cases:

(i)   If
\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}=\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}
, then
\lambda_{0}=1
.
By letting
t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}
in
(
4.54
)
,
any converged subsequence of
\{\lambda(t)\}
must converge to
1
,
which implies that
\lambda_{1}=\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}}\lambda(t)=1.

(ii)   If
\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}<\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}
, then
\lambda_{0}<1
. To prove

\displaystyle\gamma\sigma(1+\lambda)(\lambda_{0}-\lambda^{1+\gamma+\sigma})=(1+\gamma+\sigma)\lambda(1+\lambda^{\gamma})(\lambda^{\sigma}-\lambda_{0})

has a unique real root,
it suffices to show that

(4.55)

\displaystyle h(\lambda):=\frac{1+\gamma+\sigma}{\gamma\sigma}\frac{\lambda(1+\lambda^{\gamma})}{1+\lambda}+\frac{\lambda^{1+\gamma+\sigma}-\lambda_{0}}{\lambda^{\sigma}-\lambda_{0}}=0

has a unique root for
\lambda\in(0,\infty)
.

Denote
\lambda_{0}^{+}=(\lambda_{0})^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma+\sigma}}

and
\lambda_{0}^{-}=(\lambda_{0})^{\frac{1}{\sigma}}.

If
\lambda>\lambda_{0}^{+}
or
\lambda<\lambda_{0}^{-}
,
we see from
(
4.55
)
that
h(\lambda)>0
.
Meanwhile, it is direct to check from
(
4.55
)
that

\displaystyle h(\lambda_{0}^{+})=\frac{1+\gamma+\sigma}{\gamma\sigma}\,\frac{\lambda_{0}^{+}(1+(\lambda_{0}^{+})^{\gamma})}{1+\lambda_{0}^{+}}>0,\qquad\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow\lambda_{0}^{-}{+}}h(\lambda)=-\infty,

and, for
\lambda\in(\lambda_{0}^{-},\lambda_{0}^{+})
,

\displaystyle h^{\prime}(\lambda)=\frac{1+\gamma+\sigma}{\gamma\sigma}\frac{1+(1+\gamma)\lambda^{\gamma}+\gamma\lambda^{1+\gamma}}{(1+\lambda)^{2}}+\frac{(1+\gamma+\sigma)\lambda^{1+\gamma}(\lambda^{\sigma}{-}\lambda_{0})+\sigma(\lambda_{0}{-}\lambda^{1+\gamma+\sigma})}{\lambda^{1-\sigma}(\lambda^{\sigma}{-}\lambda_{0})^{2}}>0,

which implies that there exists a unique
\lambda=\lambda_{1}\in(\lambda_{0}^{-},\lambda_{0}^{+})\subset(0,1)

such that
h(\lambda_{1})=0
.
Therefore, we conclude

\displaystyle\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}}\lambda(t)=\lambda_{1}<1\qquad{\rm if}\ \overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}<\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}.

(iii)   If
\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}>\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}
,
then
\lambda_{0}>1
so that
(\lambda_{0})^{-1}<1
.
By taking
\bar{\lambda}:=\lambda^{-1}
into
(
4.54
)
,

(4.56)

\displaystyle\gamma\sigma(1+\bar{\lambda})\big((\lambda_{0})^{-1}-\bar{\lambda}^{1+\gamma+\sigma}\big)\cong(1+\gamma+\sigma)\bar{\lambda}(1+\bar{\lambda}^{\gamma})\big(\bar{\lambda}^{\sigma}-(\lambda_{0})^{-1}\big),

which is the same as
(
4.54
)

via replacing
\bar{\lambda}
and
(\lambda_{0})^{-1}
by
\lambda
and
\lambda_{0}
, respectively.

Since
(\lambda_{0})^{-1}<1
, by the same arguments
as
{\rm(ii)}
,
equation
(
4.56
)
has a unique real root
\bar{\lambda}_{1}\in((\lambda_{0})^{-\frac{1}{\sigma}},(\lambda_{0})^{-\frac{1}{1+\gamma+\sigma}})\subset(0,1)
so that

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}=\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}}\lambda(t)=(\bar{\lambda}_{1})^{-1}>1\qquad{\rm if}\ \overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}>\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}.

2.
We now prove
(
4.29
)
with
O_{1}(1)
in
(
4.36
)
.
Since
\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}\neq\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}
,
it follows from
(
4.44
)
that
\lambda_{1}\neq 1
.

(a).
By taking
A_{-}=(1+o(1))(\lambda_{1})^{\gamma}A_{+}
into
(
4.53
)
,
it is direct to check that, as
t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}
,

\displaystyle A_{+}

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))|C_{\gamma}|\bigg(\frac{(\overline{C}_{\gamma})^{2}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}}\bigg)^{\frac{\gamma}{\sigma}}\bigg(\frac{\gamma\sigma}{(1+\gamma)(1+\sigma)}\frac{(\lambda_{1})^{\gamma}(1+\lambda_{1})}{1+(\lambda_{1})^{\gamma}}+\frac{1+\gamma+\sigma}{(1+\gamma)(1+\sigma)}\bigg)^{\frac{\gamma}{\sigma}}\,(t-t_{p})^{\frac{\gamma}{\sigma}}

(4.57)

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))|C_{\gamma}|\bigg(\frac{\big(\overline{C}_{\gamma}\big)^{2}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}}\bigg)^{\frac{\gamma}{\sigma}}(Q_{+})^{\frac{\gamma}{\sigma}}\,(t-t_{p})^{\frac{\gamma}{\sigma}},

\displaystyle A_{-}

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))|C_{\gamma}|\bigg(\frac{(\overline{C}_{\gamma})^{2}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}}\bigg)^{\frac{\gamma}{\sigma}}\bigg(\frac{\gamma\sigma}{(1+\gamma)(1+\sigma)}\frac{1+\lambda_{1}}{\lambda_{1}(1+(\lambda_{1})^{\gamma})}+\frac{1+\gamma+\sigma}{(1+\gamma)(1+\sigma)}\bigg)^{\frac{\gamma}{\sigma}}\,(t-t_{p})^{\frac{\gamma}{\sigma}}

(4.58)

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))|C_{\gamma}|\bigg(\frac{\big(\overline{C}_{\gamma}\big)^{2}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}}\bigg)^{\frac{\gamma}{\sigma}}(Q_{-})^{\frac{\gamma}{\sigma}}\,(t-t_{p})^{\frac{\gamma}{\sigma}},

where
Q_{\pm}
, given by
(
4.37
)
, can be seen from
(
4.44
)
that

(4.59)

\displaystyle Q_{-}<1<Q_{+}\,\,\,{\rm if}\ \overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}>\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}},\qquad\,\,Q_{+}<1<Q_{-}\,\,\,{\rm if}\ \overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}<\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}.

Then, by
(
4.49
)
,
it is direct to check from
(
4.57
)
–
(
4.58
)
that

(4.60)

\displaystyle l_{p}^{\pm}=\pm(1+o(1))\overline{C}_{\gamma}\bigg(\frac{(\overline{C}_{\gamma})^{2}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{\pm}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{\sigma}}(Q_{\pm})^{\frac{1+\sigma}{\sigma}}\,(t-t_{p})^{\frac{1+\sigma}{\sigma}}\qquad\mbox{ as $t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}$},

which, by
(
4.47
)
, implies that

(4.61)

\displaystyle l^{\pm}=\pm(1+o(1))\bigg(\frac{(\overline{C}_{\gamma})^{2}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{\pm}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{\sigma}}(Q_{\pm})^{\frac{1}{\sigma}}\,(t-t_{p})^{\frac{1}{\sigma}}\qquad\mbox{ as $t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}$}.

(b).
By
(
4.18
)
, it follows from
(
4.41
)
and
(
4.47
)
that,
as
t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}
,

\displaystyle x(t)-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)

\displaystyle=x(t)-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t))+(t-t_{p})(f^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t))-f^{\prime}(c))

\displaystyle=l_{p}^{\pm}+(t-t_{p})(f^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t))-f^{\prime}(c))

\displaystyle=l_{p}^{\pm}+(t-t_{p})\big({-}\overline{C}_{\gamma}\,l^{\pm}+\big(\overline{C}_{\sigma_{\pm}}+o(1)\big)\,{\rm sgn}(l^{\pm})|l^{\pm}|^{1+\sigma}\big)

\displaystyle=l_{p}^{\pm}-\overline{C}_{\gamma}(t-t_{p})\big(l^{\pm}-(1+o(1))l_{p}^{\pm}\big)

(4.62)

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))l_{p}^{\pm}-\overline{C}_{\gamma}(t-t_{p})l^{\pm},

which, by combining with
(
4.59
)
–
(
4.61
)
, implies that,
as
t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}
,

\displaystyle x(t)-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))l_{p}^{\pm}-\overline{C}_{\gamma}(t-t_{p})l^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\pm(1+o(1))\overline{C}_{\gamma}\bigg(\frac{(\overline{C}_{\gamma})^{2}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{\pm}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{\sigma}}(Q_{\pm})^{\frac{1}{\sigma}}(Q_{\pm}-1)\,(t-t_{p})^{\frac{1+\sigma}{\sigma}}

\displaystyle=(1+o(1)){\rm sgn}\big(\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}-\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}\big)\overline{C}_{\gamma}\bigg(\frac{(\overline{C}_{\gamma})^{2}Q_{\pm}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{\pm}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{\sigma}}\,|Q_{\pm}-1|\,(t-t_{p})^{\frac{1+\sigma}{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\cong{\rm sgn}\big(\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}-\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}\big)O_{1}(1)\,(t-t_{p})^{\frac{1+\sigma}{\sigma}}.

It remains to check
O_{1}(1)
in
(
4.36
)
,
i.e.
,

\bigg(\frac{(\overline{C}_{\gamma})^{2}Q_{+}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{\sigma}}|Q_{+}-1|=\bigg(\frac{(\overline{C}_{\gamma})^{2}Q_{-}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{\sigma}}|Q_{-}-1|,

which is implied by the following two facts:
It is direct to check from
(
4.37
)
that

\displaystyle|Q_{+}-1|=\frac{\gamma\sigma}{(1+\gamma)(1+\sigma)}\,\frac{\big|(\lambda_{1})^{1+\gamma}-1\big|}{1+(\lambda_{1})^{\gamma}}=\lambda_{1}|Q_{-}-1|,

and, by
A_{-}=(1+o(1))(\lambda_{1})^{\gamma}A_{+}
,
it follows from
(
4.57
)
–

4.58
)
that

\lambda_{1}=\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}}\bigg(\frac{A_{-}(t)}{A_{+}(t)}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}=\bigg(\frac{Q_{-}}{Q_{+}}\lambda_{0}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{\sigma}}.

3.
We now prove
(
4.31
)
with
O_{2}(1)
in
(
4.36
)
.
Since
\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}=\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}=\overline{C}_{\sigma}
,
then
\lambda_{1}=1
so that
Q_{+}=Q_{-}
.
By
(
4.60
)
–
(
4.61
)
,
it follows from
(
4.62
)
that

(4.63)

\displaystyle x(t)-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)=o(1)(t-t_{p})^{\frac{1+\sigma}{\sigma}}.

Thus, to estimate shock

(a).
Since

\lambda(t)=1+o(1)\qquad{\rm as}\ t\rightarrow t_{p}{+},

\frac{\lambda(t)^{1+\gamma}-1}{\lambda(t)^{\gamma}+1}=\frac{\big(1+(\lambda(t)-1)\big)^{1+\gamma}-1}{2+o(1)}=\frac{1+o(1)}{2}(1+\gamma)(\lambda(t)-1).

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{t-t_{p}}\big(x(t)-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)\big)

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\frac{N}{1+\alpha}\frac{(1+\gamma)(1-\gamma)+\sigma}{(1+\gamma)(1+\sigma)}\frac{(A_{-}(t))^{2+\alpha}-(A_{+}(t))^{2+\alpha}}{A_{-}(t)+A_{+}(t)}

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\frac{N}{1+\alpha}\frac{(1+\gamma)(1-\gamma)+\sigma}{(1+\gamma)(1+\sigma)}(A_{+}(t))^{1+\alpha}\,\frac{\lambda(t)^{1+\gamma}-1}{\lambda(t)^{\gamma}+1}

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\frac{N}{1+\alpha}\frac{(1+\gamma)(1-\gamma)+\sigma}{2(1+\gamma)(1+\sigma)}(A_{+}(t))^{1+\alpha}(1+\gamma)(\lambda(t)-1)

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\frac{(1+\gamma)(1-\gamma)+\sigma}{2(1+\sigma)}\frac{N}{1+\alpha}\big((A_{-}(t))^{1+\alpha}-(A_{+}(t))^{1+\alpha}\big)

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\frac{(1+\gamma)(1-\gamma)+\sigma}{2(1+\sigma)}\big(|f^{\prime}(u^{-}(t))-f^{\prime}(c)|-|f^{\prime}(u^{+}(t))-f^{\prime}(c)|\big)

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\frac{(1+\gamma)(1-\gamma)+\sigma}{2(1+\sigma)}\big((f^{\prime}(u^{-}(t))-f^{\prime}(c))+(f^{\prime}(u^{+}(t))-f^{\prime}(c))\big)

(4.64)

\displaystyle=-(1+o(1))\frac{(1+\gamma)(1-\gamma)+\sigma}{2(1+\sigma)}\,\overline{C}_{\gamma}\,(l^{+}+l^{-}).

Then, by

(c).

)

By
(
2.44
)
, it follows from
(

)
and
(

that,
as

\displaystyle|u(x,t)-c|=|\varphi(x_{0}+\tilde{l}^{\pm})-c|=|C_{\gamma}+o(1)|\,|\tilde{l}^{\pm}|^{\gamma}.

Then, for any

with
\delta>\max\{1-(Q_{\pm})^{-1}\}

)
can be established as follows:

(i)

For
t\geq t_{p}
, if
(x,t)\in\Omega_{\pm}
near
(x_{p},t_{p})
with

\displaystyle\pm\big(x-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)-\nu_{\delta}^{\pm}|t-t_{p}|^{\frac{1+\sigma}{\sigma}}\big)\geq 0,

then it follows from

4.84
)
that

(4.86)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,|u(x,t)-c|\leq(1+o(1))\delta^{-\frac{\gamma}{1+\sigma}}|C_{\gamma}|\bigg(\frac{\overline{C}_{\gamma}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{\pm}}}\bigg)^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\sigma}}|x-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)|^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\sigma}};

and, if
(x,t)\in\Omega_{\pm}
near

\displaystyle\nu_{\delta}^{-}|t-t_{p}|^{\frac{1+\sigma}{\sigma}}\leq x-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)\leq\nu_{\delta}^{+}|t-t_{p}|^{\frac{1+\sigma}{\sigma}},

then it follows from

\displaystyle|u(x,t)-c|\leq(1+o(1))(1-\delta)^{-\frac{\gamma}{\sigma}}|C_{\gamma}|\bigg(\frac{(\overline{C}_{\gamma})^{2}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{\pm}}}\bigg)^{\frac{\gamma}{\sigma}}|t-t_{p}|^{\frac{\gamma}{\sigma}}.

(ii)

For
t<t_{p}
, if
(x,t)\in\Omega_{\pm}
near
(x_{p},t_{p})
, by
\delta\in(0,1)

(
4.86

(
4.73
)
.

For the case that
\overline{C}_{\sigma_{\pm}}=\overline{C}_{\sigma}

that
Q_{\pm}=1
.
Then, for any

,

(
4.30
)
follows immediately
via replacing
\overline{C}_{\sigma_{\pm}}
by
\overline{C}_{\sigma}
.

\displaystyle=l^{-}-{\rm sgn}(C_{\gamma_{-}})(1+o(1))t_{p}\overline{C}_{\gamma_{-}}|l^{-}|^{\gamma_{-}(1+\alpha)}=-(1+o(1))O^{-}_{4}(1)|l^{-}|^{\varrho_{0}},

2.
We now prove that

\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}_{1}:=\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}}\tilde{\lambda}(t)=\frac{1+\gamma_{+}+\sigma_{+}}{\gamma_{+}\sigma_{+}}\,\frac{|C_{\gamma_{+}}|^{\frac{1+\sigma_{+}}{\gamma_{+}}}}{|C_{\gamma_{-}}|^{\frac{1}{\varrho}}}\,\frac{\overline{C}_{\gamma_{+}}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}}\,O_{4}^{-}(1),

(a).

\displaystyle\frac{x(t)-t_{p}}{t-t_{p}}-f^{\prime}(u^{-}(t))=\frac{l_{p}^{-}}{t-t_{p}}=-\frac{1+o(1)}{t-t_{p}}\Big(\frac{A_{-}}{O_{-}(1)}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\varrho}}.\end{cases}

Similar to

, we have

\displaystyle U_{+}=-(1+o(1))\frac{1+\sigma_{+}}{1+\gamma_{+}+\sigma_{+}}A_{+},\qquad U_{-}=-{\rm sgn}(C_{\gamma_{-}})\frac{1+o(1)}{1+\varrho}A_{-},

(4.93)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,=\frac{-\frac{N+o(1)}{2+\alpha}\big((A_{-})^{2+\alpha}-(A_{+})^{2+\alpha}\big)-{\rm sgn}(C_{\gamma_{-}})\frac{1+o(1)}{1+\varrho}\frac{N+o(1)}{1+\alpha}A_{-}\big((A_{-})^{1+\alpha}+(A_{+})^{1+\alpha}\big)}{-{\rm sgn}(C_{\gamma_{-}})\frac{1+o(1)}{1+\varrho}A_{-}+(1+o(1))\frac{1+\sigma_{+}}{1+\gamma_{+}+\sigma_{+}}A_{+}},

\displaystyle-\frac{1+o(1)}{t-t_{p}}\Big(\frac{A_{-}}{O_{-}(1)}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\varrho}}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,=\frac{-\frac{N+o(1)}{2+\alpha}\big((A_{-})^{2+\alpha}-(A_{+})^{2+\alpha}\big)-\frac{1+\sigma_{+}}{1+\gamma_{+}+\sigma_{+}}\frac{N+o(1)}{1+\alpha}A_{+}\big((A_{-})^{1+\alpha}+(A_{+})^{1+\alpha}\big)}{-{\rm sgn}(C_{\gamma_{-}})\frac{1+o(1)}{1+\varrho}A_{-}+(1+o(1))\frac{1+\sigma_{+}}{1+\gamma_{+}+\sigma_{+}}A_{+}}.

(4.95)

\displaystyle\cong\frac{-[(1+\varrho)+{\rm sgn}(C_{\gamma_{-}})(1+\gamma_{+})](A_{-})^{2+\alpha}-{\rm sgn}(C_{\gamma_{-}})(1+\gamma_{+})A_{-}(A_{+})^{1+\alpha}+(1+\varrho)(A_{+})^{2+\alpha}}{(1+\gamma_{+}+\sigma_{+})(A_{-})^{2+\alpha}+(1+\gamma_{+})(1+\sigma_{+})A_{+}(A_{-})^{1+\alpha}+\gamma_{+}\sigma_{+}(A_{+})^{2+\alpha}}.

(b).
We now prove

Otherwise, there are two cases:

which, by a simple calculation, leads to the following contradiction,

\displaystyle 1=O(1)(t-t_{p})^{\varrho}(A_{-})^{\varrho(1+\alpha)-1}=o(1)\qquad{\rm as}\ t\rightarrow t_{p}{+},

where the last step is due to the fact that

and

(ii)

so that the left side of

For the case that

with

,

\displaystyle\frac{1+\varrho}{1+\gamma_{+}+\sigma_{+}}\frac{(O_{-}(1))^{\frac{1}{\varrho}}}{(O_{+}(1))^{\frac{1+\sigma_{+}}{\gamma_{+}}}}\,\frac{(A_{+})^{\frac{1+\sigma_{+}}{\gamma_{+}}}}{(A_{-})^{\frac{1}{\varrho}}}\cong\frac{(1+\gamma_{+})(A_{+})^{1+\alpha}}{(1+\gamma_{+}+\sigma_{+})(A_{-})^{1+\alpha}},

which, by a simple calculation, implies that
A_{+}=O(1)
.
This contradicts to the fact that
\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{p}{+}}u^{+}(t)=c
.

Therefore,

.

\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}=(1+o(1))\frac{1+\gamma_{+}+\sigma_{+}}{\gamma_{+}\sigma_{+}}\frac{(O_{+}(1))^{\frac{1+\sigma_{+}}{\gamma_{+}}}}{(O_{-}(1))^{\frac{1}{\varrho}}}=(1+o(1))\frac{1+\gamma_{+}+\sigma_{+}}{\gamma_{+}\sigma_{+}}\frac{|C_{\gamma_{+}}|^{\frac{1+\sigma_{+}}{\gamma_{+}}}}{|C_{\gamma_{-}}|^{\frac{1}{\varrho}}}\,\frac{\overline{C}_{\gamma_{+}}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}}\,O_{4}^{-}(1),

which, by letting

)

\displaystyle\quad\,\times\bigg(\frac{\overline{C}_{\gamma_{+}}}{O_{4}^{-}(1)}\bigg)^{\varrho}\bigg(\frac{\big(\overline{C}_{\gamma_{+}}\big)^{2}}{\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}}\bigg)^{\frac{\varrho}{\sigma_{+}}}\,(t-t_{p})^{\frac{\varrho(1+\sigma_{+})}{\sigma_{+}}}.

Equivalently, this can also be seen as follows:

which, by

\displaystyle=l_{p}^{-}-\overline{C}_{\gamma_{+}}(t-t_{p})(l^{-}-(1+o(1))l_{p}^{-})

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))l_{p}^{-}=-(1+o(1))O_{3}^{+}(1)\,(t-t_{p})^{\frac{1+\sigma_{+}}{\sigma_{+}}}.

4.
For

near

\displaystyle x-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)

\displaystyle=\tilde{l}^{-}-{\rm sgn}(C_{\gamma_{-}})(1+o(1))t_{p}\overline{C}_{\gamma_{-}}|\tilde{l}^{-}|^{\gamma_{-}(1+\alpha)}=-(1+o(1))O^{-}_{4}(1)|\tilde{l}^{-}|^{\varrho_{0}},

\displaystyle=u(x_{p}+l_{p}^{-},t_{p})-c=\varphi(x_{0}+\tilde{l}^{-})-c=-(C_{\gamma_{-}}+o(1))|\tilde{l}^{-}|^{\gamma_{-}}

\displaystyle=-(1+o(1))C_{\gamma_{-}}\big((O_{4}^{-}(1))^{-1}|x-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)|\big)^{\frac{\gamma_{-}}{\varrho_{0}}}

(4.100)

\displaystyle=-(1+o(1))C_{\gamma_{-}}(O_{4}^{-}(1))^{-\varrho}|x-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)|^{\varrho}.

5.
For the case that
c=\underline{D}_{+}\Phi(x_{0})>\overline{D}_{-}\Phi(x_{0})=a
,
the behavior of solution

near

to the case that

and

In fact, by the continuity of solution

at point
(x_{p},t_{p})

the forward generalized characteristic of the point lying on characteristic
L_{a}(x_{0})

for the case that

,
or the shock curve emitting from
x_{0}
for the case that

,
must lie on the left of point
(x_{p},t_{p})
.
This means that, for
(x,t)\in\Omega_{-}
near
(x_{p},t_{p})

x_{0}=x-tf^{\prime}(u(x,t))
so that

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(u(x,t))-f^{\prime}(c)

\displaystyle=\frac{x-x_{0}}{t}-f^{\prime}(c)=\frac{1}{t}\big(x-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)\big)

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\overline{C}_{\gamma_{+}}\big(x-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)\big)

\displaystyle=-(1+o(1))\overline{C}_{\gamma_{+}}|x-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)|.

(
4.89
)
,

(
A.5
)
and

\displaystyle u^{-}(t)-c

\displaystyle=u(x_{p}+l_{p}^{-},t_{p})-c

\displaystyle=-\Big(\frac{1+\alpha}{N+o(1)}|f^{\prime}(u(x_{p}+l_{p}^{-},t_{p}))-f^{\prime}(c)|\Big)^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}

\displaystyle=-\Big(\frac{1+\alpha}{N+o(1)}\overline{C}_{\gamma_{+}}|l_{p}^{-}|\Big)^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}=-(1+o(1))|C_{\gamma_{+}}|\,|l_{p}^{-}|^{\gamma_{+}},

and, instead of
(
4.100
)
, we have

\displaystyle u(x,t)-c

\displaystyle=-\Big(\frac{1+\alpha}{N+o(1)}|f^{\prime}(u(x,t))-f^{\prime}(c)|\Big)^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}

\displaystyle=-(1+o(1))|C_{\gamma_{+}}|\,|x-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)|^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))C_{\gamma_{+}}|x-x_{p}-(t-t_{p})f^{\prime}(c)|^{\gamma_{+}}.

Up to now, we have completed the proof of Theorem
4.4
.
∎

Remark 4.1
.

For Theorems
4.1
and
4.4
, it is worth to point out that,

the continuous shock generation points are not necessarily to be
the first blowup point of entropy solutions,
which even are not assumed to be isolated
.
Furthermore, we present the following three observations:

(i)

For the case that
0<t_{p}^{+}<t_{p}^{-}\in(0,\infty]

(
resp.,

0<t_{p}^{-}<t_{p}^{+}\in(0,\infty]
),
the developed shock curve is caused by the compression of local characteristics
emitting from the right (
resp.,
left) side of
x_{0}
,
so is the convexity of such a shock curve.

(ii)

For the case that
t_{p}^{+}=t_{p}^{-}\in(0,\infty)
,
the developed shock curve is caused by the compression of local characteristics
emitting from both sides of
x_{0}
. Moreover, if
\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}\neq\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}
,
the strengths of the compression from the two sides of
x_{0}
are different
so that the convexity of such a shock curve is determined by the magnitude of

\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}
and
\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}
;
and if
\overline{C}_{\sigma_{+}}=\overline{C}_{\sigma_{-}}
,
such strengths are of the same magnitude
so that the convexity of the developed shock curve is determined by the sign of
the coefficient
\overline{C}_{\rho}
of
the higher term
|l|^{1+\sigma+\rho}
as in
(
4.19
)
.

(iii)

In addition,

O^{\pm}_{3}(1)
with
Q^{\pm}_{3}
in
(
4.38
)
can be viewed as the limits of

O_{1}(1)
with
Q_{\pm}
in
(
4.36
)
–
(
4.37
)
as
(\lambda_{1})^{\pm 1}\rightarrow 0{+}
, respectively.
Combining this with the continuity of
O_{1}(1)
and
Q_{\pm}
with respect to
\lambda_{1}

and the continuity of
\lambda_{1}
with respect to
\lambda_{0}
,
may indicate some exceptional stability of the developed shock curves and entropy solutions
under the perturbation of the initial data function
\varphi(x)
near
x_{0}
.

5.
Fine Structures of Entropy Solutions

In this section, we focus on the fine structures of entropy solutions.
In §5.1, we provide and prove
the structures of entropy solutions inside the backward characteristic triangles in Theorem
5.3
;
in §5.2–5.3, we prove
the directional limits of entropy solutions at the discontinuous points in Theorem
5.4

and the general structures of shocks in Theorem
5.5
;
and in §5.4, we establish
the fine structures of entropy solutions on the shock sets in Theorem
5.6
.

5.1.
New structures of entropy solutions inside the backward characteristic triangles

By the definition of
{\mathcal{U}}(x,t)
in
(
2.5
)
,
for any
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
,

{\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})
is a bounded closed set such that

{\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})\subset[u(x_{0}{+},t_{0}),u(x_{0}{-},t_{0})]=:I(x_{0},t_{0})\subset\mathbb{R}.

Let
\mathring{{\mathcal{U}}}(x_{0},t_{0})
and
\partial{\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})

be the interior and the boundary of
{\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})
respectively,
and let
{\mathcal{U}}^{c}(x_{0},t_{0}):=I(x_{0},t_{0})-{\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})

be the complement of
{\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})
under
I(x_{0},t_{0})
.
Then

(5.1)

I(x_{0},t_{0})=\partial{\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})\cup\mathring{{\mathcal{U}}}(x_{0},t_{0})\cup{\mathcal{U}}^{c}(x_{0},t_{0}).

Since
{\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})
is a closed set,
then
\mathring{{\mathcal{U}}}(x_{0},t_{0})
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{c}(x_{0},t_{0})
are both open sets
so that they can be expressed by disjoint unions of at most countable open intervals respectively:

(5.2)

\mathring{{\mathcal{U}}}(x_{0},t_{0})=\cup_{m}I_{m}:=\cup_{m}(a_{m},b_{m}),\qquad{\mathcal{U}}^{c}(x_{0},t_{0})=\cup_{n}J_{n}:=\cup_{n}(c_{n},d_{n})

with
a_{m},b_{m},c_{n},d_{n}\in\partial{\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})
.

From
(
5.1
)
–
(
5.2
)
,
I:=I(x_{0},t_{0})
can be expressed by
the following countable disjoint union:

\displaystyle I=I(x_{0},t_{0})

\displaystyle=\partial{\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})\cup\big(\cup_{m}I_{m}\big)\cup\big(\cup_{n}J_{n}\big)

(5.3)

\displaystyle=\partial{\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})\cup\big(\cup_{m}(a_{m},b_{m})\big)\cup\big(\cup_{n}(c_{n},d_{n})\big).

For any point set
F\subset\mathbb{R}
, let

(5.4)

\Delta_{F}(x_{0},t_{0}):=\big\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\,:\,(x,t)\in l_{u}(x_{0},t_{0})\quad{\rm for}\ u\in F\big\},

where
l_{u}(x_{0},t_{0})
is defined by
(
2.43
)
,
i.e.
,

l_{u}(x_{0},t_{0})=\big\{(x,t)\,:\,x=x_{0}+(t-t_{0})f^{\prime}(u)\quad{\rm for}\ t\in(0,t_{0})\big\}.

The triangle region
\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})
,
defined by applying
(
5.4
)
to
I=I(x_{0},t_{0})
,
is called the backward characteristic triangle from point
(x_{0},t_{0})
,
which is a triangle when
{\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})
contains more than one point,
and is a single characteristic line when
{\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})
contains only one point.

Denote
u^{\pm}_{0}:=u(x_{0}{\pm},t_{0})
.
Then the backward characteristic triangle
\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})
and
\mathbb{R}\times(0,t_{0})

can be expressed by the following countable disjoint unions:

(5.5)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})\,=\Delta_{\partial{\mathcal{U}}}(x_{0},t_{0})\cup\big(\cup_{m}\Delta_{I_{m}}(x_{0},t_{0})\big)\cup\big(\cup_{n}\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})\big),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\mathbb{R}\times(0,t_{0})=\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})\cup\Delta_{({-}\infty,u^{+}_{0})}(x_{0},t_{0})\cup\Delta_{(u^{-}_{0},\infty)}(x_{0},t_{0}).\end{cases}

See Fig.
5.1
for the details.

Figure 5.1.
The partitions of
\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})
and
\mathbb{R}\times(0,t_{0})
as in
(
5.5
)
.

We first have the following properties of the backward characteristic triangles.

Proposition 5.1
.

For any
(x_{1},t_{1}),(x_{2},t_{2})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
,

\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})
and
\Delta_{I}(x_{2},t_{2})
must possess one of the following two relations
:

(i)

\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})\cap\Delta_{I}(x_{2},t_{2})=\varnothing
;

(ii)

\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})\supset\Delta_{I}(x_{2},t_{2})\,

or
\,\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})\subset\Delta_{I}(x_{2},t_{2})
.

Furthermore, if
\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})\subset\Delta_{I}(x_{2},t_{2})
, then
one of the following two cases holds
:

(i)

\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})\subset l_{u}(x_{2},t_{2})
for some
u\in\mathcal{U}(x_{2},t_{2})
;

(ii)

\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})\subset\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{2},t_{2})
for some
J_{n}\subset\mathcal{U}^{c}(x_{2},t_{2})
.

Proof.

From
(
2.44
)
, for any
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
c\in\mathcal{U}(x_{0},t_{0})
,
the entropy solution
u(x,t)
is continuous on
l_{c}(x_{0},t_{0})
so that,
for any
u_{i}\in\mathcal{U}(x_{i},t_{i})
,

l_{u_{1}}(x_{1},t_{1})
and
l_{u_{2}}(x_{2},t_{2})
are disjoint, or one containing another.
Then it is direct to check that,
if
\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})\cap\Delta_{I}(x_{2},t_{2})\neq\varnothing
,
then
\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})\supset\Delta_{I}(x_{2},t_{2})
or
\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})\subset\Delta_{I}(x_{2},t_{2})
.

Furthermore, if
\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})\subset\Delta_{I}(x_{2},t_{2})
,
then
(x_{1},t_{1})\in\Delta_{I}(x_{2},t_{2})
.
From
(
5.5
)
,
if
(x_{1},t_{1})\in\Delta_{\partial{\mathcal{U}}}(x_{2},t_{2})
or
(x_{1},t_{1})\in\Delta_{I_{m}}(x_{2},t_{2})
,
then
(x_{1},t_{1})\in l_{u}(x_{2},t_{2})
for some
u\in\mathcal{U}(x_{2},t_{2})
so that
u(x_{1}{\pm},t_{1})=u
,
which implies that
\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})\subset l_{u}(x_{2},t_{2})
.
If
(x_{1},t_{1})\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{2},t_{2})
for some
J_{n}=(c_{n},d_{n})\subset{\mathcal{U}}^{c}(x_{2},t_{2})
,
it follows from
(
2.44
)
and
c_{n},d_{n}\in\partial{\mathcal{U}}(x_{2},t_{2})
that

x_{2}-t_{2}f^{\prime}(d_{n})\leq x_{1}-t_{1}f^{\prime}(u(x_{1}{-},t_{1}))\leq x_{1}-t_{1}f^{\prime}(u(x_{1}{+},t_{1}))\leq x_{2}-t_{2}f^{\prime}(c_{n}),

which implies that
\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})\subset\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{2},t_{2})
.
∎

Lemma 5.2

(Forward generalized characteristics)
.

For any
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
,
there exists a unique forward generalized characteristic
:

(5.6)

X(x_{0},t_{0})=\big\{x=x(t)\,:\,x(t_{0})=x_{0},\,\,t_{0}\leq t<\infty\big\}

such that

(5.7)

\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})\subset\Delta_{I}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})\subset\Delta_{I}(x(t_{2}),t_{2})\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ t_{2}>t_{1}>t_{0};

see also
Fig.

5.2
.
Furthermore, the following properties hold
:

(i)

For any fixed
t_{1}
with
t_{1}>t_{0}
,

X(x(t_{1}),t_{1})=X(x_{0},t_{0})
on
[t_{1},\infty)
.

(ii)

If
u(x_{0}{+},t_{0})<u(x_{0}{-},t_{0})
,
X(x_{0},t_{0})
is a discontinuity of the entropy solution
u(x,t)
.
For any
t_{1}>t_{0}
, there exists
J_{n}\subset\mathcal{U}^{c}(x_{1},t_{1})

such that
(x(t),t)\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})
on
[t_{0},t_{1})
.

In the above,
\Delta_{I}(x,t)
with
I
and
\Delta_{J_{n}}(x,t)
with
J_{n}
are given by
(
5.1
)
–
(
5.4
)
.

Figure 5.2.
The forward generalized characteristic
x(t)
(red curve) with
\Delta_{I}(x(t),t)
(black triangle)
and
\Delta_{J_{n}}(x(t),t)
(blue triangle)
in Lemma
5.2
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into four steps.

1.
For any
t_{1}\geq t_{0}
, let

(5.8)

A_{\pm}(x_{0},t_{0},t_{1}):=\big\{x\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\pm\big(x+(t_{0}-t_{1})f^{\prime}(u(x{+},t_{1}))-x_{0}\big)>0\big\}.

Then

a(t_{1}):=\sup A_{-}(x_{0},t_{0},t_{1})\leq\inf A_{+}(x_{0},t_{0},t_{1})=:b(t_{1}).

We first claim:
\,a(t_{1})=b(t_{1})

for any

t_{1}\geq t_{0}
.

This can be seen as follows:
For
t_{1}=t_{0}
, it is direct to check that
a(t_{0})=b(t_{0})=x_{0}
.
For the case that
t_{1}>t_{0}
,
if
a(t_{1})<b(t_{1})
,
then, for any
x,x^{\prime}\in(a(t_{1}),b(t_{1}))
with
x<x^{\prime}
,

(5.9)

x+(t_{0}-t_{1})f^{\prime}(u(x{+},t_{1}))=x_{0}=x^{\prime}+(t_{0}-t_{1})f^{\prime}(u(x^{\prime}{+},t_{1})),

so that

(x_{0},t_{0})\in l_{u(x{+},t_{1})}(x,t_{1})\cap l_{u(x^{\prime}{+},t_{1})}(x^{\prime},t_{1}),

which, by
(
2.44
)
, implies

(5.10)

u(x{+},t_{1})=u(x_{0}{\pm},t_{0})=u(x^{\prime}{+},t_{1}).

On the other hand, since
f^{\prime}(u)
is strictly increasing,
(
5.9
)
implies

f^{\prime}(u(x^{\prime}{+},t_{1}))-f^{\prime}(u(x{+},t_{1}))=\frac{x^{\prime}-x}{t_{1}-t_{0}}>0,

so that
u(x^{\prime}{+},t_{1})>u(x{+},t_{1})
,
which contradicts to
(
5.10
)
.

Denote
X(x_{0},t_{0}):x=x(t):=a(t)
for
t\geq t_{0}
.
Then
X(x_{0},t_{0})
with
x(t_{0})=x_{0}
is a single-valued function on
[t_{0},\infty)
.

2.
We now show that, for any
t_{1}>t_{0}
,

(x(t_{1}),t_{1})
is the unique point on line
t=t_{1}
such that

\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})\subset\Delta_{I}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})
.
If
x<x(t_{1})
, then
x\in A_{-}(x_{0},t_{0},t_{1})
,
which, by
(
5.8
)
, implies that

x+(t_{0}-t_{1})f^{\prime}(u(x{+},t_{1}))<x_{0}
.
Thus,
(x_{0},t_{0})
lies on the right side of
\Delta_{I}(x,t_{1})
,
which, by Proposition
5.1
, implies that
\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})\cap\Delta_{I}(x,t_{1})=\varnothing
;
and if
x>x(t_{1})
, from
(
2.37
)
and
(
5.8
)
,
for any
x^{\prime}\in(x(t_{1}),x)
,

x_{0}<x^{\prime}+(t_{0}-t_{1})f^{\prime}(u(x^{\prime}{+},t_{1}))\leq x+(t_{0}-t_{1})f^{\prime}(u(x{-},t_{1})),

so that
(x_{0},t_{0})
lies on the left side of
\Delta_{I}(x,t_{1})
,
which implies that
\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})\cap\Delta_{I}(x,t_{1})=\varnothing
.

For
\Delta_{I}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})
,
by Lemma
2.9
,

\lim_{x\rightarrow x(t_{1}){\pm}}u(x{+},t_{1})=u(x(t_{1}){\pm},t_{1})
so that, by
(
5.8
)
,

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle x(t_{1})+(t_{0}-t_{1})f^{\prime}(u(x(t_{1}){+},t_{1}))=\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow x(t_{1}){+}}\big(x+(t_{0}-t_{1})f^{\prime}(u(x{+},t_{1}))\big)\geq x_{0},\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle x(t_{1})+(t_{0}-t_{1})f^{\prime}(u(x(t_{1}){-},t_{1}))=\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow x(t_{1}){-}}\big(x+(t_{0}-t_{1})f^{\prime}(u(x{+},t_{1}))\big)\leq x_{0},\end{cases}

which implies that
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\Delta_{I}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})

so that
\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})\subset\Delta_{I}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})
.

Thus, for any
t_{2}>t_{1}>t_{0}
,
\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})\subset\Delta_{I}(x(t_{i}),t_{i})

so that
\Delta_{I}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})\cap\Delta_{I}(x(t_{2}),t_{2})\neq\varnothing
,
which, by Proposition
5.1
and
(x(t_{2}),t_{2})\notin\Delta_{I}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})
, implies
(
5.7
)
.

3.
We now prove that
X(x_{0},t_{0})

is continuous on
[t_{0},\infty)
with
\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{+}}x(t)=x_{0}
.

Denote
y^{\pm}(x(t),t):=x(t)-tf^{\prime}(u(x(t){\pm},t))
.
From
(
5.7
)
, for any
t_{2}>t_{1}\geq t_{0}
,

(5.11)

y^{-}(x(t_{2}),t_{2})\leq y^{-}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})\leq y^{+}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})\leq y^{+}(x(t_{2}),t_{2}),

so that
y^{-}(x(t),t)
is nonincreasing and
y^{+}(x(t),t)
is nondecreasing.
Letting
t_{2}\rightarrow t_{1}{+}
in
(
5.11
)
,

y^{-}(\overline{x(t_{1}+)},t_{1})\leq y^{-}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})\leq y^{+}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})\leq y^{+}(\underline{x(t_{1}+)},t_{1}),

where
\overline{x(t_{1}+)}:=\mathop{\overline{\lim}}_{t_{2}\rightarrow t_{1}{+}}x(t_{2})

and
\underline{x(t_{1}+)}:=\mathop{\underline{\lim}}_{t_{2}\rightarrow t_{1}{+}}x(t_{2})
.

Since
y^{\pm}(\cdot,t_{1})
are nondecreasing,
then
\overline{x(t_{1}+)}\leq x(t_{1})\leq\underline{x(t_{1}+)}
,
which implies

\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow t_{1}{+}}x(t)=x(t_{1})\qquad{\rm for\ any\ }t_{1}\geq t_{0}.

Similarly, letting
t_{1}\rightarrow t_{2}{-}
in
(
5.11
)
,

y^{-}(x(t_{2}),t_{2})\leq y^{-}(\underline{x(t_{2}-)},t_{2})\leq y^{+}(\overline{x(t_{2}-)},t_{2})\leq y^{+}(x(t_{2}),t_{2}),

so that, by Lemma
2.8
,

\overline{x(t_{2}-)}\leq x(t_{2})\leq\underline{x(t_{2}-)}
,
which implies

\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{2}{-}}x(t)=x(t_{2})\qquad{\rm for\ any\ }t_{2}>t_{0},

where
\overline{x(t_{2}-)}:=\mathop{\overline{\lim}}_{t_{1}\rightarrow t_{2}{-}}x(t_{1})

and
\underline{x(t_{2}-)}:=\mathop{\underline{\lim}}_{t_{1}\rightarrow t_{2}{-}}x(t_{1})
.

Therefore,

X(x_{0},t_{0})
is continuous on
[t_{0},\infty)
with
\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{+}}x(t)=x_{0}
.

4.
For any fixed
t_{1}>t_{0}
,
the forward generalized characteristic of point
(x(t_{1}),t_{1})
is given by

X(x(t_{1}),t_{1}):=\big\{x=x_{1}(t)\,:\,x_{1}(t_{1})=x(t_{1}),\,t_{1}\leq t<\infty\big\}.

Then, by the definition of forward generalized characteristics in
(
5.6
)
–
(
5.7
)
,
for any
t_{2}>t_{1}
,

\varnothing\neq\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})\subset\Delta_{I}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})=\Delta_{I}(x_{1}(t_{1}),t_{1})\subset\Delta_{I}(x(t_{2}),t_{2})\cap\Delta_{I}(x_{1}(t_{2}),t_{2}),

so that, by Proposition
5.1
,

\Delta_{I}(x(t_{2}),t_{2})\supset\Delta_{I}(x_{1}(t_{2}),t_{2})\qquad{\rm or}\ \Delta_{I}(x(t_{2}),t_{2})\subset\Delta_{I}(x_{1}(t_{2}),t_{2}),

which implies that

x(t_{2})=x_{1}(t_{2})
.
By the arbitrariness of
t_{2}
with
t_{2}>t_{1}
and
x_{1}(t_{1})=x(t_{1})
,
we conclude that
X(x(t_{1}),t_{1})=X(x_{0},t_{0})
on
[t_{1},\infty)
.

Finally, if
u(x_{0}{+},t_{0})<u(x_{0}{-},t_{0})
, then
\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})
is a triangle so that,
by
(
5.7
)
,
\Delta_{I}(x(t),t)
is also a triangle for any
t>t_{0}
,
which implies that
u(x(t){+},t)<u(x(t){-},t)
for any
t>t_{0}
.
This means that
X(x_{0},t_{0})
is a discontinuity of entropy solution
u(x,t)
.

Since
X(x_{0},t_{0})

is the forward generalized characteristic of
(x_{0},t_{0})
,
then, for any
t\in(t_{0},t_{1})
,

\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})\subset\Delta_{I}(x(t),t)\subset\Delta_{I}(x(t_{1}),t_{1}),

which, by Proposition
5.1
, implies that

(x(t),t)\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})
for some
J_{n}\subset\mathcal{U}^{c}(x_{1},t_{1})
.
By the continuity of
x=x(t)
and

\Delta_{J_{n_{1}}}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})\cap\Delta_{J_{n_{2}}}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})=\varnothing
for any
n_{1}\not=n_{2}
,
the forward generalized characteristic
x=x(t)
on
[t_{0},t_{1})
contains in the same
\Delta_{J_{n}}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})
.
∎

Theorem 5.3

(Structures of entropy solutions inside the backward characteristic triangles)
.

For any
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
,
the entropy solution
u(x,t)
on
\mathbb{R}\times(0,t_{0})
as in
(
5.5
)

satisfies the following properties
:

(i)

If
(x,t)\in\Delta_{\partial\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})
,
then
u(x{-},t)=u(x{+},t)
,
and
u(x{\pm},t)\in\partial\mathcal{U}(x_{0},t_{0})
.

(ii)

If there exists
I_{m}=(a_{m},b_{m})
,
then solution
u(x,t)
in
\Delta_{I_{m}}(x_{0},t_{0})
can be concretely expressed by

(5.12)

u(x,t)=(f^{\prime})^{-1}(\frac{x-x_{0}}{t-t_{0}})\in I_{m},

and the initial data function
\varphi(x)
satisfies

(5.13)

\varphi(x)=(f^{\prime})^{-1}(\frac{x_{0}-x}{t_{0}})\qquad\,\,\,{a.e.}\ x\in[x_{0}-t_{0}f^{\prime}(b_{m}),\,x_{0}-t_{0}f^{\prime}(a_{m})].

(iii)

If there exists
J_{n}=(c_{n},d_{n})
,
then, for any
(x_{1},t_{1})\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})
,
the forward generalized characteristic
X(x_{1},t_{1}):x=x(t)
for
t\geq t_{1}

passes through point
(x_{0},t_{0})
,
and there exists
\underline{t}<t_{0}
such that

(5.14)

u(x(t){+},t)<u(x(t){-},t)\qquad\,{\rm on}\ t\in(\underline{t},t_{0}].

Furthermore, let
X(x_{i},t_{i}):\,x=x_{i}(t)
for
t\geq t_{i}

be the forward generalized characteristics with respect to two different points
(x_{i},t_{i})\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})
for
i=1,2
.
Then there exists

\bar{t}
with
\max\{t_{1},t_{2}\}<\bar{t}<t_{0}
such that

(5.15)

x_{1}(t)=x_{2}(t)\qquad{\rm on}\ [\bar{t},t_{0}].

Moreover, the set of discontinuous points of the entropy solution
u(x,t)
in
\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0}):

\Gamma(\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})):=\big\{(x,t)\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})\,:\,u(x{+},t)<u(x{-},t)\big\}

is path-connected.

(iv)

For any point
(x_{1},t_{1})\in\Delta_{({-}\infty,u^{+}_{0})}(x_{0},t_{0})\cup\Delta_{(u^{-}_{0},\infty)}(x_{0},t_{0})
,
the forward generalized characteristic of point
(x_{1},t_{1})
do not pass point
(x_{0},t_{0})
.

In the above,

\partial\mathcal{U}(x_{0},t_{0})
,

\Delta_{\partial\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})
,
\Delta_{I_{m}}(x_{0},t_{0})
with
I_{m}
,
and
\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})
with
J_{n}

are given by
(
5.1
)
–
(
5.4
)
.
See also

Fig.

2.3
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into four steps accordingly.

1.
If
(x,t)\in\Delta_{\partial\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})
,
there exists
c\in\partial\mathcal{U}(x_{0},t_{0})
such that
(x,t)\in l_{c}(x_{0},t_{0})

which, by
(
2.44
)
, implies that
u(x{+},t)=u(x{-},t)=c
.

2.
Suppose that
I_{m}=(a_{m},b_{m})\neq\varnothing
.
Since
I_{m}\subset\mathring{\mathcal{U}}(x_{0},t_{0})\subset\mathcal{U}(x_{0},t_{0})
,
then, for any
(x,t)\in\Delta_{I_{m}}(x_{0},t_{0})
,
there exists
c\in I_{m}\subset\mathcal{U}(x_{0},t_{0})
such that
(x,t)\in l_{c}(x_{0},t_{0})

which, by
(
2.44
)
, implies that
u(x{\pm},t)=c
. Thus,

x=x_{0}+(t-t_{0})f^{\prime}(c)=x_{0}+(t-t_{0})f^{\prime}(u(x{\pm},t))

which, by a simple calculation,
(
5.12
)
follows.

To prove
(
5.13
)
, let
\bar{\Delta}_{I_{m}}(x_{0},t_{0})
be the closure of
\Delta_{I_{m}}(x_{0},t_{0})
.
Then
(f^{\prime})^{-1}(\frac{x-x_{0}}{t-t_{0}})
is continuous on the trapezoidal area

\bar{\Delta}_{I_{m}}(x_{0},t_{0})\cap(\mathbb{R}\times[0,\frac{t_{0}}{2}])

so that it is uniformly continuous.
By
(
2.2
)
and the Lebesgue differential theorem,
for
x\in(x_{0}-t_{0}f^{\prime}(b_{m}),\,x_{0}-t_{0}f^{\prime}(a_{m}))
,

\displaystyle\varphi(x)

\displaystyle\mathop{=}\limits^{a.e.}\lim\limits_{x^{\prime}\rightarrow x}\frac{1}{x^{\prime}-x}\int_{x}^{x^{\prime}}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi=\lim\limits_{x^{\prime}\rightarrow x}\bigg(\frac{1}{x^{\prime}-x}\,\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow 0{+}}\int_{x}^{x^{\prime}}u(\xi,t)\,{\rm d}\xi\bigg)

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{x^{\prime}\rightarrow x}\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow 0{+}}\bigg(\frac{1}{x^{\prime}-x}\int_{x}^{x^{\prime}}(f^{\prime})^{-1}(\frac{\xi-x_{0}}{t-t_{0}})\,{\rm d}\xi\bigg)

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow 0{+}}\bigg(\lim\limits_{x^{\prime}\rightarrow x}\frac{1}{x^{\prime}-x}\int_{x}^{x^{\prime}}(f^{\prime})^{-1}(\frac{\xi-x_{0}}{t-t_{0}})\,{\rm d}\xi\bigg)

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow 0{+}}(f^{\prime})^{-1}(\frac{x-x_{0}}{t-t_{0}})=(f^{\prime})^{-1}(\frac{x_{0}-x}{t_{0}}).

3.
Suppose that
J_{n}=(c_{n},d_{n})\neq\varnothing
.
From Proposition
5.1
, for any
(x_{1},t_{1})\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})
,

\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})\subset\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})\subset\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})

so that, by Lemma
5.2
,
(x_{0},t_{0})
lies on
X(x_{1},t_{1})
.

(a).
We first prove
(
5.14
)
. Let

\underline{t}:=\inf\big\{t\in[t_{1},\infty)\,:\,u(x(t){+},t)<u(x(t){-},t)\,\,\,{\rm for}\ (x(t),t)\in X(x_{1},t_{1})\big\}.

Since
J_{n}\neq\varnothing
, then
u(x_{0}{+},t_{0})<u(x_{0}{-},t_{0})
so that
\underline{t}\leq t_{0}
.
We claim that
\underline{t}<t_{0}
.
Otherwise, if
\underline{t}=t_{0}
, then
u(x(t){+},t)=u(x(t){-},t)
for any
t\in(t_{1},t_{0})

so that, by
(
5.7
)
,

\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})\subset\Delta_{I}(x(t),t)=l_{u(x(t){-},t)}(x(t),t).

Thus,
(x_{1},t_{1})\in l_{u(x(t){-},t)}(x(t),t)
so that,
by
(
2.44
)
,
u(x(t){-},t)=u(x_{1},t_{1})
for
t\in(t_{1},t_{0})
.
This means that
X(x_{1},t_{1})
satisfies

(5.16)

x(t)=x_{1}-(t_{1}-t)f^{\prime}(u(x_{1},t_{1}))\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ t\in(t_{1},t_{0}).

Since
X(x_{1},t_{1})
is continuous, then
\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{-}}x(t)=x_{0}

so that, by
(
2.42
)
,

(5.17)

u(x_{1},t_{1})=\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{-}}u(x(t){-},t)\in\mathcal{U}(x_{0},t_{0}).

Noting that
(x_{1},t_{1})\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})
,
and letting
t\rightarrow t_{0}{-}
in
(
5.16
)
,
we obtain

u(x_{1},t_{1})=(f^{\prime})^{-1}(\frac{x_{1}-x_{0}}{t_{1}-t_{0}})\in(c_{n},d_{n})\subset\mathcal{U}^{c}(x_{0},t_{0}),

which contradicts to
(
5.17
)
.
Thus,
\underline{t}<t_{0}
and
(
5.14
)
follows.

(b).
We now prove
(
5.15
)
.
By Lemma
5.2
,

(x_{i},t_{i})\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})\subset\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})
for
i=1,2
, which
imply that
X(x_{i},t_{i})
passes
(x_{0},t_{0})
.
Since
x_{1}(t_{0})=x_{0}=x_{2}(t_{0})
, we let

\bar{t}:=\inf\big\{t\in(0,\infty)\,:\,x_{1}(t)=x_{2}(t)\big\}.

Then

\bar{t}\leq t_{0}
.
To prove
(
5.15
)
, it suffices to show that
\bar{t}<t_{0}
.

Otherwise, if
\bar{t}=t_{0}
, then
x_{1}(t)\neq x_{2}(t)
for
t<t_{0}
.
Without loss of generality, assume that
x_{1}(t)<x_{2}(t)
.
From
(
5.14
)
, there exists
\tilde{t}<t_{0}
such that

u(x_{1}(t){+},t)<u(x_{1}(t){-},t),\quad u(x_{2}(t){+},t)<u(x_{2}(t){-},t)\,\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ t\in[\tilde{t},t_{0}],

so that, for any
t\in[\tilde{t},t_{0}]
,

\displaystyle x_{0}-t_{0}f^{\prime}(d_{n})

\displaystyle\leq y^{-}(x_{1}(\tilde{t}),\tilde{t})<y^{+}(x_{1}(\tilde{t}),\tilde{t})\leq y^{+}(x_{1}(t),t)

(5.18)

\displaystyle\leq y^{-}(x_{2}(t),t)\leq y^{-}(x_{2}(\tilde{t}),\tilde{t})<y^{+}(x_{2}(\tilde{t}),\tilde{t})\leq x_{0}-t_{0}f^{\prime}(c_{n}),

where
y^{\pm}(x_{i}(t),t):=x_{i}(t)-tf^{\prime}(u(x_{i}(t){\pm},t))
for
i=1,2
.

Since
y^{+}(x_{1}(t),t)
is nondecreasing in
t
,
letting
t\rightarrow t_{0}{-}
in
(
5.18
)
,

x_{0}-t_{0}f^{\prime}(d_{n})<y^{+}(x_{1}(\tilde{t}),\tilde{t})\leq y_{0}:=\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{-}}y^{+}(x_{1}(t),t)\leq y^{-}(x_{2}(\tilde{t}),\tilde{t})<x_{0}-t_{0}f^{\prime}(c_{n}),

which implies

(5.19)

(f^{\prime})^{-1}(\frac{x_{0}-y_{0}}{t_{0}})\in(c_{n},d_{n})\subset\mathcal{U}^{c}(x_{0},t_{0}).

On the other hand, since
X(x_{1},t_{1}):x=x_{1}(t)
for
t\geq t_{1}

is continuous and
x_{1}(t_{0})=x_{0}
, then, by
(
2.41
)
,
it follows from
y^{+}(x_{1}(t),t)

=x_{1}(t)-tf^{\prime}(u(x_{1}(t){+},t))
that

(f^{\prime})^{-1}(\frac{x_{0}-y_{0}}{t_{0}})=\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{-}}u(x_{1}(t){+},t)\in\mathcal{U}(x_{0},t_{0}),

which contradicts to
(
5.19
)
.
Thus,
\bar{t}<t_{0}
. By the definition of
\bar{t}
,
(
5.15
)
holds.

(c).
Moreover, for any two points
(x_{i},t_{i})\in\Gamma(\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0}))
for
i=1,2
,
by Lemma
5.2
(ii), the forward generalized characteristics
X(x_{1},t_{1})
and
X(x_{2},t_{2})

are both discontinuities of the entropy solution
u(x,t)
so that

(
5.15
)
directly implies that
\Gamma(\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0}))
is path-connected.

4
. For any point
(x_{1},t_{1})\in\Delta_{({-}\infty,u^{+}_{0})}(x_{0},t_{0})\cup\Delta_{(u^{-}_{0},\infty)}(x_{0},t_{0})
,
then
(x_{1},t_{1})\not\in\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})
.
From
t_{1}<t_{0}
,
(x_{0},t_{0})\not\in\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})
.
Then, by Proposition
5.1
,
\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})\cap\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})=\varnothing
.
On the other hand, it follows from
(
5.7
)
that,
if
(x_{0},t_{0})\in X(x_{1},t_{1})
, then
\Delta_{I}(x_{1},t_{1})\subset\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})
.
This is a contradiction.
Thus,
(x_{0},t_{0})\not\in X(x_{1},t_{1})
,
which means that the forward generalized characteristic
X(x_{1},t_{1})
of point
(x_{1},t_{1})
do not pass point
(x_{0},t_{0})
.

Up to now, we have completed the proof of Theorem
5.3
.
∎

Remark 5.1
.

From Theorem
5.3
,
every shock curve passing through
(x_{0},t_{0})
must contain
in the backward characteristic triangle
\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})
when
t<t_{0}
.
Furthermore,
since
\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})\cap\Delta_{J_{n^{\prime}}}(x_{0},t_{0})=\varnothing
for any
n\neq n^{\prime}
,
two shock curves passing through
(x_{0},t_{0})
collide with each other at a time
t<t_{0}

if and only if
they are contained in the same triangle
\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})
,
and two shock curves passing through
(x_{0},t_{0})
collide with each other at
t=t_{0}

if and only if
they are contained in two different triangles
\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})
.
This means that
there is a one-to-one correspondence
between the path-connected branches of
the set of discontinuous points in
\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})

and the disjoint intervals in
\mathcal{U}^{c}(x_{0},t_{0})=\cup_{n}J_{n}
.

5.2.
Directional limits of entropy solutions at discontinuous points

For any fixed
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
, if
u(x_{0}{+},t_{0})<u(x_{0}{-},t_{0})
,
the forward generalized characteristic
X(x_{0},t_{0}):x=x(t)
for
t\geq t_{0}
is a continuous single-valued curve,
along which the entropy solution
u(x,t)
is discontinuous.
Denote

l^{\pm}(t):=x_{0}+(t-t_{0})f^{\prime}(u(x_{0}{\pm},t_{0}))\qquad{\rm for}\ t\in(0,t_{0}),

and let

(5.20)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\Delta^{-}(x_{0},t_{0}):=\big\{(x,t)\,:\,x<x(t)\ {\rm if}\ t\geq t_{0},\,\,\,x<l^{-}(t)\ {\rm if}\ t\in(0,t_{0})\big\},\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\Delta^{+}(x_{0},t_{0}):=\big\{(x,t)\,:\,x>x(t)\ {\rm if}\ t\geq t_{0},\,\,\,x>l^{+}(t)\ {\rm if}\ t\in(0,t_{0})\big\}.\end{cases}

If
\mathcal{U}^{c}(x_{0},t_{0})=\cup_{n}J_{n}\neq\varnothing
, there exists
J_{n}\neq\varnothing
.
Let
X(x_{n},t_{n}):x=x_{n}(t)
for
t\geq t_{n}

be the forward generalized characteristic of point
(x_{n},t_{n})\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})
.
Then, by Theorem
5.3
,
there exists
\hat{t}_{n}\in(t_{n},t_{0})
such that

u(x_{n}(t){+},t)<u(x_{n}(t){-},t)
for any
t\in[\hat{t}_{n},t_{0}]
.
We set

(5.21)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\Delta^{-}_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0}):=\big\{(x,t)\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})\,:\,x<x_{n}(t)\,\,\,{\rm for}\ t\in(\hat{t}_{n},t_{0})\big\},\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\Delta^{+}_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0}):=\big\{(x,t)\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})\,:\,x>x_{n}(t)\,\,\,{\rm for}\ t\in(\hat{t}_{n},t_{0})\big\}.\end{cases}

The directional limits of entropy solutions at the discontinuous points are as follows:

Theorem 5.4

(Directional limits of entropy solutions)
.

For any
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
with
u(x_{0}{+},t_{0})<u(x_{0}{-},t_{0})
,

(i)

If
x=x(t)
for
t\geq t_{0}
is the forward generalized characteristic
X(x_{0},t_{0})
of point
(x_{0},t_{0})
, then

(5.22)

\lim_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{3}{(x,t)\rightarrow(x_0,t_0)}{(x,t)\in\Delta^-(x_0,t_0)}}u(x{\pm},t)=u(x_{0}{-},t_{0}),\qquad\lim_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{3}{(x,t)\rightarrow(x_0,t_0)}{(x,t)\in\Delta^+(x_0,t_0)}}u(x{\pm},t)=u(x_{0}{+},t_{0}),

(5.23)

\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{+}}u(x(t){-},t)=u(x_{0}{-},t_{0}),\qquad\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{+}}u(x(t){+},t)=u(x_{0}{+},t_{0}).

(ii)

If there exists
J_{n}\subset\mathcal{U}^{c}(x_{0},t_{0})
such that
J_{n}=(c_{n},d_{n})\neq\varnothing
, then

(5.24)

\lim_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{3}{(x,t)\rightarrow(x_0,t_0)}{(x,t)\in\Delta^-_{J_n}(x_0,t_0)}}u(x{\pm},t)=d_{n},\qquad\lim_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{3}{(x,t)\rightarrow(x_0,t_0)}{(x,t)\in\Delta^+_{J_n}(x_0,t_0)}}u(x{\pm},t)=c_{n},

(5.25)

\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{-}}u(x_{n}(t){-},t)=d_{n},\qquad\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{-}}u(x_{n}(t){+},t)=c_{n}.

In the above,
\Delta^{\pm}(x_{0},t_{0})
and
\Delta^{\pm}_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})

are given by
(
5.20
)
and
(
5.21
)
, respectively
;

and the curve
x=x_{n}(t)
for
t\geq t_{n}

is the forward generalized characteristic
X(x_{n},t_{n})
of any point
(x_{n},t_{n})\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})
.
See also
Fig.

5.3
.

Figure 5.3.
The directional areas
\Delta^{\pm}(x_{0},t_{0})
and
\Delta_{J_{n}}^{\pm}(x_{0},t_{0})
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps accordingly.

1
. From
(
2.41
)
, for any
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
,

(5.26)

\limsup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{3}{(x,t)\rightarrow(x_0,t_0)}{(x,t)\in\Delta^-(x_0,t_0)}}u(x{\pm},t)\leq u(x_{0}{-},t_{0}),\qquad\liminf_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{3}{(x,t)\rightarrow(x_0,t_0)}{(x,t)\in\Delta^+(x_0,t_0)}}u(x{\pm},t)\geq u(x_{0}{+},t_{0}).

Denote
y^{\pm}(x,t):=x-tf^{\prime}(u(x{\pm},t))
.
Then, from Lemma
2.8
,

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle y^{\pm}(x,t)\leq y^{-}(x(t),t)\leq y^{-}(x_{0},t_{0})\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ (x,t)\in\Delta^{-}(x_{0},t_{0}),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle y^{\pm}(x,t)\geq y^{+}(x(t),t)\geq y^{+}(x_{0},t_{0})\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ (x,t)\in\Delta^{+}(x_{0},t_{0}),\end{cases}

so that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle x\leq y^{-}(x_{0},t_{0})+tf^{\prime}(u(x\pm,t))\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ (x,t)\in\Delta^{-}(x_{0},t_{0}),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle x\geq y^{+}(x_{0},t_{0})+tf^{\prime}(u(x\pm,t))\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ (x,t)\in\Delta^{+}(x_{0},t_{0}),\end{cases}

which, by the strictly increasing property of
f^{\prime}(u)
, implies

(5.27)

\liminf_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{3}{(x,t)\rightarrow(x_0,t_0)}{(x,t)\in\Delta^-(x_0,t_0)}}u(x\pm,t)\geq u(x_{0}{-},t_{0}),\qquad\limsup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{3}{(x,t)\rightarrow(x_0,t_0)}{(x,t)\in\Delta^+(x_0,t_0)}}u(x\pm,t)\leq u(x_{0}{+},t_{0}).

Combining
(
5.26
)
with
(
5.27
)
leads to
(
5.22
)
.

From
(
5.7
)
in Lemma
5.2
,

y^{-}(x(t),t)\leq y^{-}(x_{0},t_{0})\leq y^{+}(x_{0},t_{0})\leq y^{+}(x(t),t),

so that, from
\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{+}}x(t)=x_{0}
,
by letting
t\rightarrow t_{0}{+}
, we obtain

\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{+}}u(x(t){-},t)\geq u(x_{0}{-},t_{0}),\qquad\mathop{\overline{\lim}}\limits_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{+}}u(x(t){+},t)\leq u(x_{0}{+},t_{0}),

which, by
(
2.41
)
, implies
(
5.23
)
.

2
. From Proposition
5.1
, for any
(x,t)\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})
,
we see that
\Delta_{I}(x,t)\subset\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})
so that,
for any
(x,t)\in\Delta^{+}_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})
,

x_{0}-t_{0}f^{\prime}(d_{n})\leq y^{-}(x_{n}(\hat{t}_{n}),\hat{t}_{n})<y^{+}(x_{n}(\hat{t}_{n}),\hat{t}_{n})\leq y^{+}(x_{n}(t),t)\leq y^{\pm}(x,t)\leq x_{0}-t_{0}f^{\prime}(c_{n}),

and, for any
(x,t)\in\Delta^{-}_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})
,

x_{0}-t_{0}f^{\prime}(d_{n})\leq y^{\pm}(x,t)\leq y^{-}(x_{n}(t),t)\leq y^{-}(x_{n}(\hat{t}_{n}),\hat{t}_{n})<y^{+}(x_{n}(\hat{t}_{n}),\hat{t}_{n})\leq x_{0}-tf^{\prime}(c_{n}).

Since
f^{\prime}(u)
is strictly increasing,
letting
(x,t)\in\Delta_{J_{n}}^{\pm}(x_{0},t_{0})\rightarrow(x_{0},t_{0})
respectively,
we obtain

(5.28)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle c_{n}\leq\liminf\limits_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{3}{(x,t)\rightarrow(x_0,t_0)}{(x,t)\in\Delta^+_{J_n}(x_0,t_0)}}u(x{\pm},t)\leq\limsup\limits_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{3}{(x,t)\rightarrow(x_0,t_0)}{(x,t)\in\Delta^+_{J_n}(x_0,t_0)}}u(x{\pm},t)<d_{n},\\[14.22636pt]
\displaystyle c_{n}<\liminf\limits_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{3}{(x,t)\rightarrow(x_0,t_0)}{(x,t)\in\Delta^-_{J_n}(x_0,t_0)}}u(x{\pm},t)\leq\limsup\limits_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{3}{(x,t)\rightarrow(x_0,t_0)}{(x,t)\in\Delta^-_{J_n}(x_0,t_0)}}u(x{\pm},t)\leq d_{n}.\end{cases}

From
(
2.41
)
, the upper- and lower-limits in
(
5.28
)
are all in
\mathcal{U}(x_{0},t_{0})
,
which, by
[c_{n},d_{n}]\cap\mathcal{U}(x_{0},t_{0})=\{c_{n},d_{n}\}
, yields
(
5.24
)
.

From
\Delta_{I}(x_{n}(t),t)\subset\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})

and
u(x_{n}(\hat{t}_{n}){+},\hat{t}_{n})<u(x_{n}(\hat{t}_{n}){-},\hat{t}_{n})
,

x_{0}-t_{0}f^{\prime}(d_{n})\leq y^{-}(x_{n}(t),t)\leq y^{-}(x_{n}(\hat{t}),\hat{t})<y^{+}(x_{n}(\hat{t}),\hat{t})\leq y^{+}(x_{n}(t),t)\leq x_{0}-t_{0}f^{\prime}(c_{n}),

so that, from
\lim_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{-}}x_{n}(t)=x_{0}
,
by letting
t\rightarrow t_{0}{-}
, we obtain

\begin{cases}\displaystyle c_{n}<\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{-}}u(x_{n}(t){-},t)\leq\mathop{\overline{\lim}}\limits_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{-}}u(x_{n}(t){-},t)\leq d_{n},\\[11.38109pt]
\displaystyle c_{n}\leq\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{-}}u(x_{n}(t){+},t)\leq\mathop{\overline{\lim}}\limits_{t\rightarrow t_{0}{-}}u(x_{n}(t){+},t)<d_{n},\end{cases}

which, by
[c_{n},d_{n}]\cap\mathcal{U}(x_{0},t_{0})=\{c_{n},d_{n}\}
, yields
(
5.25
)
.
∎

Remark 5.2
.

From Lemma
2.9
, the limit of the entropy solution
u(x,t)

under any sequence
(x_{n},t_{n})\rightarrow(x_{0},t_{0})
contains in
\mathcal{U}(x_{0},t_{0})
.
Furthermore, from Theorem
5.4
,
the directional limit should be
what a point in
\mathcal{U}(x_{0},t_{0})
in the respective case is described exactly.
We point out
that
the directional limits in any triangle
\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})
are independent of
the choice of the forward generalized characteristics
.

5.3.
Left- and right-derivatives of shock curves and the piecewise smoothness

From Lemma
5.2
, if
u(x_{0}{+},t_{0})<u(x_{0}{-},t_{0})
,
the forward generalized characteristic
X(x_{0},t_{0}):x=x(t)
for
t\geq t_{0}

is a single-valued continuous curve along which the entropy solution
u(x,t)
is discontinuous,
i.e.
,

u(x(t){+},t)<u(x(t){-},t)
for any
t>t_{0}
.
The opposite is also true,
which can be seen in the following theorem about the general structures of shocks.

Theorem 5.5

(General structures of shocks)
.

Let
x=x(t)
for
t\geq t_{0}
be a single-valued continuous curve,
along which the entropy solution
u(x,t)
is discontinuous,
i.e.
,

u(x(t){+},t)<u(x(t){-},t)
for any
t>t_{0}
.
Then
x=x(t)
is the forward generalized characteristic of
(x_{0},t_{0})
,
which can be described by the triangle sequence
\{\Delta_{I}(x(t),t)\}

and a unique triangle sequence
\{\Delta_{J_{n}}(x(t),t)\}

with
J_{n}\subset\mathcal{U}^{c}(x(t),t)
as

(5.29)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\Delta_{I}(x_{0},t_{0})\subset\Delta_{I}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})\subset\Delta_{I}(x(t_{2}),t_{2})\quad&{\rm for\ any}\ t_{2}>t_{1}>t_{0},\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle\Delta_{J_{n}}(x_{0},t_{0})\subset\Delta_{J_{n}}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})\subset\Delta_{J_{n}}(x(t_{2}),t_{2})\quad&{\rm for\ any}\ t_{2}>t_{1}>t_{0}.\end{cases}

Furthermore, the
curve
:

x=x(t)
for
t\geq t_{0}
is a Lipschitz continuous shock such that

(i)

For any
t>t_{0}
,
x=x(t)
is differentiable from the right, and

(5.30)

\frac{\,{\rm d}^{+}x(t)}{\,{\rm d}t}:=\lim_{t^{\prime}\rightarrow t{+}}\frac{x(t^{\prime})-x(t)}{t^{\prime}-t}=\frac{f(u(x(t){-},t))-f(u(x(t){+},t))}{u(x(t){-},t)-u(x(t){+},t)}=:\frac{[f(u)]}{[u]}\Big|_{I}.

(ii)

For any
t>t_{0}
,
x=x(t)
is differentiable from the left, and

(5.31)

\frac{\,{\rm d}^{-}x(t)}{\,{\rm d}t}:=\lim_{t^{\prime}\rightarrow t{-}}\frac{x(t^{\prime})-x(t)}{t^{\prime}-t}=\frac{f(d_{n})-f(c_{n})}{d_{n}-c_{n}}=:\frac{[f(u)]}{[u]}\Big|_{J_{n}}

for the
J_{n}=(c_{n},d_{n})\subset\mathcal{U}^{c}(x(t),t)

such that
(x(\tau),\tau)\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x(t),t)
on
[t_{0},t)
.

(iii)

Except at most countable points of
t
with
t>t_{0}
,

\frac{\,{\rm d}^{+}x(t)}{\,{\rm d}t}=\frac{\,{\rm d}^{-}x(t)}{\,{\rm d}t}
.

In the above,
\Delta_{I}(x,t)
with
I

and
\Delta_{J_{n}}(x,t)
with
J_{n}

are given by
(
5.1
)
–
(
5.4
)
.
See also
Fig.

5.4
.

Figure 5.4.
The general structures of shocks
as in Theorem
5.5
.

Proof.

From Lemma
5.2
, for any
t>t_{0}
,
there exists a unique point
(x,t)
such that
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\Delta_{I}(x,t)
.
To prove
(
5.29
)
for the triangle sequence
\{\Delta_{I}(x(t),t)\}
,
it suffices to show that
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\Delta_{I}(x(t),t)
for any
t>t_{0}
.
This follows by applying Step 2 in the proof of Lemma
5.2
to curve
x=x(t)
.
Moreover, by Lemma
5.2
, for any
t>t_{0}
,
there exists a unique
J_{n}\subset\mathcal{U}^{c}(x(t),t)
such that

(x(\tau),\tau)\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x(t),t)
for any
\tau\in[t_{0},t)
.
This means that
(
5.29
)
holds for the triangle sequence
\{\Delta_{J_{n}}(x(t),t)\}
.

1
. For any
t^{\prime}>t
, denote
u^{\pm}(t^{\prime}):=u(x(t^{\prime}){\pm},t^{\prime})
.
From
(
2.18
)
,

u^{\pm}(t^{\prime})\in\mathcal{U}(x(t^{\prime}),t^{\prime};\,t)
so that,
by
(
2.16
)
–
(
2.19
)
,

E(u^{-}(t^{\prime});\,x(t^{\prime}),t^{\prime};\,t)=E(u^{+}(t^{\prime});\,x(t^{\prime}),t^{\prime};\,t),

which is equivalent to

(5.32)

(t^{\prime}-t)\int^{u^{-}(t^{\prime})}_{u^{+}(t^{\prime})}f^{\prime\prime}(s)\big(u(x(t^{\prime})-(t^{\prime}-t)f^{\prime}(s),t)-s\big)\,{\rm d}s=0.

Let
x_{\pm}(t^{\prime}):=x(t^{\prime})-(t^{\prime}-t)f^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t^{\prime}))
.
Taking
\xi=x(t^{\prime})-(t^{\prime}-t)f^{\prime}(s)
into
(
5.32
)
,

(5.33)

\int^{x_{-}(t^{\prime})}_{x(t)}u(\xi,t)\,{\rm d}\xi+\int^{x(t)}_{x_{+}(t^{\prime})}u(\xi,t)\,{\rm d}\xi=-(t^{\prime}-t)\int^{u^{-}(t^{\prime})}_{u^{+}(t^{\prime})}sf^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s.

Denote
U_{\pm}:=U_{\pm}(t^{\prime},t)
by

U_{\pm}=U_{\pm}(t^{\prime},t):=\frac{1}{x_{\pm}(t^{\prime})-x(t)}\int^{x_{\pm}(t^{\prime})}_{x(t)}u(\xi,t)\,{\rm d}\xi.

From
(
2.42
)
and
(
5.23
)
,

\lim_{t^{\prime}\rightarrow t{+}}u^{\pm}(t^{\prime})=u(x(t){\pm},t)
and

\lim_{x\rightarrow x(t){\pm}}u(x,t)=u(x(t){\pm},t)
.
By
x_{-}(t^{\prime})<x(t)<x_{+}(t^{\prime})
and
\lim_{t^{\prime}\rightarrow t{+}}x_{\pm}(t^{\prime})=x(t)
, then

\lim_{t^{\prime}\rightarrow t{+}}U_{\pm}(t^{\prime},t)=u(x(t){\pm},t)

which, by
u(x(t){-},t)>u(x(t){+},t)
, implies that

U_{-}(t^{\prime},t)-U_{+}(t^{\prime},t)\neq 0

for sufficiently small
t^{\prime}-t>0
.
Thus, by taking
U_{\pm}=U_{\pm}(t^{\prime},t)
into
(
5.33
)
,

U_{-}\,\big(x_{-}(t^{\prime})-x(t)\big)-U_{+}\,\big(x_{+}(t^{\prime})-x(t)\big)=-(t^{\prime}-t)\int^{u^{-}(t^{\prime})}_{u^{+}(t^{\prime})}sf^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s,

so that, by a simple calculation, we obtain

\frac{x(t^{\prime})-x(t)}{t^{\prime}-t}=\frac{f(u^{-}(t^{\prime}))-f(u^{+}(t^{\prime}))+\big(U_{-}-u^{-}(t^{\prime})\big)f^{\prime}(u^{-}(t^{\prime}))-\big(U_{+}-u^{+}(t^{\prime})\big)f^{\prime}(u^{+}(t^{\prime}))}{U_{-}-U_{+}},

which, by letting
t^{\prime}\rightarrow t{+}
, yields
(
5.30
)
.

2
. Since
x=x(t)
for
t\geq t_{0}
is the forward generalized characteristic of
(x_{0},t_{0})
,
then, by Lemma
5.2
, for any
t>t_{0}
,
there exists
J_{n}=(c_{n},d_{n})\subset\mathcal{U}^{c}(x(t),t)
such that

(x(\tau),\tau)\in\Delta_{J_{n}}(x(t),t)
for
\tau\in[t_{0},t)
.
From
(
2.18
)
,
for any
t^{\prime}<t
,
\,c_{n},d_{n}\in\mathcal{U}(x(t),t)=\mathcal{U}(x(t),t;t^{\prime})
so that,
by
(
2.16
)
–
(
2.19
)
,

E(d_{n};x(t),t;t^{\prime})=E(c_{n};x(t),t;t^{\prime})
,
i.e.
,

(t-t^{\prime})\int^{d_{n}}_{c_{n}}f^{\prime\prime}(s)\big(u(x(t)-(t-t^{\prime})f^{\prime}(s),t^{\prime})-s\big)\,{\rm d}s=0,

which, by letting
y_{-}(t^{\prime}):=x(t)-(t-t^{\prime})f^{\prime}(d_{n})
and
y_{+}(t^{\prime}):=x(t)-(t-t^{\prime})f^{\prime}(c_{n})
, implies

(5.34)

\int^{y_{-}(t^{\prime})}_{y_{+}(t^{\prime})}u(\xi,t^{\prime})\,{\rm d}\xi=-(t-t^{\prime})\int^{d_{n}}_{c_{n}}sf^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s.

Denote
V_{\pm}:=V_{\pm}(t^{\prime},t)
by

V_{\pm}=V_{\pm}(t^{\prime},t):=\frac{1}{y_{\pm}(t^{\prime})-x(t^{\prime})}\int^{y_{\pm}(t^{\prime})}_{x(t^{\prime})}u(\xi,t^{\prime})\,{\rm d}\xi.

Since
y_{-}(t^{\prime})<x(t^{\prime})<y_{+}(t^{\prime})
and
\lim_{t^{\prime}\rightarrow t{-}}y_{\pm}(t^{\prime})=x(t)
,
by Theorem
5.4
(ii),

\lim_{t^{\prime}\rightarrow t{-}}V_{-}(t^{\prime},t)=d_{n},\qquad\lim_{t^{\prime}\rightarrow t{-}}V_{+}(t^{\prime},t)=c_{n},

which, by
d_{n}>c_{n}
, implies that

V_{-}(t^{\prime},t)-V_{+}(t^{\prime},t)\neq 0
for sufficiently small
t^{\prime}-t<0
.
Thus, by taking
V_{\pm}=V_{\pm}(t^{\prime},t)
into
(
5.34
)
,

V_{-}\,\big(y_{-}(t^{\prime})-x(t^{\prime})\big)-V_{+}\,\big(y_{+}(t^{\prime})-x(t^{\prime})\big)=-(t-t^{\prime})\int^{d_{n}}_{c_{n}}sf^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s,

so that, by a simple calculation, we obtain

\frac{x(t^{\prime})-x(t)}{t^{\prime}-t}=\frac{f(d_{n})-f(c_{n})+\big(V_{-}-d_{n}\big)f^{\prime}(d_{n})-\big(V_{+}-c_{n}\big)f^{\prime}(c_{n})}{V_{-}-V_{+}},

which, by letting
t^{\prime}\rightarrow t{-}
, yields
(
5.31
)
.

Since
u(x{\pm},t)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+})
,

(
5.30
)
–
(
5.31
)
imply that

x=x(t)
for
t\geq t_{0}
is a Lipschitz continuous shock curve.

3
. For any
t>t_{0}
, denote
u^{\pm}(t):=u(x(t){\pm},t)
.
If
\mathcal{U}(x(t),t)=\big\{u^{+}(t),u^{-}(t)\big\}
,
then
d_{n}=u^{-}(t)
and
c_{n}=u^{+}(t)
so that

\frac{{\rm d}^{+}x(t)}{{\rm d}t}=\frac{{\rm d}^{-}x(t)}{{\rm d}t}
.

On the other hand, if there exists
t_{1}>t_{0}
such that

(5.35)

\frac{{\rm d}^{+}x(t)}{{\rm d}t}\Big|_{t=t_{1}}\neq\frac{{\rm d}^{-}x(t)}{{\rm d}t}\Big|_{t=t_{1}},

then
d_{n}\neq u^{-}(t_{1})
or
c_{n}\neq u^{+}(t_{1})
so that

J_{n}(t_{1}):=J_{n}=(c_{n},d_{n})\subsetneqq[u^{+}(t_{1}),u^{-}(t_{1})].

Then, from
(
5.3
)
, there exists
J_{n^{\prime}}
or
I_{m}\subset[u^{+}(t_{1}),u^{-}(t_{1})]
such that

J_{n^{\prime}}\cap J_{n}=\varnothing
or
I_{m}\cap J_{n}=\varnothing
, respectively.
Denote the open interval
J_{n^{\prime}}
or
I_{m}
by
J^{\prime}_{n}(t_{1})
.
Then
J^{\prime}_{n}(t_{1})\cap J_{n}(t_{1})=\varnothing
.

We set

(5.36)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle Y_{n}(t_{1}):=\big\{y\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,y=x(t_{1})-t_{1}f^{\prime}(s)\quad{\rm for}\ s\in J_{n}(t_{1})\big\},\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle Y^{\prime}_{n}(t_{1}):=\big\{y\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,y=x(t_{1})-t_{1}f^{\prime}(s)\quad{\rm for}\ s\in J^{\prime}_{n}(t_{1})\big\}.\end{cases}

Then
Y_{n}(t_{1})
and
Y^{\prime}_{n}(t_{1})
are open intervals on the
x
–axis such that

Y_{n}(t_{1})\cap Y^{\prime}_{n}(t_{1})=\varnothing.

For any
t_{2}\in(t_{0},t_{1})
,
by Lemma
5.2
,
(x(\tau),\tau)\in\Delta_{J_{n}(t_{1})}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})
on
\tau\in[t_{0},t_{1})

so that
(x(t_{2}),t_{2})\in\Delta_{J_{n}(t_{1})}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})
.
If
(
5.35
)
holds for
t=t_{2}
,
by Proposition
5.1
(ii),

\Delta_{J_{n}(t_{2})}(x(t_{2}),t_{2})\cup\Delta_{J^{\prime}_{n}(t_{2})}(x(t_{2}),t_{2})\subset\Delta_{I}(x(t_{2}),t_{2})\subset\Delta_{J_{n}(t_{1})}(x(t_{1}),t_{1}),

which, by
J^{\prime}_{n}(t_{1})\cap J_{n}(t_{1})=\varnothing
, implies
that

\Delta_{J^{\prime}_{n}(t_{1})}(x(t_{1}),t_{1})\cap\Delta_{J^{\prime}_{n}(t_{2})}(x(t_{2}),t_{2})=\varnothing.

This, by
(
5.36
)
, infers that
Y^{\prime}_{n}(t_{1})\cap Y^{\prime}_{n}(t_{2})=\varnothing
.

Therefore, for any
t_{1}>t_{0}
such that
(
5.35
)
holds,
there exists an open interval
Y^{\prime}_{n}(t_{1})
on the
x
–axis such that the map:

t_{1}\mapsto Y^{\prime}_{n}(t_{1})
is one-to-one with
Y^{\prime}_{n}(t_{1})\cap Y^{\prime}_{n}(t_{2})=\varnothing
if
t_{1}\neq t_{2}
.
Since the disjoint open intervals on the
x
–axis are at most countable,
then
x=x(t),t\geq t_{0}
, has the same left- and right-derivatives
except at most countable points of
t\geq t_{0}
.

This completes the proof of Theorem
5.5
.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

Along any shock curve
x(t)
for
t\geq t_{0}
, the following equation holds:

(5.37)

E(u(x(t){-},t);\,x(t),t)=E(u(x(t){+},t);\,x(t),t)\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ t>t_{0}.

In general, we can not directly integrate the ordinary differential equation
(
5.30
)
.
However, it is interesting to see that, in general,
the expression of shock curves can be obtained by using
(
5.37
)
,
if the primitive of the initial data function
\varphi(x)
is known
.

Remark 5.4
.

Since
f^{\prime}(u)
is strictly increasing,
(
5.30
)
–
(
5.31
)

imply that, along any shock of the entropy solution
u(x,t)
,

\frac{{\rm d}^{\pm}x(t)}{{\rm d}t}\in(f^{\prime}(u(x(t){+},t)),f^{\prime}(u(x(t){-},t))).

This means that
the entropy solution
given by the solution formula
(
2.8
)

is the unique Lax’s entropy solution in

[
39
,
40
]
.
Furthermore, since
\frac{{\rm d}x(t)}{{\rm d}t}=f^{\prime}(u(x(t),t))

along any shock-free characteristic, the forward generalized characteristics are exactly
the generalized characteristics introduced by Dafermos
[
19
,
20
,
21
]
.

5.4.
Fine structures of entropy solutions on the shock sets

In this subsection, we show the fine structures of entropy solutions on the shock sets.
In fact, by combining the results in §3–4 and §5.1–5.4,
we can obtain a comprehensive understanding of the fine structures of entropy solutions on the shock set
in the upper half-plane,
which, in detail, are presented in the following theorem.

Theorem 5.6

(Fine structures of entropy solutions on the shock sets)
.

Any point
(x,t)
with
t>0
must be one of the following five cases
:

(i)

If
u(x{-},t)=u(x{+},t)
with
\mathcal{C}_{t}(x)\neq\varnothing
,
then
(x,t)

is a point that
lies in the inner of a characteristic segment.

(ii)

If
u(x{-},t)=u(x{+},t)
with
\mathcal{C}_{t}(x)=\varnothing
,
then
(x,t)
is a continuous shock generation point.
The formation and development of shocks,
especially including the optimal regularities of the entropy solution and the shock curve near the continuous shock generation point,
are given by
Theorems
4.1
and
4.4
.

(iii)

If
u(x{-},t)>u(x{+},t)
and the closed set
\mathcal{U}(x,t)=[u(x{+},t),u(x{-},t)]
,
then
(x,t)
is a discontinuous shock generation point.
The entropy solution inside the backward characteristic triangle from such points is given by
Theorem
5.3
(ii)
.

(iv)

If
u(x{-},t)>u(x{+},t)

and the open set
\mathcal{U}^{c}(x,t)\subset[u(x{+},t),u(x{-},t)]
is an open interval,
then
(x,t)
is a point of shocks,
and there is only one shock passing through point
(x,t)

that any two shocks passing point
(x,t)
must coincide with each other after some
\bar{t}<t
.

Moreover, if
\mathcal{U}^{c}(x,t)=(u(x{+},t),u(x{-},t))
,
point
(x,t)
is a regular point of shocks
;

and if
\mathcal{U}^{c}(x,t)\subsetneqq(u(x{+},t),u(x{-},t))
,
point
(x,t)
is an irregular point of shocks,
at which there is a collision of a shock with one or two centered compression waves.
The directional limits of the entropy solution at such points are
given by
Theorem
5.4
.
The left- and right-derivatives of the shock curve at such points are given by
(
5.30
)
–
(
5.31
)
.

(v)

If
u(x{-},t)>u(x{+},t)
and the open set
\mathcal{U}^{c}(x,t)

is a disjoint union of two or more open intervals,
then there are at least two shocks collided at point
(x,t)

and
(x,t)
is an irregular point of shocks,
at which there is a collision of
more than one shock with or without centered compression waves.

Furthermore, there is a one-to-one correspondence to the shocks collided at point
(x,t)

and the open intervals in the disjoint union of
\mathcal{U}^{c}(x,t)
.
The directional limits of the entropy solution
at such points are given by
Theorem
5.4
.
The right-derivative of the shock curve at such points is given by
(
5.30
)
,
and the left-derivatives of the shock branches at such points are given by
(
5.31
)
correspondingly.

In the above,
\mathcal{U}^{c}(x,t)
is the complement of
\mathcal{U}(x,t)

as in
(
5.1
)
.
See
Fig.

5.5
for the details.

Figure 5.5.
Different types of points on the shock sets.

Proof.

We divide the proof into five steps accordingly.

1
. According to Lemma
2.9
and Corollary
3.10
(ii), (i) is true.

2
.
By Lemma
2.9
,
the solution
u(x,t)
is continuous at point
(x,t)
.
From Corollary
3.10
(i),
point
(x,t)
is a continuous shock generation point.

3
.
Since
u(x{-},t)>u(x{+},t)
,
then the solution
u(x,t)
is discontinuous at point
(x,t)
.
From
\mathcal{U}(x,t)=[u(x{+},t),u(x{-},t)]
,
by Theorem
5.3
(ii) and Corollary
3.10
(i),
point
(x,t)
is a discontinuous shock generation point.

4
. Since
\mathcal{U}^{c}(x,t)
is an open interval,
there is only one
J_{n}
at point
(x,t)
.
By Theorem
5.3
(iii),
there exists only one shock passing point
(x,t)
in the sense that
any two shocks passing point
(x,t)
must coincide with each other after some time
\bar{t}<t
.

Moreover, if
\mathcal{U}^{c}(x,t)=J_{n}=(u(x{+},t),u(x{-},t))
,
then
\mathring{{\mathcal{U}}}(x,t)=\varnothing
.
Thus, there exist no centered compression waves centered at point
(x,t)
,
so that
(x,t)
is a regular point of shock curves.
If
\mathcal{U}^{c}(x,t)=J_{n}\subsetneqq(u(x{+},t),u(x{-},t))
,
then
\mathring{{\mathcal{U}}}(x,t)\not=\varnothing
.
Thus, there exists one or two
I_{m}\subset\mathring{{\mathcal{U}}}(x,t)
at point
(x,t)
,
which means that there exist one or two centered compression waves centered at point
(x,t)
.
Thus, point
(x,t)
is an irregular point of shocks,
which is generated by the collision of a shock with one or two centered compression waves.

5
. If
\mathcal{U}^{c}(x,t)
can be expressed by a disjoint union of two or more open intervals,
then there exist at least two
J_{n}\subset\mathcal{U}^{c}(x,t)
at point
(x,t)
.
By Theorem
5.3
(iii),
there exist at least two shocks collided at point
(x,t)
,
and there is a one-to-one correspondence to the shocks collided at point
(x,t)

and the open intervals in the disjoint union of
\mathcal{U}^{c}(x,t)
.
∎

6.
Invariants, Divides, and Global Structures of Entropy Solutions

In this section, we analyze the global structures of entropy solutions.
In §6.1, we present and prove four invariants of entropy solutions;
in §6.2, we provide and prove the necessary and sufficient conditions respectively for the divides as well as their locations and speeds of entropy solutions in Theorem
6.9
,
and obtain all the divides of entropy solutions in Theorem
6.10
;
in §6.3, we establish the global structures of entropy solutions in Theorem
6.12
.

6.1.
Four invariants of entropy solutions

For the initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
the following limits are all finite numbers:

(6.1)

\begin{cases}\overline{u}_{l}:=\displaystyle\mathop{\overline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow-\infty}\frac{1}{x}\int^{x}_{0}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi,\quad&\underline{u}_{l}:=\displaystyle\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow-\infty}\frac{1}{x}\int^{x}_{0}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi,\\[11.38109pt]
\overline{u}_{r}:=\displaystyle\mathop{\overline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{x}\int^{x}_{0}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi,\quad&\underline{u}_{r}:=\displaystyle\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{x}\int^{x}_{0}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi.\end{cases}

In order to show that

\overline{u}_{l}
,
\underline{u}_{l}
,
\overline{u}_{r}
, and
\underline{u}_{r}
, given by
(
6.1
)
,
are all the invariants of entropy solutions, we need the following lemma.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
c\in\mathcal{U}(x_{1},t_{0})
and
d\in\mathcal{U}(x_{2},t_{0})
with
t_{0}>0
,
and let the line segments
x_{1}(t)
and
x_{2}(t)
be given by

x_{1}(t)=x_{1}+(t-t_{0})f^{\prime}(c),\quad x_{2}(t)=x_{2}+(t-t_{0})f^{\prime}(d)\qquad\,\,{\rm for}\ t\in[0,t_{0}].

Then, for any
t\in[0,t_{0}]
,

(6.2)

tc\big(f^{\prime}(d)-f^{\prime}(c)\big)\leq\int_{x_{1}(t)}^{x_{2}(t)}u(x,t)\,{\rm d}x-\int_{x_{1}(0)}^{x_{2}(0)}\varphi(x)\,{\rm d}x\leq td\big(f^{\prime}(d)-f^{\prime}(c)\big).

In particular, if
c=d
, then

(6.3)

\int_{x_{1}(t)}^{x_{2}(t)}u(x,t)\,{\rm d}x=\int_{x_{1}-t_{0}f^{\prime}(c)}^{x_{2}-t_{0}f^{\prime}(c)}\varphi(x)\,{\rm d}x\qquad\,\,{\rm for\ any}\ t\in[0,t_{0}].

Proof.

Letting
\varphi_{2}=\varphi
and
\varphi_{1}\equiv c
in
(
2.58
)
,
and replacing
u_{2,1}
and
u_{2,2}
by
c\in\mathcal{U}_{2}(x_{1},t_{0})

and
d\in\mathcal{U}_{2}(x_{2},t_{0})
into
(
2.58
)
respectively,
we obtain that, for any
t\in[0,t_{0}]
,

tc\big(f^{\prime}(d)-f^{\prime}(c)\big)\leq\int_{x_{1}(t)}^{x_{2}(t)}u(x,t)\,{\rm d}x-\int_{x_{1}(0)}^{x_{2}(0)}\varphi(x)\,{\rm d}x\leq\int_{x_{2}(t)-tf^{\prime}(d)}^{x_{2}(t)-tf^{\prime}(c)}\varphi(x)\,{\rm d}x.

Similarly, letting
\varphi_{2}=\varphi
and
\varphi_{1}\equiv d
in
(
2.58
)
,
and replacing
u_{2,1}
and
u_{2,2}
by
c\in\mathcal{U}_{2}(x_{1},t_{0})

and
d\in\mathcal{U}_{2}(x_{2},t_{0})
into
(
2.58
)
respectively,
we obtain that, for any
t\in[0,t_{0}]
,

\int_{x_{1}(t)-tf^{\prime}(d)}^{x_{1}(t)-tf^{\prime}(c)}\varphi(x)\,{\rm d}x\leq\int_{x_{1}(t)}^{x_{2}(t)}u(x,t)\,{\rm d}x-\int_{x_{1}(0)}^{x_{2}(0)}\varphi(x)\,{\rm d}x\leq td\big(f^{\prime}(d)-f^{\prime}(c)\big).

This implies
(
6.2
)
.

In particular,
(
6.3
)
follows by taking
c=d
into
(
6.2
)
.
∎

Theorem 6.2

(Four invariants of entropy solutions)
.

Suppose that
\overline{u}_{l}
,
\underline{u}_{l}
,
\overline{u}_{r}
, and
\underline{u}_{r}

are given by
(
6.1
)
.
Let
u=u(x,t)
be the entropy solution of the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)
.
Then, for any
t>0
,

(6.4)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathop{\overline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow-\infty}\frac{1}{x}\int^{x}_{0}u(\xi,t)\,{\rm d}\xi=\overline{u}_{l},\quad&\displaystyle\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow-\infty}\frac{1}{x}\int^{x}_{0}u(\xi,t)\,{\rm d}\xi=\underline{u}_{l},\\[11.38109pt]
\displaystyle\mathop{\overline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{x}\int^{x}_{0}u(\xi,t)\,{\rm d}\xi=\overline{u}_{r},\quad&\displaystyle\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{x}\int^{x}_{0}u(\xi,t)\,{\rm d}\xi=\underline{u}_{r}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Fix
t>0
. Since
\varphi(x)
and
u(x,t)
are uniformly bounded by
\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}
,
then, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
taking points
(0,t)
and
(x,t)
into
(
6.2
)
, we obtain

O_{1}(1)+\int^{x}_{0}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi\leq\int^{x}_{0}u(\xi,t)\,{\rm d}\xi\leq\int^{x}_{0}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi+O_{2}(1),

which, by multiplying
\frac{1}{x}
and letting
x\rightarrow\pm\infty
, implies
(
6.4
)
.
∎

6.2.
Locations and speeds of divides

A divide is a global-in-time characteristic that does not interact
with any other characteristic for all time
t
.
A more detailed definition of divides is given as

Definition 6.3
.

A point
x_{0}
on the
x
–axis is called a divide generation point
if there exists
c\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\mathcal{U}(x,t)=\{c\}
for any point
(x,t)\in L_{c}(x_{0}):=\{x=x_{0}+tf^{\prime}(c),\,t>0\}
.
If
x_{0}
is a divide generation point,
line
L_{c}(x_{0})
is called a divide emitting from
x_{0}
with speed
c
,
and the correspondingly speed
c
is called a divide generation value of
x_{0}
.

The point set
\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
of divide generation values of
x_{0}
is defined by

(6.5)

\mathcal{D}(x_{0}):=\big\{c\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\mathcal{U}(x,t)=\{c\}\quad{\rm for\ any}\ (x,t)\in L_{c}(x_{0})\ {\rm and}\ t>0\big\}.

From Definition
6.3
, the following three are equivalent:

(i)
\,x_{0}
is a divide generation point.
(ii)
\,L_{c}(x_{0})
is a divide for some
c\in\mathbb{R}
.
(iii)
\,\mathcal{D}(x_{0})\neq\varnothing
.

Since a divide
L_{c}(x_{0})
is necessarily a characteristic,
by Definition
3.1
,

(6.6)

\mathcal{D}(x_{0})\subset\mathcal{C}(x_{0})\subset[\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(x_{0}),\underline{{\rm D}}_{+}\Phi(x_{0})].
