We first present the necessary and sufficient conditions for the divides in Proposition
6.4
,
and a basic property of
\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
in Proposition
6.5
.

Proposition 6.4
.

L_{c}(x_{0}):\,x=x_{0}+tf^{\prime}(c)
for
t>0
is a divide if and only if

(6.7)

\Phi(l;x_{0},c)=\int^{x_{0}+l}_{x_{0}}(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi\geq 0\qquad\,{\rm for\ any}\ l\in\mathbb{R}.

Proof.

First, if
L_{c}(x_{0})
is a divide,
then
\mathcal{U}(x,t)=\{c\}
for any
(x,t)\in L_{c}(x_{0})
so that

(6.8)

E(c;x,t)-E(u;x,t)\geq 0\qquad{\rm for\ any\ }(x,t)\in L_{c}(x_{0})\ {\rm and}\ u\in\mathbb{R}.

For any given
l_{0}\neq 0
, for sufficiently large
t
,
there exists
u(t)\in\mathbb{R}
such that

l_{0}=t(f^{\prime}(c)-f^{\prime}(u(t)))
,
which, by the strictly increasing property of
f^{\prime}(u)
, implies that

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}u(t)=c

and

u(t)\neq c
.
From
(
3.5
)
, taking
l_{0}
and
u=u(t)
into
(
6.8
)
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\int^{x_{0}+l_{0}}_{x_{0}}(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi

\displaystyle\geq-t\int^{u(t)}_{c}(s-c)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=-t\,\big((u(t)-c)f^{\prime}(u(t))-(f(u(t))-f(c))\big)

\displaystyle=l_{0}(u(t)-c)\frac{f^{\prime}(u(t))-f^{\prime}(\hat{u}(t))}{f^{\prime}(u(t))-f^{\prime}(c)}

\displaystyle=l_{0}\,(u(t)-c)\,\theta\ \longrightarrow 0\qquad{\rm as}\ t\rightarrow\infty,

where
\hat{u}(t)
lies between
c
and
u(t)
, and
\theta\in(0,1)
.
This, by the arbitrariness of
l_{0}
, implies
(
6.7
)
.

On the other hand, suppose that
(
6.7
)
holds.
For any fixed
(x,t)\in L_{c}(x_{0})
, if
u\neq c
,
then, letting
l=t(f^{\prime}(c)-f^{\prime}(u))
in
(
6.7
)
, we obtain

E(c;x,t)-E(u;x,t)\geq t\int^{u}_{c}(s-c)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s>0,

which means that
c
is the unique point at which
E(\cdot\,;x,t)
attains its maximum.
This implies that
\mathcal{U}(x,t)=\{c\}
.
By the arbitrariness of point
(x,t)\in L_{c}(x_{0})
,
L_{c}(x_{0})
is a divide.
∎

Proposition 6.5
.

If
\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
defined by
(
6.5
)
has more than one element,
then it is a closed interval
:

(i)

For any
c_{-},c_{+}\in\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
with
c_{-}<c_{+}
,
then
[c_{-},c_{+}]\subset\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
;

(ii)

If
(c_{-},c_{+})\subset\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
,
then
[c_{-},c_{+}]\subset\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps accordingly.

1
. Suppose that
c_{-},c_{+}\in\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
with
c_{-}<c_{+}
.
From
(
6.7
)
, for any
c\in(c_{-},c_{+})
,

\int^{x_{0}+l}_{x_{0}}(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi\geq\int^{x_{0}+l}_{x_{0}}(\varphi(\xi)-c_{\pm})\,{\rm d}\xi\geq 0\qquad\,\,{\rm if}\ \pm l\geq 0,

which, by Proposition
6.4
, implies that
c\in\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
.
Thus,
[c_{-},c_{+}]\subset\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
.

2
. If
(c_{-},c_{+})\subset\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
, then, from
(
6.7
)
,
for any
c\in(c_{-},c_{+})
,

\int^{x_{0}+l}_{x_{0}}(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi\geq 0\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ l\in\mathbb{R},

which, by letting
c\rightarrow c_{-}{+}
and
c\rightarrow c_{+}{-}
respectively,
implies that
(
6.7
)
holds for
c=c_{-}
and
c=c_{+}
.
This means that
c_{-},c_{+}\in\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
so that
[c_{-},c_{+}]\subset\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
.
∎

From Proposition
6.5
,

\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
may be the empty set, a single point set, or a closed interval.
In order to determine the point set
\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
exactly,
we introduce the convex hull of
\Phi(x)=\int_{0}^{x}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi
over
({-}\infty,\infty)
,
denoted by
\bar{\Phi}(x)
, as follows:

(6.9)

\bar{\Phi}(x)=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ x\in\mathbb{R},

where
\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)
is the convex-truncated function of
\Phi(x)
over
[-N,N]
given by

(6.10)

\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)=\begin{cases}\mathop{cov}_{[-N,N]}\Phi(x)\qquad&{\rm if}\ |x|\leq N,\\[2.84526pt]
\Phi(x)\qquad&{\rm if}\ |x|\geq N.\end{cases}

Furthermore, let
\mathcal{K}_{0}
be the set of points
at which
\Phi(x)
equals its convex hull
\bar{\Phi}(x)
,
i.e.
,

(6.11)

\mathcal{K}_{0}=\big\{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,\Phi(x_{0})=\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})\big\}.

In order to keep the coherence of writing,
we list the properties of
\bar{\Phi}(x)
in Lemmas
6.6
–
6.8
,
and postpone their proofs in Appendix B for completeness.

Lemma 6.6
.

\bar{\Phi}(x)>-\infty
on
\mathbb{R}
if and only if

(6.12)

\displaystyle\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\pm\infty}(\Phi(x)-cx)>-\infty\qquad{\rm for\ some}\ c\in\mathbb{R}.

Lemma 6.7
.

There are three cases of
\bar{\Phi}(x)
:

(i)

If
\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
,
then
\bar{\Phi}(x)>-\infty
on
\mathbb{R}
and satisfies that

(6.13)

\overline{u}_{l}\leq{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(y)\leq{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(y)\leq\underline{u}_{r}\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ y\in\mathbb{R},

and
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
such that

(6.14)

\inf_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}}{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})=\overline{u}_{l},\qquad\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}}{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})=\underline{u}_{r}.

(ii)

If
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
,
then
\bar{\Phi}(x)>-\infty
on
\mathbb{R}
if and only if

(6.15)

\mathop{\underline{\lim}}_{x\rightarrow\pm\infty}(\Phi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}x)>-\infty.

Furthermore, if
\bar{\Phi}(x)>-\infty
on
\mathbb{R}
, then
there exists
b_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\bar{\Phi}(x)=\underline{u}_{r}x+b_{0}
.
There exist two subcases
:

(a)

\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
if
\Phi(x_{0})=\underline{u}_{r}x_{0}+b_{0}

for some
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
;

(b)

\mathcal{K}_{0}=\varnothing
if
\Phi(x)>\underline{u}_{r}x+b_{0}
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
.

(iii)

If
\overline{u}_{l}>\underline{u}_{r}
, then
\bar{\Phi}(x)\equiv-\infty
.

Lemma 6.8
.

If
\bar{\Phi}(x)>-\infty
on
\mathbb{R}
,

\bar{\Phi}(x)
is convex on
\mathbb{R}
.
Furthermore, if
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
, then

(i)

For any
x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
,

(6.16)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})=\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow x_{0}{+}}\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})}{x-x_{0}}=\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}\Phi(x_{0}),\\[11.38109pt]
\displaystyle{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})=\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow x_{0}{-}}\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})}{x-x_{0}}=\sup_{l<0}\Delta_{l}\Phi(x_{0}),\end{cases}

where
\Delta_{l}\Phi(x_{0})=\frac{1}{l}\big(\Phi(x_{0}+l)-\Phi(x_{0})\big)
for
l\neq 0
.

(ii)

\mathcal{K}_{0}
is a closed set,
and
\mathcal{K}^{c}_{0}:=\mathbb{R}-\mathcal{K}_{0}
is an open set.
The following three properties hold
:

(a)

If
x^{+}_{0}:=\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}<\infty
,
then
x^{+}_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
with
{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x^{+}_{0})=\underline{u}_{r}
such that

(6.17)

\bar{\Phi}(x)=\Phi(x^{+}_{0})+\underline{u}_{r}(x-x^{+}_{0})\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ x\geq x^{+}_{0}.

(b)

If
x^{-}_{0}:=\inf\mathcal{K}_{0}>-\infty
,
then
x^{-}_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
with
{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x^{-}_{0})=\overline{u}_{l}
such that

(6.18)

\bar{\Phi}(x)=\Phi(x^{-}_{0})+\overline{u}_{l}(x-x^{-}_{0})\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ x\leq x^{-}_{0}.

(c)

Let the open set
\mathcal{K}^{c}_{0}:=\cup_{n}(e_{n},h_{n})
.
If
(e_{n},h_{n})
is bounded, then

(6.19)

\bar{\Phi}(x)={\Phi}(e_{n})+{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(e_{n})(x-e_{n})\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ x\in[e_{n},h_{n}],

where
{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(e_{n})={\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(h_{n})=\frac{{\Phi}(h_{n})-{\Phi}(e_{n})}{h_{n}-e_{n}}
.

We now present the main theorems of divides.

Theorem 6.9

(Necessary and sufficient conditions for the locations and speeds of divides)
.

Let
\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
be defined by
(
6.5
)
,
and let
\bar{\Phi}(x)
as in
(
6.9
)
–
(
6.10
)
be the convex hull of
\Phi(x)

over
({-}\infty,\infty)
with
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
.
Then

(i)

If
\bar{\Phi}(x)>-\infty
on
\mathbb{R}
,
then
c\in\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
if and only if

(6.20)

\Phi(x_{0})=\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}),\qquad{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})\leq c\leq{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}).

(ii)

If
\bar{\Phi}(x)\equiv-\infty
,
then
\mathcal{D}(x_{0})=\varnothing
for any
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.

In the above,
{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})
and
{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})

are given by
(
6.16
)
.
See
Figs.

6.1
–
6.2
for the details.

Figure 6.1.
The graph of
\Phi(x)
and
\bar{\Phi}(x)
when
\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
.

Figure 6.2.
The graph of
\Phi(x)
and
\bar{\Phi}(x)
when
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps accordingly.

1
. First, if
c\in\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
, then
(
6.7
)
holds.
By taking
x:=x_{0}+l
into
(
6.7
)
,

\Phi(x)\geq c(x-x_{0})+\Phi(x_{0})\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ x\in\mathbb{R}.

Since
\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)

is the convex hull of
\Phi(x)
over
[-N,N]
and
\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)={\Phi}(x)
for
|x|\geq N
, then

\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)\geq c(x-x_{0})+\Phi(x_{0})
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
so that
\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})\geq\Phi(x_{0})
.
Meanwhile, from
(
6.10
)
,
\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})\leq\Phi(x_{0})
for any
N>0
.
Thus,
\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})=\Phi(x_{0})
.
Letting
N\rightarrow\infty
, we obtain

\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})=\Phi(x_{0}).

Furthermore, it is direct to check from
(
6.7
)
that

\sup_{l<0}\frac{1}{l}\big(\Phi(x_{0}+l)-\Phi(x_{0})\big)\leq c\leq\inf_{l>0}\frac{1}{l}\big(\Phi(x_{0}+l)-\Phi(x_{0})\big),

which, by
(
6.16
)
,
implies that
{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})\leq c\leq{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})
.

On the other hand, if
\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})=\Phi(x_{0})

and
{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})\leq c\leq{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})
,
then it follows from
(
6.16
)
that
(
6.7
)
holds,
i.e.
,

c\in\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
.

2
. Fix
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.
Since
\bar{\Phi}(x)\equiv-\infty
, by Lemma
6.6
,
for any fixed
c\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}(\Phi(x)-cx)=-\infty\qquad{\rm or}\quad\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow-\infty}(\Phi(x)-cx)=-\infty

which is to say that:

(a)

If
\mathop{\underline{\lim}}_{x\rightarrow\infty}(\Phi(x)-cx)=-\infty
,
then
\Phi(x)-cx<\Phi(x_{0})-cx_{0}
for sufficiently large
x>x_{0}

so that
\int^{x}_{x_{0}}(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi<0
,
which, by
(
6.7
)
, implies that
c\notin\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
.

(b)

If
\mathop{\underline{\lim}}_{x\rightarrow-\infty}(\Phi(x)-cx)=-\infty
,
then
\Phi(x)-cx<\Phi(x_{0})-cx_{0}
for sufficiently negative
x<x_{0}

so that
\int^{x}_{x_{0}}(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi<0
,
which, by
(
6.7
)
, implies that
c\notin\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
.

By the arbitrariness of
c\in\mathbb{R}
, we see that
\mathcal{D}(x_{0})=\varnothing
.
Then, by the arbitrariness of
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, we conclude that

\mathcal{D}(x_{0})=\varnothing
for any
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.
∎

Theorem 6.10

(Locations and speeds of divides of entropy solutions)
.

Let
\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
and
\bar{\Phi}(x)
be defined by
(
6.5
)
and
(
6.9
)
, respectively.
For the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)

with initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,

(i)

If
\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
,
the entropy solution
u(x,t)
of the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)
has divides.
For any
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathcal{D}(x_{0})\neq\varnothing
if and only if
\Phi(x_{0})=\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})
,
which implies that, in this case,

(6.21)

\mathcal{D}(x_{0})=[{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}),{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})].

Furthermore, there exist two cases
:

(a)

If
{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})={\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})
,
there exists a unique divide emitting from
x_{0}
given by

(6.22)

L_{{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})}(x_{0}):\,x=x_{0}+tf^{\prime}({\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}))\qquad{\rm for}\ t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}.

(b)

If
{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})<{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})
,
the divides emitting from
x_{0}
form
a rarefaction wave
:

(6.23)

u(x,t)=(f^{\prime})^{-1}(\frac{x-x_{0}}{t})

for
x\in(x_{0}+tf^{\prime}({\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})),x_{0}+tf^{\prime}({\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})))
.

(ii)

If
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
,
the entropy solution
u(x,t)
of the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)
has divides
if and only if
there exists
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\Phi(x_{0})=\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})
,
which implies that, in this case, the unique divide emitting from
x_{0}
is given by

(6.24)

L_{\underline{u}_{r}}(x_{0}):\,x=x_{0}+tf^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})\qquad\,\,{\rm for}\ t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}.

(iii)

If
\overline{u}_{l}>\underline{u}_{r}
,
the entropy solution
u(x,t)
of the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)
has no divides.

In the above,
{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})
and
{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})

are given by
(
6.16
)
.
See also
Figs.

6.3
–
6.4
.

Figure 6.3.
Divides of entropy solutions when
\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
.

Figure 6.4.
Divides of entropy solutions when
\overline{u}_{l}\geq\underline{u}_{r}
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps accordingly.

1
. From Lemma
6.7
(i),
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
implies that
the entropy solution
u(x,t)
has divides.
By Theorem
6.9
(i),
\mathcal{D}(x_{0})\neq\varnothing

if and only if
\Phi(x_{0})=\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})
,
and
(
6.21
)
follows by
(
6.14
)
and
(
6.20
)
.
Furthermore, if
\mathcal{D}(x_{0})\neq\varnothing
,

(
6.22
)
–
(
6.23
)
follow by Definition
6.3
.

2
. From Lemma
6.7
(ii) and Theorem
6.9
,
the entropy solution
u(x,t)
has divides if and only if
there exists
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\Phi(x_{0})=\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})
.
If this is the case,
from Lemma
6.7
(ii),
\bar{\Phi}^{\prime}(x)\equiv\underline{u}_{r}
so that,
by Definition
6.3
,
(
6.24
)
holds.

3
. If
\overline{u}_{l}>\underline{u}_{r}
,
from Lemma
6.7
(iii),
\bar{\Phi}(x)\equiv-\infty
so that,
by Theorem
6.9
(ii),
\mathcal{D}(x_{0})=\varnothing
for any
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
which implies that the entropy solution
u(x,t)
has no divides.
∎

Example 6.1

(Initial data as a compact perturbation of a constant)
.

Let the initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfy that

\varphi(x)-m
has compact support,
i.e.
,

{\rm spt}(\varphi(x)-m)\subset[-R,R]
for some
R>0
.

It is direct to check that
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}=m
,
and

\mathop{\underline{\lim}}_{x\rightarrow\pm\infty}(\Phi(x)-mx)>-\infty.

By Lemma
\ref{lem:c5.2}{\rm(ii)}
,
we have

\bar{\Phi}(x)=mx+b_{0}\qquad{\rm for}\ b_{0}=\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\{\Phi(x)-mx\}.

Since
{\rm spt}(\varphi(x)-m)\subset[-R,R]
,

\Phi(x)-mx
is constant if
|x|>R
so that
there exists
x_{0}
such that
\Phi(x)-mx
attains infimum at
x_{0}
.
From
(
6.11
)
,
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
is given by

(6.25)

\mathcal{K}_{0}=\big\{x_{0}\,:\,\Phi(x_{0})-mx_{0}=\min_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\{\Phi(x)-mx\}\big\}.

From Theorem
\ref{the:c5.2}{\rm(ii)}
,
all the divides of the entropy solution
u(x,t)
are given by

(6.26)

L_{m}(x_{0}):\,x=x_{0}+tf^{\prime}(m)\qquad{\rm for}\ t\in\mathbb{R}^{+},

where
x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}
given by
(
6.25
)
.

Example 6.2

(Periodic initial data)
.

Let the initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be a periodic function
with the minimum positive period
p>0
and the average
m
.

It is direct to check that

\frac{1}{x}\int^{x}_{0}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi\rightarrow m
as
x\rightarrow\pm\infty

so that

\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}=m
, and

\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\pm\infty}\int^{x}_{0}(\varphi(\xi)-m)\,{\rm d}\xi=\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\pm\infty}\int^{x}_{[\frac{x}{p}]\,p}(\varphi(\xi)-m)\,{\rm d}\xi\geq-\big(\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}+|m|\big)p>-\infty.

By Lemma
\ref{lem:c5.2}{\rm(ii)}
,

\bar{\Phi}(x)=mx+b_{0}
with
b_{0}=\min_{x\in[0,p]}\{\Phi(x)-mx\}
.
Then, from
(
6.11
)
,

(6.27)

\mathcal{K}_{0}=\big\{x_{0}+np\,:\,x_{0}\ {\rm minimazes}\ \Phi(x)-mx\,\,\,{\rm on}\ [0,p],\,\,n\in\mathbb{Z}\big\}.

Since
\Phi(x)-mx
is continuous on
[0,p]
, then
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
.
From Theorem
\ref{the:c5.2}{\rm(ii)}
,
all the divides of the entropy solution
u(x,t)
are given by
(
6.26
)

for
x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}
given by
(
6.27
)
.

Example 6.3

(Initial data as the
L^{1}
perturbation of a constant)
.

Let the initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})

satisfy that
\varphi(x)-m\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
.
It is direct to check that
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}=m
and

\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\pm\infty}(\Phi(x)-mx)=\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\pm\infty}\int^{x}_{0}(\varphi(\xi)-m)\,{\rm d}\xi\geq-\|\varphi(x)-m\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}>-\infty.

By Lemma
\ref{lem:c5.2}{\rm(ii)}
,

\bar{\Phi}(x)=mx+b_{0}
with
b_{0}=\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\{\Phi(x)-mx\}
.
Then, from
(
6.11
)
,

(6.28)

\mathcal{K}_{0}=\big\{x_{0}\,:\,\Phi(x_{0})-mx_{0}=\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\{\Phi(x)-mx\}\big\}.

Then there exist two cases:
If

\Phi(x)-mx
attains its infimum only at
-\infty
or
\infty
,
then
\mathcal{K}_{0}=\varnothing
so that,
by Theorem
\ref{the:c5.2}{\rm(ii)}
,
the entropy solution
u(x,t)
has no divides;
and if
\Phi(x)-mx
attains its infimum at some finite points,
then
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
so that the entropy solution
u(x,t)
has divides,
and all the divides are given by
(
6.26
)

for
x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}
given by
(
6.28
)
.

6.3.
Global structures of entropy solutions

In this subsection,
we establish the global structures of entropy solutions of the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)
.
First, we give a partition of the upper half-plane
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
as follows:

If
\bar{\Phi}(x)>-\infty
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
,
by the continuity of
\Phi(x)
and
\bar{\Phi}(x)
,

\mathcal{K}_{0}
is a closed set so that
\mathcal{K}^{c}_{0}=\mathbb{R}-\mathcal{K}_{0}
is open.
Thus,
\mathcal{K}^{c}_{0}
can be expressed as a disjoint union of open intervals,
i.e.
,

(6.29)

\mathcal{K}^{c}_{0}:=\cup_{n}(e_{n},h_{n})

with
e_{n}
(
resp.
,
h_{n}
) lying in
\mathcal{K}_{0}
if
e_{n}
(
resp.
,
h_{n}
) is finite.

From Lemma
6.8
(ii), if
(e_{n},h_{n})
is a bounded interval, then

(6.30)

c_{n}:={\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(e_{n})={\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(h_{n})=\frac{\Phi(h_{n})-\Phi(e_{n})}{h_{n}-e_{n}},

which, by Theorem
6.10
, implies that
L_{c_{n}}(e_{n})
and
L_{c_{n}}(h_{n})
are both divides.
From
(
6.29
)
, there exists no divides in
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
,
where
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
is defined by

(6.31)

\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}:=\big\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\,:\,e_{n}+tf^{\prime}(c_{n})<x<h_{n}+tf^{\prime}(c_{n})\big\}.

Similarly, if
x_{0}^{+}=\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}<\infty
,
then
L_{\underline{u}_{r}}(x_{0}^{+})
is a divide,
and there exists no divides in
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
,
where
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
is defined by

(6.32)

\mathcal{H}_{\infty}:=\big\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\,:\,x>x^{+}_{0}+tf^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})\big\};

and if
x_{0}^{-}=\inf\mathcal{K}_{0}>-\infty
,
then
L_{\overline{u}_{l}}(x_{0}^{-})
is a divide,
and there exists no divides in
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
,
where
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
is defined by

(6.33)

\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}:=\big\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\,:\,x<x^{-}_{0}+tf^{\prime}(\overline{u}_{l})\big\}.

Let
\mathcal{K}
be the union of all divides of the entropy solution
u(x,t)
,
i.e.
,

(6.34)

\mathcal{K}:=\big\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\,:\,(x,t)\in L_{c}(x_{0})\,\,\,\,{\rm for\ }x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}\ {\rm and}\ c\in\mathcal{D}(x_{0})\big\}.

Therefore, if the entropy solution
u(x,t)
has at least one divide, then
there is a disjoint partition of
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
given by

(6.35)

\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}=\mathcal{K}\cup\big(\cup_{n}\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}\big),

in which
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}=\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
if
h_{n}=\infty
,
and
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}=\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
if
e_{n}=-\infty
.

If the entropy solution
u(x,t)
has no divides,

(
6.35
)
can be regarded as

\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}=\mathcal{H}_{({-}\infty,\infty)}

in the sense that
\mathcal{K}=\varnothing
,
and there exists only one
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
with
e_{n}=-\infty
and
h_{n}=\infty
.

Before giving Theorem
6.12

on the global structures of entropy solutions,
we need the following lemma on the relation between shock curves and divides.

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
x=x(t)
for
t\geq t_{0}
be a shock curve of the entropy solution
u(x,t)
,
i.e.
,

u(x(t){+},t)<u(x(t){-},t)
for any
t>t_{0}
.
For any
t>t_{0}
, define

(6.36)

y^{\pm}(t,\tau):=x(t)+(\tau-t)f^{\prime}(u(x(t){\pm},t))\qquad{\rm for}\ \tau\in[0,t).

Then
y^{\pm}(\infty,\tau):=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{\pm}(t,\tau)
exist for any
\tau\geq 0
,
and

(i)

If
y^{+}(\infty,0)<\infty
, line
L_{c}(y^{+}(\infty,0))
, defined by

(6.37)

L_{c}(y^{+}(\infty,0)):\,y^{+}(\infty,\tau)=y^{+}(\infty,0)+\tau f^{\prime}(c)\qquad{\rm for}\ \tau\geq 0,

is a divide emitting from
y^{+}(\infty,0)
with speed
c
satisfying

(6.38)

c:=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}u(x(t){+},t)={\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(y^{+}(\infty,0)).

Let
v^{+}(t)
be uniquely determined by
y^{+}(\infty,0)=x(t)-tf^{\prime}(v^{+}(t))
for large
t
, then

(6.39)

0\leq\int^{y^{\pm}(t,0)}_{y^{+}(\infty,0)}(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi\leq(v^{+}(t)-c)\big(y^{\pm}(t,0)-y^{+}(\infty,0)\big).

(ii)

If
y^{-}(\infty,0)>-\infty
, line
L_{d}(y^{-}(\infty,0))
, defined by

(6.40)

L_{d}(y^{-}(\infty,0)):\,y^{-}(\infty,\tau)=y^{-}(\infty,0)+\tau f^{\prime}(d)\qquad{\rm for}\ \tau\geq 0,

is a divide emitting from
y^{-}(\infty,0)
with speed
d
satisfying

(6.41)

d:=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}u(x(t){-},t)={\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(y^{-}(\infty,0)).

Let
v^{-}(t)
be uniquely determined by
y^{-}(\infty,0)=x(t)-tf^{\prime}(v^{-}(t))
for large
t
, then

(6.42)

0\leq\int^{y^{\pm}(t,0)}_{y^{-}(\infty,0)}(\varphi(\xi)-d)\,{\rm d}\xi\leq(v^{-}(t)-d)\big(y^{\pm}(t,0)-y^{-}(\infty,0)\big).

In the above,

{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})
and
{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})

are given by
(
6.16
)
.

Proof.

From
(
5.7
)
,
y^{-}(\cdot,\tau)

(
resp.
,

y^{+}(\cdot,\tau)
)
is nonincreasing
(
resp.
, nondecreasing
)
for any fixed
\tau\geq 0
.
Then, by Lemma
2.8
, for any
t_{2}>t_{1}>t_{0}
,

y^{-}(t_{2},\tau)\leq y^{-}(t_{1},\tau)<y^{+}(t_{1},\tau)\leq y^{+}(t_{2},\tau),

so that
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{\pm}(t,\tau)
exist for any
\tau\geq 0

with limit
y^{+}(\infty,\tau)

(
resp.
,
y^{-}(\infty,\tau)
)

being a finite number or
\infty

(
resp.
,
-\infty
)
.

1
. If
y^{+}(\infty,0)<\infty
, it follows from
(
6.36
)
that

y^{+}(t,\tau)-y^{+}(t,0)=\tau f^{\prime}(u(x(t){+},t))
.
Since
u(x(t){+},t)
is uniformly bounded,
then
y^{+}(\infty,\tau)<\infty
for any
\tau>0
so that

y^{+}(\infty,\tau)-y^{+}(\infty,0)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\tau f^{\prime}(u(x(t){+},t))=\tau f^{\prime}(\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}u(x(t){+},t)),

which, by the strictly increasing property of
f^{\prime}(u)
, implies

(6.43)

c=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}u(x(t){+},t)=(f^{\prime})^{-1}(\tfrac{y^{+}(\infty,\tau)-y^{+}(\infty,0)}{\tau}).

(a)
. Since
(y^{+}(t,\tau),\tau)
lies on the characteristic:
l_{u(x(t){+},t)}(x(t),t)
,
then from Lemma
2.9
,

\mathcal{U}(y^{+}(t,\tau),\tau)=\big\{u(x(t){+},t)\big\}
for any
\tau\in(0,t)

so that, for any
\tau\in(0,t)
,

(6.44)

E(u(x(t){+},t);y^{+}(t,\tau),\tau)-E(u;y^{+}(t,\tau),\tau)\geq 0\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ u\in\mathbb{R}.

Then, for any fixed
\tau>0
, it follows from
(
6.43
)
–
(
6.44
)
that,
for any
u\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\ E(c;y^{+}(\infty,\tau),\tau)-E(u;y^{+}(\infty,\tau),\tau)

\displaystyle=-\int^{y^{+}(\infty,\tau)-\tau f^{\prime}(c)}_{y^{+}(\infty,\tau)-\tau f^{\prime}(u)}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi-\tau\int^{c}_{u}sf^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\Big({-}\int^{y^{+}(t,\tau)-\tau f^{\prime}(u(x(t){+},t))}_{y^{+}(t,\tau)-\tau f^{\prime}(u)}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi-\tau\int^{u(x(t){+},t)}_{u}sf^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s\Big)

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\big(E(u(x(t){+},t);y^{+}(t,\tau),\tau)-E(u;y^{+}(t,\tau),\tau)\big)\geq 0,

which implies that
c\in\mathcal{U}(y^{+}(\infty,\tau),\tau)
for any
\tau>0
.

From Lemma
2.9
, along line
y^{+}(\infty,\tau)=y^{+}(\infty,0)+\tau f^{\prime}(c)
,

u(y^{+}(\infty,\tau){\pm},\tau)\equiv c
for any
\tau>0
,
so that,
L_{c}(y^{+}(\infty,0))
is a divide.
By Theorem
6.9
,
c={\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(y^{+}(\infty,0))
so that

(
6.38
)
follows from
(
6.43
)
.

(b)
.
Furthermore, from
(
6.36
)
and
y^{\pm}(\infty,\tau)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{\pm}(t,\tau)
,
for sufficiently large
t
,

\displaystyle\frac{x(t)-y^{+}(\infty,0)}{t}=\frac{y^{+}(t,0)-y^{+}(\infty,0)}{t}+f^{\prime}(u(x(t){+},t))\in(f^{\prime}({-}\infty),f^{\prime}(\infty)),

which, by the strictly increasing property of
f^{\prime}(u)
, implies that, for large
t
,
there exists a unique
v^{+}(t)
such that
y^{+}(\infty,0)=x(t)-tf^{\prime}(v^{+}(t))
.
Then, from
u^{\pm}(t):=u(x(t){\pm},t)\in\mathcal{U}(x(t),t)
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq E(u^{\pm}(t);x(t),t)-E(v^{+}(t);x(t),t)

\displaystyle=-\int^{x(t)-tf^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t))}_{x(t)-tf^{\prime}(v^{+}(t))}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi+t\int^{v^{+}(t)}_{u^{\pm}(t)}sf^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s

(6.45)

\displaystyle=-\int^{y^{\pm}(t,0)}_{y^{+}(\infty,0)}(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi+t\int^{v^{+}(t)}_{u^{\pm}(t)}(s-c)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s.

Since
L_{c}(y^{+}(\infty,0))
in
(
6.37
)

is a divide emitting from
y^{+}(\infty,0)
with speed
c
, from
(
6.7
)
,

\int^{y^{\pm}(t,0)}_{y^{+}(\infty,0)}(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi\geq 0
for any
t>0

and, by the convexity of
f(u)
,

f(v^{+}(t))\geq f(u^{\pm}(t))+f^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t))(v^{+}(t)-u^{\pm}(t)).

Then
(
6.39
)
is inferred by
(
6.45
)
as follows:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\int^{y^{\pm}(t,0)}_{y^{+}(\infty,0)}(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi\leq t\int^{v^{+}(t)}_{u^{\pm}(t)}(s-c)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=t\big((v^{+}(t)-c)f^{\prime}(v^{+}(t))-(u^{\pm}(t)-c)f^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t))+f(u^{\pm}(t))-f(v^{+}(t))\big)

\displaystyle\leq t\big(v^{+}(t)(f^{\prime}(v^{+}(t))-f^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t)))-c(f^{\prime}(v^{+}(t))-f^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t)))\big)

\displaystyle=t(v^{+}(t)-c)\big(f^{\prime}(v^{+}(t))-f^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t))\big)

\displaystyle=(v^{+}(t)-c)\big(y^{\pm}(t,0)-y^{+}(\infty,0)\big).

2
. Similar to Step 1 above, it can be checked that,
if
y^{-}(\infty,0)>-\infty
,
line
L_{d}(y^{-}(\infty,0))
in
(
6.40
)
is a divide emitting from

y^{-}(\infty,0)
with
d={\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(y^{-}(\infty,0))
,
and
(
6.41
)
–
(
6.42
)
hold.
∎

Theorem 6.12

(Global dynamic patterns of entropy solutions)
.

Let
u=u(x,t)
be the entropy solution of the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)

with initial data in
L^{\infty}
.
Then the following statements hold
:

(i)

If
\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\,\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
,
then the entropy solution
u(x,t)
possesses divides.
The necessary and sufficient conditions for the locations and speeds of divides are given by
Theorem

6.9

and all the divides of

u(x,t)
are given by
Theorem

6.10
.

Furthermore,
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
can be partitioned into

\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}=\mathcal{K}\cup\big(\cup_{n}\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}\big)
as in
(
6.35
)
.
Then

u(x,t)
possesses no shocks in
\mathcal{K}
;

any two shocks in each
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}

(
resp.,
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
, or
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty})
must coincide each other in a finite time,
in which the shock set in each
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}

(
resp.,
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
, or
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty})
is path-connected.

Moreover, the entropy solution
u(x,t)
turns into a single shock asymptotically
in each
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}

(
resp.,
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
, or
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty})
,
and the fine structures of

u(x,t)
on the shock set
in each
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}

(
resp.,
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
, or
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty})

are given by
Theorem

5.6
.

(ii)

If
\overline{u}_{l}>\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}=\varnothing
,
the entropy solution has no divides and
any two shocks in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
must coincide each other in a finite time,
in which the shock set in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
is path-connected.
Moreover, the entropy solution
u(x,t)
turns into a single shock asymptotically in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
,
and the fine structures of

u(x,t)
on the shock set in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}

are given by
Theorem

5.6
.

In the above,
\overline{u}_{l}
and
\underline{u}_{r}
, and
\mathcal{K}_{0}

are given by
(
6.1
)
and
(
6.11
)
;

and
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\pm\infty}

are given by
(
6.31
)
–
(
6.33
)
.
See
Figs.

2.4
–
2.6
for the details.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps accordingly.

1
. Suppose that
\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
.
From Theorem
6.10
, the entropy solution has at least one divide.
If there exists
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
,
then there exists no divide in
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
so that,
the forward generalized characteristic of each point
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}

must become a shock in a finite time.
This means that
the shock set in
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
,
denoted by
\Gamma(\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})})
, is not empty.

From Lemma
5.2
,
for any two points
(x_{i},t_{i})\in\Gamma(\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})})
for
i=1,2
,
the forward generalized characteristics
X(x_{i},t_{i})
for
i=1,2
are shock curves so that

X(x_{i},t_{i})\subset\Gamma(\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})})
.
Then, by Lemma
6.11
,

X(x_{1},t_{1})
and
X(x_{2},t_{2})
both possess the properties that

y^{-}(\infty,0)=e_{n}
and
y^{+}(\infty,0)=h_{n}
.
This means that the backward characteristic triangles,
belonging to the points on the forward generalized characteristics
X(x_{1},t_{1})
and
X(x_{2},t_{2})
respectively,
must intersect each other at some time
t_{3}>\max\{t_{1},t_{2}\}
.
Thus, by Proposition
5.1
, these two backward characteristic triangles
must coincide with each other.
By the uniqueness of the forward generalized characteristic in Lemma
5.2
,

X(x_{1},t_{1})
and
X(x_{2},t_{2})
coincide with each other for
t\geq t_{3}
.
This means that
any two shocks in
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
must coincide with each other in a finite time,
and points
(x_{1},t_{1})
and
(x_{2},t_{2})
can be connected by the curve

\big\{X(x_{1},t_{1})\,:\,t_{1}\leq t\leq t_{3}\big\}\cup\big\{X(x_{2},t_{2})\,:\,t_{2}\leq t\leq t_{3}\big\}\subset\Gamma(\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}),

which, by the arbitrariness of
(x_{i},t_{i})
for
i=1,2
, implies that

\Gamma(\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})})
is path-connected.
Therefore,
entropy solution
u(x,t)
turns into a single shock asymptotically
in
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
.
For the fine structures of entropy solution
u(x,t)

on the shock set in
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
,
see Theorem
5.6
.

Similar to the case of
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
,
the case of
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
follows by the facts that
there exists no divide in
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}

and, from Lemma
6.11
,

y^{-}(\infty,0)=x_{0}^{+}
and
y^{+}(\infty,0)=\infty

hold for any forward generalized characteristic
X(x_{1},t_{1})
with
(x_{1},t_{1})\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
.
The case of
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
follows by the facts that
there exists no divide in
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
,
and from Lemma
6.11
,

y^{-}(\infty,0)=-\infty
and
y^{+}(\infty,0)=x_{0}^{-}

hold for any forward generalized characteristic
X(x_{1},t_{1})
with
(x_{1},t_{1})\in\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
.

2
. Suppose that
\overline{u}_{l}>\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}=\varnothing
.
By Theorem
6.10
, the entropy solution has no divide.
From Lemma
6.11
,

y^{-}(\infty,0)=-\infty
and
y^{+}(\infty,0)=\infty

hold for any forward generalized characteristic
X(x_{1},t_{1})
with
(x_{1},t_{1})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
Then, for any two points
(x_{i},t_{i})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
for
i=1,2
,
the backward characteristic triangles,
belonging to the points on the forward generalized characteristics
X(x_{i},t_{i})
for
i=1,2
,
intersect each other at some time
t_{3}>\max\{t_{1},t_{2}\}
,
which, by Proposition
5.1
, implies that
these two backward characteristic triangles coincide with each other.

Let
\Gamma(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+})
be the shock set in the whole
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
By the uniqueness of the forward generalized characteristic in Lemma
5.2
,

X(x_{1},t_{1})
and
X(x_{2},t_{2})
coincide with each other for
t\geq t_{3}
,
which means that
any two shocks in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
must coincide with each other in a finite time,
and points
(x_{1},t_{1})
and
(x_{2},t_{2})
can be connected by the curve

\big\{X(x_{1},t_{1})\,:\,t_{1}\leq t\leq t_{3}\big\}\cup\big\{X(x_{2},t_{2})\,:\,t_{2}\leq t\leq t_{3}\big\}\subset\Gamma(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}),

which, by the arbitrariness of
(x_{i},t_{i})
for
i=1,2
, implies that

\Gamma(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+})
is path-connected.
Therefore,
entropy solution
u(x,t)
turns into a single shock asymptotically
in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
For the fine structures of entropy solution
u(x,t)

on the shock set in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
,
see Theorem
5.6
.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

From §
3
–§
6
,

we can roughly solve the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)

with initial data
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})

nearly at a level of solving the Riemann problem

in the following sense:
Combining Fig.
2.2
and Figs.
2.4
–
2.6
with
Figs.
3.1
–
3.3
,
we can obtain a pretty clear image of the entropy solution
only by using the information of the graph of
\Phi(x)=\int_{0}^{x}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi
,
including the initial waves for the Cauchy problem,
the path-connected branches of the shock sets,
the divides, and the rough profile,
etc.
.
This shows the power of the new solution formula
(
2.8
)
with
(
2.4
)
in some sense.

7.
Asymptotic Behaviors of Entropy Solutions with Initial Data in
L^{\infty}

In this section,
we focus on the asymptotic behaviors of entropy solutions with initial data in
L^{\infty}

respectively in the
L^{\infty}
–norm and the
L^{p}_{{\rm loc}}
–norm.
In §7.1, by using the results on invariants, divides,
and global structures of entropy solutions in §6,
the asymptotic behaviors of entropy solutions in the
L^{\infty}
–norm are established
by proving that the asymptotic profile is either a rarefaction-constant solution
(as a function only consisting of some constant regions,
centered rarefaction waves, and/or rarefaction regions)
or a single shock in Theorem
7.3
,
and obtaining the corresponding decay rates
in Theorems
7.4
–
7.5
with Corollary
7.6
;
in §7.2, by introducing the generalized
N
–waves,
the asymptotic behaviors of entropy solutions in the
L^{p}_{{\rm loc}}
–norm are established
by proving that the entropy solutions decay to the generalized
N
–waves
and obtaining the corresponding decay rates in Theorem
7.9
with Lemma
7.8
.

By Lemma
6.8
, if
\bar{\Phi}(x)>-\infty
, then
\bar{\Phi}(x)
is a convex function on
\mathbb{R}
.
Let
\tilde{u}=\tilde{u}(x,t)
is the entropy solution of the following Cauchy problem

(7.1)

\begin{cases}\tilde{u}_{t}+f(\tilde{u})_{x}=0\qquad{\rm for\ }(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+},\\[2.84526pt]
\tilde{u}(x,0)=\bar{\Phi}^{\prime}(x).\end{cases}

Denote the convex hull of
\bar{\Phi}(x)
over
({-}\infty,\infty)
by
\bar{\bar{\Phi}}(x)
.
Since
\bar{\Phi}(x)
is convex, then

\bar{\Phi}(x)\equiv\bar{\bar{\Phi}}(x)
,
which, by Theorem
3.8
and
(
6.21
)
, implies that, for any
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathcal{D}(x_{0})=[{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\bar{\Phi}}(x_{0}),{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\bar{\Phi}}(x_{0})]=[{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}),{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})]=\mathcal{C}(x_{0}).

Then every characteristic of solution
\tilde{u}(x,t)
of the Cauchy problem
(
7.1
)

is a divide
so that
\tilde{u}(x,t)
is a rarefaction-constant solution.
Since
\bar{\Phi}^{\prime}(x)
is nondecreasing,
then
\bar{\Phi}^{\prime}(x)
has at most countably discontinuous points,
at which a rarefaction wave is generated, and there exists a unique
divide emitting from the point at which
\bar{\Phi}^{\prime}(x)
is continuous.
Therefore, solution
\tilde{u}(x,t)
possesses at most countable rarefaction waves.

Furthermore, if
\mathcal{K}_{0}=\varnothing
,
it follows from Lemma
6.7
(ii) that
\tilde{u}(x,t)\equiv\underline{u}_{r}
;
and if
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
,
\tilde{u}(x,t)
can be determined as follows:
For
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}=\mathcal{K}\cup\big(\cup_{n}\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}\big)

as in
(
6.35
)
,
it follows from
(
6.22
)
–
(
6.23
)
that, for
(x,t)\in\mathcal{K}
,

(7.2)

\tilde{u}(x,t)=\big(f^{\prime}\big)^{-1}(\frac{x-x_{0}}{t})\qquad{\rm with}\ x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0};

and it follows from Lemma
6.8
(ii) that,

(7.3)

\displaystyle\tilde{u}(x,t)=\begin{cases}c_{n}\quad&{\rm if}\ (x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})},\\[2.84526pt]
\underline{u}_{r}\quad&{\rm if}\ (x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty},\\[2.84526pt]
\overline{u}_{l}\quad&{\rm if}\ (x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{{-}\infty}.\end{cases}

In the above,
\overline{u}_{l}
and
\underline{u}_{r}
are given by
(
6.1
)
;

\mathcal{K}_{0}
and
\mathcal{K}
are given by
(
6.11
)
and
(
6.34
)
, respectively;
and
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
with
c_{n}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\pm\infty}
are given by
(
6.30
)
–
(
6.33
)
.

7.1.
Decay of entropy solutions in the
L^{\infty}
–norm

We first have the following lemma on the asymptotic behaviors of the entropy solutions
which possess at least one divide.

Lemma 7.1
.

Suppose that the initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying that

\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
.
Let
u=u(x,t)
and
\tilde{u}=\tilde{u}(x,t)
be the entropy solutions
of the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)

and the Cauchy problem
(
7.1
)
, respectively.
Then

(7.4)

u(x,t)=\tilde{u}(x,t)\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ (x,t)\in\mathcal{K},

and the entropy solution
u=u(x,t)
has at most countable rarefaction waves on
\mathcal{K}
.

Furthermore, the rarefaction-constant solution
\tilde{u}(x,t)
is the asymptotic profile
of the entropy solution
u=u(x,t)
.
More specifically, the following statements hold

(
see also
Fig.

7.1
)
:

(i)

If there exists
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}=:\mathcal{H}_{(e,h)}
in
(
6.31
)

with
c_{n}=:c
given by
(
6.30
)
,
define
e(t):=e+tf^{\prime}(c)
and
h(t):=h+tf^{\prime}(c)
.
Then
\tilde{u}(x,t)\equiv c
on
\mathcal{H}_{(e,h)}
,

(7.5)

\int^{h(t)}_{e(t)}u(x,t)\,{\rm d}x=\int^{h}_{e}\varphi(x)\,{\rm d}x=c(h-e)\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ t>0;

and, for any forward generalized characteristic
X(x_{0},t_{0}):\,x=x(t)
for
t\geq t_{0}

with
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathcal{H}_{(e,h)}
, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.6)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad f^{\prime}(u(x,t))-f^{\prime}(c)=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{x-e(t)}{t}+o(1)\,t^{-1}\quad&{\rm uniformly\ for}\ e(t)<x<x(t),\\[11.38109pt]
\displaystyle\frac{x-h(t)}{t}+o(1)\,t^{-1}\quad&{\rm uniformly\ for}\ x(t)<x<h(t),\end{cases}

(7.7)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}u(x(t){\pm},t)=c,\qquad\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\dfrac{x(t)}{t}=f^{\prime}(c).

Moreover, if
f^{\prime\prime}(u)=(N+o(1))|u-c|^{\alpha}
as
u\rightarrow c
for
\alpha\geq 0
and
N>0
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.8)

|u(x,t)-c|\leq(C+o(1))\Big(\frac{h-e}{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}

holds uniformly in
x
with
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{(e,h)}
, and

(7.9)

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\theta(t)=\frac{1}{2},\qquad\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\big(x(t)-tf^{\prime}(c)\big)=\frac{e+h}{2},

where
C=\big(\frac{1+\alpha}{N}\big)^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}
and

\theta(t)
is given by

\theta(t):=\frac{f^{\prime}(u(x(t){-},t))-f^{\prime}(c)}{f^{\prime}(u(x(t){-},t))-f^{\prime}(u(x(t){+},t))}.

(ii)

If
x_{0}^{+}=\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}<\infty
,

\tilde{u}(x,t)\equiv\underline{u}_{r}
on
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
as in
(
6.32
)
.
Define
x_{0}^{+}(t):=x_{0}^{+}+tf^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})
.
Then, for any forward generalized characteristic
X(x_{0},t_{0}):\,x=x(t)
for
t\geq t_{0}

with
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.10)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad f^{\prime}(u(x,t))-f^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})=\frac{x-x_{0}^{+}(t)}{t}+o(1)\,t^{-1}\quad\,\,{\rm uniformly\ for}\ x_{0}^{+}(t)<x<x(t),

(7.11)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}u(x(t){\pm},t)=\underline{u}_{r},\qquad\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\dfrac{x(t)}{t}=f^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r}).

Furthermore, define
l^{+}(t):=y^{+}(t,0)-x_{0}^{+}
with
y^{+}(t,0)
given by
(
6.36
)
. Then

(7.12)

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{l^{+}(t)}\int^{x_{0}^{+}+l^{+}(t)}_{x_{0}^{+}}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi=\underline{u}_{r}.

(iii)

If
x_{0}^{-}=\inf\mathcal{K}_{0}>-\infty
,

\tilde{u}(x,t)\equiv\overline{u}_{l}
on
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
as in
(
6.33
)
.
Define
x_{0}^{-}(t):=x_{0}^{-}+tf^{\prime}(\overline{u}_{l})
.
Then, for any forward generalized characteristic
X(x_{0},t_{0}):\,x=x(t)
for
t\geq t_{0}

with
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.13)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad f^{\prime}(u(x,t))-f^{\prime}(\overline{u}_{l})=\frac{x-x_{0}^{-}(t)}{t}+o(1)\,t^{-1}\quad\,\,{\rm uniformly\ for}\ x(t)<x<x_{0}^{-}(t),

(7.14)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}u(x(t){\pm},t)=\overline{u}_{l},\qquad\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\dfrac{x(t)}{t}=f^{\prime}(\overline{u}_{l}).

Furthermore, define
l^{-}(t):=y^{-}(t,0)-x_{0}^{-}
with
y^{-}(t,0)
given by
(
6.36
)
. Then

(7.15)

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{l^{-}(t)}\int^{x_{0}^{-}+l^{-}(t)}_{x_{0}^{-}}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi=\overline{u}_{l}.

In the above,

\overline{u}_{l}
and
\underline{u}_{r}
,
\mathcal{K}_{0}
, and
\mathcal{K}

are given by
(
6.1
)
,
(
6.11
)
, and
(
6.34
)
, respectively.

Figure 7.1.
Asymptotic behaviors of entropy solutions possessing divides.

Proof.

From Lemma
6.7
,
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
is equivalent to
that
\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
.
Since
\bar{\Phi}(x)
is convex on
({-}\infty,\infty)
,
then
\bar{\Phi}(x)\equiv\bar{\bar{\Phi}}(x)
so that,
for any
x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
,

\Phi(x_{0})=\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})=\bar{\bar{\Phi}}(x_{0}),\qquad[{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}),{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})]=[{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\bar{\Phi}}(x_{0}),{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\bar{\Phi}}(x_{0})],

which means that each divide of the entropy solution
u(x,t)
is a divide
of the rarefaction-constant solution
\tilde{u}(x,t)
.
From the definition of
\mathcal{K}
in
(
6.34
)
,
(
7.4
)
holds so that
the entropy solution
u=u(x,t)
has at most countable rarefaction waves on
\mathcal{K}
.

1
. Since
e,h\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
and
c={\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(e)
={\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(h)
,
it follows from
(
6.19
)
and
(
7.1
)
that
\tilde{u}(x,t)\equiv c
on
\mathcal{H}_{(e,h)}
.
From Theorem
6.9
,

e(t)=e+tf^{\prime}(c)
and
h(t)=h+tf^{\prime}(c)
for
t>0

are both divides, which, by Proposition
6.4
, implies
that

\int^{h}_{e}(\varphi(x)-c)\,{\rm d}x\geq 0
and
\int^{e}_{h}(\varphi(x)-c)\,{\rm d}x\geq 0

so that
\int^{h}_{e}(\varphi(x)-c)\,{\rm d}x=0
.
Thus,
(
7.5
)
follows from
(
6.3
)
immediately.

Since there exists no divide in
\mathcal{H}_{(e,h)}
,
any forward generalized characteristic
X(x_{0},t_{0}):\,x=x(t)
for
t\geq t_{0}
with
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathcal{H}_{(e,h)}

must become a shock curve when
t>t_{1}
for some
t_{1}\geq t_{0}
large enough.
Then, by Lemma
6.11
,

(7.16)

y^{-}(\infty,0)=e,\qquad y^{+}(\infty,0)=h.

From Lemma
2.8
, for any
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{(e,h)}
with
e(t)<x<x(t)
,

x-tf^{\prime}(u(x,t))
lies between
e=e(t)-tf^{\prime}(c)
and
y^{-}(t,0)

so that, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

0\leq x-e(t)-t\big(f^{\prime}(u(x,t))-f^{\prime}(c)\big)\leq y^{-}(t,0)-e\ \longrightarrow 0,

which implies that
(
7.6
)
holds for the case that

(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{(e,h)}
with
e(t)<x<x(t)
.
Similarly,
(
7.6
)
holds for the case that

(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{(e,h)}
with
x(t)<x<h(t)
.

Since
f^{\prime}(u)
is strictly increasing,
it follows from
(
7.6
)
that
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}u(x(t){\pm},t)=c
.
From
x(t)\in(e(t),h(t))
for any
t\geq t_{0}
,

f^{\prime}(c)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{e(t)}{t}\leq\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{x(t)}{t}\leq\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{h(t)}{t}=f^{\prime}(c),

which yields
(
7.7
)
.

If, in addition,
f^{\prime\prime}(u)=(N+o(1))|u-c|^{\alpha}
as
u\rightarrow c
for some
\alpha\geq 0
and
N>0
,
from
(
7.6
)
and
(
A.5
)
,
we first have

(7.17)

\displaystyle|u(x,t)-c|

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1+\alpha}{N+o(1)}\Big)^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}\,\big|f^{\prime}(u(x,t))-f^{\prime}(c)\big|^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}\leq(C+o(1))\,(h-e)^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}.

We now give the proof of
(
7.9
)
, which is divided into three steps.

(a).
Denote
u^{\pm}(t):=u(x(t){\pm},t)
.
From
(
6.36
)
,

y^{+}(t,0)-y^{-}(t,0)=t\big(f^{\prime}(u^{-}(t))-f^{\prime}(u^{+}(t))\big)

so that, by the definition of
\theta(t)
,

\theta(t)\big(y^{+}(t,0)-y^{-}(t,0)\big)=t\big(f^{\prime}(u^{-}(t))-f^{\prime}(c)\big).

Then, by Lemma
6.11
, from
(
7.16
)
,

y^{-}(t,0)=e+o(1)
and
y^{+}(t,0)=h+o(1)
as
t\rightarrow\infty
, and hence

\displaystyle x(t)-tf^{\prime}(c)

\displaystyle=y^{-}(t,0)+t\big(f^{\prime}(u^{-}(t))-f^{\prime}(c)\big)

\displaystyle=y^{-}(t,0)+\theta(t)\big(y^{+}(t,0)-y^{-}(t,0)\big)

(7.18)

\displaystyle=\theta(t)\,y^{+}(t,0)+(1-\theta(t))\,y^{-}(t,0)=e+\theta(t)(h-e)+o(1),

which, by
x(t)-tf^{\prime}(c)\in(e,h)
, implies

(7.19)

0\leq\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{t\rightarrow\infty}\theta(t)\leq\mathop{\overline{\lim}}\limits_{t\rightarrow\infty}\theta(t)\leq 1.

(b).
From
(
7.18
)
–
(
7.19
)
,
to prove
(
7.9
)
, it suffices to show that

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\theta(t)=\frac{1}{2}.

In fact, since
u^{\pm}(t)\in\mathcal{U}(x(t),t)
, then

E(u^{-}(t);x(t),t)-E(u^{+}(t);x(t),t)=0
.
Thus, by the definition of
E(u;x,t)
in
(
2.4
)
,

-\int^{x(t)-tf^{\prime}(u^{-}(t))}_{x(t)-tf^{\prime}(u^{+}(t))}(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi-t\int^{u^{-}(t)}_{u^{+}(t)}(s-c)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s=0,

which, by
(
6.36
)
, implies

(7.20)

\int^{y^{+}(t,0)}_{y^{-}(t,0)}(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi=t\int^{u^{-}(t)}_{u^{+}(t)}(s-c)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s.

We claim: As
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.21)

\displaystyle t\int^{u^{-}(t)}_{u^{+}(t)}(s-c)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s=O(1)\,\Big(|\theta(t)|^{\frac{2+\alpha}{1+\alpha}}-|1-\theta(t)|^{\frac{2+\alpha}{1+\alpha}}\Big)\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha}},

(7.22)

\displaystyle\int^{y^{+}(t,0)}_{y^{-}(t,0)}(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi=o(1)\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}.

Then, if
(
7.21
)
–
(
7.22
)
hold,
it follows from
(
7.19
)
–
(
7.20
)
that

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\theta(t)=\frac{1}{2}
.

(c).
We now prove
(
7.21
)
–
(
7.22
)
.
Since
f^{\prime\prime}(u)=(N+o(1))|u-c|^{\alpha}
as
u\rightarrow c
for some
\alpha\geq 0
and
N>0
,
from
(
6.36
)
and
(
7.16
)
,

f^{\prime}(u^{-}(t))-f^{\prime}(u^{+}(t))=\frac{y^{+}(t,0)-y^{-}(t,0)}{t}=\frac{h-e+o(1)}{t},

which, by
(
A.5
)
–
(
A.6
)
, implies

\displaystyle t\int^{u^{-}(t)}_{u^{+}(t)}(s-c)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=\frac{N+o(1)}{2+\alpha}\Big(|u^{-}(t)-c|^{2+\alpha}-|u^{+}(t)-c|^{2+\alpha}\Big)\,t

\displaystyle=O(1)\,\Big(|f^{\prime}(u^{-}(t))-f^{\prime}(c)|^{\frac{2+\alpha}{1+\alpha}}-|f^{\prime}(u^{+}(t))-f^{\prime}(c)|^{\frac{2+\alpha}{1+\alpha}}\Big)\,t

\displaystyle=O(1)\,\Big(\frac{h-e+o(1)}{t}\Big)^{\frac{2+\alpha}{1+\alpha}}\,\Big(|\theta(t)|^{\frac{2+\alpha}{1+\alpha}}-|1-\theta(t)|^{\frac{2+\alpha}{1+\alpha}}\Big)\,t

\displaystyle=O(1)\,\Big(|\theta(t)|^{\frac{2+\alpha}{1+\alpha}}-|1-\theta(t)|^{\frac{2+\alpha}{1+\alpha}}\Big)\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}.

This means that
(
7.21
)
holds.

From
(
6.39
)
and
(
6.42
)
,
for sufficiently large
t
, there exists a unique
v^{\pm}(t)\in\mathbb{R}

such that

e=x(t)-tf^{\prime}(v^{-}(t))
and
h=x(t)-tf^{\prime}(v^{+}(t)).

Then, from
(
6.36
)
,

f^{\prime}(v^{-}(t))-f^{\prime}(u^{-}(t))=\frac{y^{-}(t,0)-e}{t},\qquad f^{\prime}(v^{+}(t))-f^{\prime}(u^{+}(t))=\frac{y^{+}(t,0)-h}{t},

which, by
(
7.16
)
, implies that

f^{\prime}(v^{\pm}(t))-f^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t))=o(1)\,t^{-1}.

Since
L_{c}(e)
is a divide,
then, by
(
7.17
)
and
(
A.5
)
,
it follows from
(
6.42
)
that

\displaystyle 0\leq\int^{y^{-}(t,0)}_{e}(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi

\displaystyle\leq o(1)\,\big(|v^{-}(t)-u^{-}(t)|+|u^{-}(t)-c|\big)

\displaystyle=o(1)\,\big(O(1)|f^{\prime}(v^{-}(t))-f^{\prime}(u^{-}(t))|^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}+|u^{-}(t)-c|\big)

\displaystyle=o(1)\,\big(O(1)o(1)\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}+O(1)\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}\big)=o(1)\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}.

Similarly, we have

0\leq\int^{y^{+}(t,0)}_{h}(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi=o(1)\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}.

Therefore, it follows from
(
7.5
)
that

\int^{y^{+}(t,0)}_{y^{-}(t,0)}(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi=\bigg(\int^{y^{+}(t,0)}_{h}+\int^{h}_{e}+\int^{e}_{y^{-}(t,0)}\bigg)(\varphi(\xi)-c)\,{\rm d}\xi=o(1)\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha}},

which yields
(
7.22
)
, and hence
(
7.9
)
holds.

Finally, similar to
(
7.17
)
, it follows from

(
7.6
)
and
(
7.9
)
that

\displaystyle|u(x,t)-c|

\displaystyle\leq(C+o(1))\,\max\Big\{\Big|\frac{x(t)-e(t)}{t}\Big|^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}},\,\Big|\frac{x(t)-h(t)}{t}\Big|^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}\Big\}

\displaystyle=(C+o(1))\,\Big(\frac{h-e}{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}.

2
. By the definition of
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
in
(
6.32
)
,

L_{\underline{u}_{r}}(x_{0}^{+}):\,x=x_{0}^{+}+tf^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})
for
t>0
is a divide.
From
(
6.17
)
and
(
7.1
)
,

\tilde{u}(x,t)\equiv\underline{u}_{r}
on
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
.
Since there exists no divide in
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
,
any forward generalized characteristic
X(x_{0},t_{0}):x=x(t)
for
t\geq t_{0}
with
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}

must become a shock curve when
t>t_{1}
for some
t_{1}
large enough,
i.e.
,

u(x(t){-},t)>u(x(t){+},t)
for any
t>t_{1}
.

Denote
u^{\pm}(t):=u(x(t){\pm},t)
.
By Lemma
6.11
,

y^{-}(\infty,0)=x_{0}^{+}
and
y^{+}(\infty,0)=\infty,

so that

(7.23)

\mathop{\overline{\lim}}\limits_{t\rightarrow\infty}u^{+}(t)\leq\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}u^{-}(t)=\underline{u}_{r}.

Similar to the proof of
(
7.6
)
,
it is direct to check

(
7.10
)
.
To prove
(
7.11
)
, it suffices to show:

\mathop{\underline{\lim}}_{t\rightarrow\infty}u^{+}(t)\geq\underline{u}_{r}.

In fact, since
u^{\pm}(t)\in\mathcal{U}(x(t),t)
,
by the definition of
E(u;x,t)
in
(
2.4
)
,

-\int^{x(t)-tf^{\prime}(u^{-}(t))}_{x(t)-tf^{\prime}(u^{+}(t))}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi-t\int^{u^{-}(t)}_{u^{+}(t)}sf^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s=0,

which, by
(
6.36
)
, implies

\int^{y^{+}(t,0)}_{y^{-}(t,0)}(\varphi(\xi)-\underline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi=t\int^{u^{-}(t)}_{u^{+}(t)}(s-\underline{u}_{r})f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s.

Then, by the definition of
\rho(u,v)
in
(
A.1
)
,

(7.24)

\frac{1}{y^{+}(t,0)-y^{-}(t,0)}\int^{y^{+}(t,0)}_{y^{-}(t,0)}(\varphi(\xi)-\underline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi=\rho(u^{-}(t),u^{+}(t))-\underline{u}_{r},

which, by the definition of
\underline{u}_{r}
in
(
6.1
)
, implies that

\mathop{\underline{\lim}}_{t\rightarrow\infty}\rho(u^{-}(t),u^{+}(t))=\underline{u}_{r}
.
Since solution
u\in L^{\infty}
, there exists a sequence
\{t_{n}\}

such that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}u^{+}(t_{n})=\mathop{\underline{\lim}}_{t\rightarrow\infty}u^{+}(t)
.
Thus, by
(
7.23
)
and
\rho(u,v)\in C(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R})
, we obtain

\rho(\underline{u}_{r},\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{t\rightarrow\infty}u^{+}(t))=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\rho(u^{-}(t_{n}),u^{+}(t_{n}))\geq\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{t\rightarrow\infty}\rho(u^{-}(t),u^{+}(t))=\underline{u}_{r}.

Since
\rho(\underline{u}_{r},v)
is strictly increasing in
v\in\mathbb{R}
with
\rho(\underline{u}_{r},\underline{u}_{r})=\underline{u}_{r}
,
then

\mathop{\underline{\lim}}_{t\rightarrow\infty}u^{+}(t)\geq\underline{u}_{r}
,
which, by
(
7.23
)
, implies that

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}u^{\pm}(t)=\underline{u}_{r}
.

From
y^{-}(\infty,0)=x_{0}^{+}

and
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}u^{\pm}(t)=\underline{u}_{r}
,
it is direct to check from
x(t)=y^{-}(t,0)+tf^{\prime}(u^{-}(t))
that
(
7.11
)
holds.
Since
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}u^{\pm}(t)=\underline{u}_{r}

and
\rho(u,v)\in C(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R})
,

(
7.12
)
follows by letting
t\rightarrow\infty
in
(
7.24
)
.

3
. By the same arguments as in Step 2,
it can be checked that
(
7.13
)
–
(
7.14
)
and
(
7.15
)
hold.
∎

Remark 7.1
.

From Lemma
7.1
, we have

(i)

For
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
, by
(
7.9
)
,

u(x(t){-},t)>c>u(x(t){+},t)
with
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}u(x(t){\pm},t)=c
.
It is interesting to see that
u(x(t){-},t)
and
u(x(t){+},t)
decay to
c
with the same rate
in the sense that
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\theta(t)=\frac{1}{2}
,
and any forward generalized characteristic
x(t)
in
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
takes
the straight line
x=\frac{e_{n}+h_{n}}{2}+tf^{\prime}(c)
as its asymptotic line,
where
c={\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(e_{n})={\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(h_{n})
.

(ii)

For
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
, from
(
7.11
)
,

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}u(x(t){\pm},t)=\underline{u}_{r}
.
It is interesting to see that the limit of
\frac{\Phi(x)}{x}
as
x\rightarrow\infty

may not exist generically,
but from
(
7.12
)
, for any forward generalized characteristic
x(t)
in
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
,
the limit of
\frac{\Phi(y^{+}(t,0))}{y^{+}(t,0)}
always exists as
t\rightarrow\infty
.
It is similar for
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
.

In general, for the initial data function in
L^{\infty}
,
the entropy solution may not possess some uniform decay in
x
as
t\rightarrow\infty
.
In order to obtain the uniform decay in
x
of the entropy solution to its asymptotic profile,
we need the asymptotic behavior of the initial data functions
as
x\rightarrow\pm\infty
.
From
(
6.1
)
, it can be checked that,
as
x\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.25)

\underline{u}_{r}+o_{r}(1;y)\leq\frac{1}{x}\int^{y+x}_{y}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi\leq\overline{u}_{r}+o_{r}(1;y),

where
o_{r}(1;y)
represents an infinitesimal quantity that depends on
y
;
and, as
x\rightarrow{-}\infty
,

(7.26)

\underline{u}_{l}+o_{l}(1;y)\leq\frac{1}{x}\int^{y+x}_{y}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi\leq\overline{u}_{l}+o_{l}(1;y).

where
o_{l}(1;y)
represents an infinitesimal quantity that depends on
y
.

Since
u(x,t)\in\mathcal{U}(x,t)
,
then

E(u(x,t);x,t)-E(u;x,t)\geq 0\qquad\,{\rm for\ any}\ u\in\mathbb{R}.

It is direct to check from
(
2.4
)
that

-\int^{x-tf^{\prime}(u(x,t))}_{x-tf^{\prime}(u)}(\varphi(\xi)-u)\,{\rm d}\xi-t\int^{u(x,t)}_{u}(s-u)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s\geq 0,

which is equivalent to that,
for any
u\in\mathbb{R}
,

(7.27)

0\leq\int^{u(x,t)}_{u}(s-u)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s\leq\frac{1}{t}\int_{x-tf^{\prime}(u(x,t))}^{x-tf^{\prime}(u)}(\varphi(\xi)-u)\,{\rm d}\xi.

We now present the following lemma on the asymptotic behaviors of the entropy solutions which possess no divides
and the asymptotic behaviors of entropy solutions in
\mathcal{H}_{\pm\infty}
(if it exists) when the entropy solutions possess at least one divide.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
u=u(x,t)
be the entropy solution of the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)

with initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
and let
x(t)
for
t\in[0,\infty)
be a forward generalized characteristic starting from
x(0)=x_{0}

along which the entropy solution
is discontinuous for sufficiently large
t
.
Assume that (
7.25
)–(
7.26
)
hold uniformly in
y
for
y\geq 0
and
y\leq 0
, respectively.
Then

(i)

If
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{+}(t,0)=\infty
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.28)

\underline{u}_{r}+o(1)\leq u(x\pm,t)\leq\overline{u}_{r}+o(1)\qquad{\rm uniformly\ for}\ x>x(t).

Furthermore, if
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{-}(t,0)>-\infty
,
then the forward generalized characteristic
x(t)
lies in
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
,
and
(
7.28
)
holds uniformly in
x
with
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
.

(ii)

If
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{-}(t,0)=-\infty
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.29)

\underline{u}_{l}+o(1)\leq u(x\pm,t)\leq\overline{u}_{l}+o(1)\qquad{\rm uniformly\ for}\ x<x(t).

Furthermore, if
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{+}(t,0)<\infty
,
then the forward generalized characteristic
x(t)
lies in
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
,
and
(
7.29
)
holds uniformly in
x
with
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
.

In the above,
\overline{u}_{r}
,
\underline{u}_{r}
,
\overline{u}_{l}
, and
\underline{u}_{l}

are given by
(
6.1
)
;

and
\mathcal{H}_{\pm\infty}
are given by
(
6.32
)
–
(
6.33
)
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps accordingly.

1
. Assume that
x>x(t)
.
Denote
y_{\pm}(x,t):=x-tf^{\prime}(u(x\pm,t))
and

(7.30)

\overline{y}_{r}(x,t):=x-tf^{\prime}(\overline{u}_{r}),\qquad\underline{y}_{r}(x,t):=x-tf^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r}).

We first prove the right-hand side of
(
7.28
)
by contradiction.
Otherwise, if the right-hand side of
(
7.28
)
does not hold uniformly in
x
with
x>x(t)
,
then there exist
\delta_{0}>0
and a sequence
(x_{n},t_{n})
in the region of
x>x(t)
with
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}t_{n}=\infty

such that

u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n})>\overline{u}_{r}+\delta_{0}\qquad{\rm for\ sufficiently\ large}\ n.

Since
f^{\prime}(u)
is strictly increasing, this implies that

(7.31)

\bar{l}_{n}:=\overline{y}_{r}(x_{n},t_{n})-y_{\pm}(x_{n},t_{n})=t_{n}\big(f^{\prime}(u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n}))-f^{\prime}(\overline{u}_{r})\big)\ \rightarrow\infty\qquad\mbox{as $n\rightarrow\infty$}.

By
(
A.3
)
,

\rho(u,\overline{u}_{r})
is strictly increasing in
u
,
then
there exists
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon(\delta_{0})>0
such that

\rho(u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n}),\overline{u}_{r})-\overline{u}_{r}=\rho(u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n}),\overline{u}_{r})-\rho(\overline{u}_{r},\overline{u}_{r})>\varepsilon_{0}.

From
(
7.30
)
–
(
7.31
)
,
\overline{y}_{r}(x_{n},t_{n})>y_{\pm}(x_{n},t_{n})\geq x_{0}
.
Since
(
7.25
)

holds uniformly in
y
for
y\geq x_{0}
,
then

(7.32)

\frac{1}{\bar{l}_{n}}\int_{y_{\pm}(x_{n},t_{n})}^{y_{\pm}(x_{n},t_{n})+\bar{l}_{n}}(\varphi(\xi)-\overline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi\leq o_{r}(1;y_{\pm}(x_{n},t_{n}))\ \rightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $n\rightarrow\infty$}.

Since
u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n})\in\mathcal{U}(x_{n},t_{n})
, then

E(u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n});x_{n},t_{n})-E(\overline{u}_{r};x_{n},t_{n})\geq 0
.
By taking
\overline{u}_{r}
and
(x_{n},t_{n})
in
(
7.27
)
,

0\leq\int^{u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n})}_{\overline{u}_{r}}(s-\overline{u}_{r})f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s\leq\frac{1}{t_{n}}\int_{y_{\pm}(x_{n},t_{n})}^{\overline{y}_{r}(x_{n},t_{n})}(\varphi(\xi)-\overline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi,

which, by
(
7.32
)
and
(
A.1
)
, implies that

0\leq\rho(u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n}),\overline{u}_{r})-\overline{u}_{r}\leq\frac{1}{\bar{l}_{n}}\int_{y_{\pm}(x_{n},t_{n})}^{\overline{y}_{r}(x_{n},t_{n})}(\varphi(\xi)-\overline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi\ \rightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $n\rightarrow\infty$}.

This contradicts to that
\rho(u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n}),\overline{u}_{r})-\overline{u}_{r}>\varepsilon_{0}
.

Furthermore, if
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{-}(t,0)>-\infty
,
by Lemma
6.11
,
it follows from
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{+}(t,0)=\infty
that

x(t)
lies in
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
.
Thus,
\underline{y}_{r}(x,t)>x^{+}_{0}
and
y_{\pm}(x,t)\geq x^{+}_{0}
for any
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
.
Similarly, it is direct to check that
the right-hand side of
(
7.28
)
holds uniformly in
x
for
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
.

We now prove the left-hand side of
(
7.28
)
.
There are two cases:

(a).
If
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{-}(t,0)>-\infty
,
then
x(t)
lies in
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
so that

\underline{y}_{r}(x,t)>x^{+}_{0},\quad y_{\pm}(x,t)\geq x^{+}_{0}\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ (x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}.

Otherwise, if the left-hand side of
(
7.28
)
does not hold uniformly in
x
with
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
,
there exist
\delta_{1}>0
and a sequence
(x_{n},t_{n})
with
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}t_{n}=\infty

such that

u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n})<\underline{u}_{r}-\delta_{1}\qquad{\rm for\ sufficiently\ large}\ n,

which, by the strictly increasing property of
f^{\prime}(u)
, implies that

(7.33)

\underline{l}_{n}:=y_{\pm}(x_{n},t_{n})-\underline{y}_{r}(x_{n},t_{n})=t_{n}\big(f^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})-f^{\prime}(u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n}))\big)\ \rightarrow\infty\qquad\mbox{as $n\rightarrow\infty$}.

By
(
A.3
)
,

\rho(u,\underline{u}_{r})
is strictly increasing in
u
,
then
there exists
\varepsilon_{1}=\varepsilon(\delta_{1})>0
such that

\rho(u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n}),\underline{u}_{r})-\underline{u}_{r}=\rho(u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n}),\underline{u}_{r})-\rho(\underline{u}_{r},\underline{u}_{r})<-\varepsilon_{1}.

From
(
7.30
)
and

(
7.33
)
,
y_{\pm}(x_{n},t_{n})>\underline{y}_{r}(x_{n},t_{n})>x^{+}_{0}
.
Since
(
7.25
)

holds uniformly in
y
for
y\geq x_{0}
,
then

(7.34)

\frac{1}{\underline{l}_{n}}\int_{\underline{y}_{r}(x_{n},t_{n})}^{\underline{y}_{r}(x_{n},t_{n})+\underline{l}_{n}}(\varphi(\xi)-\underline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi\geq o_{r}(1;\underline{y}_{r}(x_{n},t_{n}))\ \rightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $n\rightarrow\infty$}.

Since
u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n})\in\mathcal{U}(x_{n},t_{n})
, then

E(u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n});x_{n},t_{n})-E(\underline{u}_{r};x_{n},t_{n})\geq 0
.
By taking
\underline{u}_{r}
and
(x_{n},t_{n})
in
(
7.27
)
,

0\geq-\int^{u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n})}_{\underline{u}_{r}}(s-\underline{u}_{r})f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s\geq\frac{1}{t_{n}}\int^{y_{\pm}(x_{n},t_{n})}_{\underline{y}_{r}(x_{n},t_{n})}(\varphi(\xi)-\underline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi,

which, by
(
7.34
)
and
(
A.1
)
, implies that

0\geq\rho(u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n}),\underline{u}_{r})-\underline{u}_{r}\geq\frac{1}{\underline{l}_{n}}\int^{y_{\pm}(x_{n},t_{n})}_{\underline{y}_{r}(x_{n},t_{n})}(\varphi(\xi)-\underline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi\rightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $n\rightarrow\infty$}.

This contradicts to that
\rho(u(x_{n}{\pm},t_{n}),\underline{u}_{r})-\underline{u}_{r}<-\varepsilon_{1}
.

(b).
If
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{-}(t,0)=-\infty
,
by combining with
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}y^{+}(t,0)=\infty
,
the entropy solution has no divides so that,
by Theorem
6.10
,
the entropy solution satisfies that,
any two shocks in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}

must coincide with each other in a finite time.

Denote
\xi(t):=x_{0}+\lambda t
with
\lambda
satisfying that

(7.35)

\lambda=\frac{f(\overline{u}_{l})-f(\underline{u}_{r})}{\overline{u}_{l}-\underline{u}_{r}}\quad{\rm if\ }\overline{u}_{l}>\underline{u}_{r},\qquad\lambda=f^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})\quad{\rm if\ }\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}.

Then, for
x>x(t)
, there are two subcases:

(b1).
If
x>\max\{x(t),\xi(t)\}
,
then
y_{\pm}(x,t)\geq x_{0}
and
\underline{y}_{r}(x,t)>\xi(t)-tf^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})\geq x_{0}
.
Similar to
(a)
,
the left-hand side of
(
7.28
)
holds uniformly in
x
with
x>\max\{x(t),\xi(t)\}
.

(b2).
If
x(t)<x\leq\xi(t)
,
then

y_{\pm}(x,t)\geq x(t)-tf^{\prime}(u(x(t){+},t))=y^{+}(t,0)>x_{0}\qquad{\rm for\ large}\ t.

If
x=\xi(t)
, we set
(\xi(\kappa),\kappa):=(\xi(t),t)
;
and if
x(t)<x<\xi(t)
, by Lemma
2.8
,
the characteristic line

l_{u(x\pm,t)}(x,t):\,\xi=x+(\tau-t)f^{\prime}(u(x\pm,t))
for
\tau\in(0,t)

must intersect with the straight line
\xi(\tau)=x_{0}+\lambda\tau

at some point
(\xi(\kappa),\kappa)
with
\kappa\in(0,t)
.
Then

\kappa
satisfies that

(7.36)

x_{0}+\kappa\big(\lambda-f^{\prime}(u(x\pm,t))\big)=x-tf^{\prime}(u(x\pm,t))=y_{\pm}(x,t)\geq y^{+}(t,0),

which, by
u(x,t)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+})
and
y^{+}(\infty,0)=\infty
, implies that,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

\kappa\rightarrow\infty\qquad{\rm uniformly\ in\ }x\ {\rm for}\ x\in(x(t),\xi(t)].

Let
y_{\pm}(\xi(\kappa),\kappa):=\xi(\kappa)-\kappa f^{\prime}(u(x\pm,t))
.
Then

(7.37)

y_{\pm}(\xi(\kappa),\kappa)=y_{\pm}(x,t)>y^{+}(t,0)\ \rightarrow\infty\qquad\mbox{as $t\rightarrow\infty$}.

By Lemma
2.9
,
u(x\pm,t)\in\mathcal{U}(\xi(\kappa),\kappa)
so that

E(u(x\pm,t);\xi(\kappa),\kappa)-E(\underline{u}_{r};\xi(\kappa),\kappa)\geq 0,

which, by
(
7.27
)
, implies

(7.38)

0\geq-\int^{u(x\pm,t)}_{\underline{u}_{r}}(s-\underline{u}_{r})f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s\geq\frac{1}{\kappa}\int^{y_{\pm}(\xi(\kappa),\kappa)}_{\underline{y}_{r}(\xi(\kappa),\kappa)}(\varphi(\xi)-\underline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi.

From
(
7.35
)
,
\underline{y}_{r}(\xi(\kappa),\kappa)\geq x_{0}
.
By the same arguments as
(a)
,
it is direct to check from
(
7.38
)
that
the left-hand side of
(
7.28
)
holds uniformly in
x
with
x(t)<x\leq\xi(t)
.

Therefore,
(
7.28
)
holds uniformly in
x
with
x>x(t)
for the case of
(b)
.

2
. By the same argument as in Step 1,
it can be checked from
(
7.26
)
–
(
7.27
)
that (ii) is true.
∎

If
\overline{u}_{r}=\underline{u}_{r}
and
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{l}
,
similar to (
7.25
)–(
7.26
)
it is direct to check that,
as
x\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.39)

\frac{1}{x}\int^{y+x}_{y}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi=\underline{u}_{r}+o_{r}(1;y),

where
o_{r}(1;y)
represents an infinitesimal quantity that depends on
y
;
and, as
x\rightarrow{-}\infty
,

(7.40)

\frac{1}{x}\int^{y+x}_{y}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi=\overline{u}_{l}+o_{l}(1;y),

where
o_{l}(1;y)
represents an infinitesimal quantity that depends on
y
.

Theorem 7.3

(Asymptotic profiles in the
L^{\infty}
–norm)
.

Let
u=u(x,t)
and
\tilde{u}=\tilde{u}(x,t)
be the entropy solutions
of the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)
and the Cauchy problem
(
7.1
)
, respectively.
Assume that
(
7.39
)
and
(
7.40
)
hold uniformly in
y
for
y\geq 0
and
y\leq 0
, respectively.
Then

(i)

When
\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
.
The asymptotic profile of the entropy solution
u=u(x,t)
in the
L^{\infty}
–norm is the rarefaction-constant solution
\tilde{u}=\tilde{u}(x,t)
given by (
7.2
)–(
7.3
) as follows
:

For
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}=\mathcal{K}\cup\big(\cup_{n}\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}\big)

as in
(
6.35
)
,

(a)

In
\mathcal{K}
,
u(x,t)\equiv\tilde{u}(x,t)

and can be expressed by

(7.41)

u(x,t)=\tilde{u}(x,t)=\big(f^{\prime}\big)^{-1}(\frac{x-x_{0}}{t})\qquad{\rm with}\ x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}.

(b)

If there exists
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
with finite
e_{n}
and
h_{n}
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.42)

u(x\pm,t)\cong\tilde{u}(x,t)=c_{n}\qquad{{\rm uniformly\ in}}\ x\ {{\rm for}}\ (x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}.

(c)

If there exists
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
with finite
e_{n}
and
h_{n}=\infty
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.43)

u(x\pm,t)\cong\tilde{u}(x,t)=\underline{u}_{r}\qquad\mbox{\rm uniformly in $x$ for $(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}$}.

(d)

If there exists
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
with finite
h_{n}
and
e_{n}={-}\infty
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.44)

u(x\pm,t)\cong\tilde{u}(x,t)=\overline{u}_{l}\qquad\mbox{\rm uniformly in $x$ for $(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}$}.

(ii)

When
\overline{u}_{l}>\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}=\varnothing
.
The entropy solution
u=u(x,t)
will turn into a single shock asymptotically in

\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
There exist two cases
:

(a)

If
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.45)

u(x\pm,t)=\underline{u}_{r}+o(1)\qquad\mbox{\rm uniformly in $x$ for $(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}$}.

(b)

If
\overline{u}_{l}>\underline{u}_{r}
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.46)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle u(x\pm,t)=\overline{u}_{l}+o(1)\quad&\mbox{\rm uniformly in $x$ for $x<x(t)$},\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle u(x\pm,t)=\underline{u}_{r}+o(1)\quad&\mbox{\rm uniformly in $x$ for $x>x(t)$},\end{cases}

where
x=x(t)
for
t\in[0,\infty)
is a forward generalized characteristic
emitting from any point on the
x
–axis.

In the above,
\overline{u}_{l}
and
\underline{u}_{r}
are given by
(
6.1
)
;

\mathcal{K}_{0}
and
\mathcal{K}
are given by
(
6.11
)
and
(
6.34
)
, respectively
;

and
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
with
c_{n}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\pm\infty}
are given by
(
6.30
)
–
(
6.33
)
.

Proof.

The proof is divided into two steps accordingly.

1.
If
\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
,
by Lemma
6.7
,

\bar{\Phi}(x)>-\infty
and is convex so that,
the Cauchy problem
(
7.1
)
admits a unique rarefaction-constant solution

\tilde{u}=\tilde{u}(x,t)
.
Thus, it follows from
(
7.4
)
that
u(x,t)\equiv\tilde{u}(x,t)
on
\mathcal{K}
, and
(
7.41
)
follows from
(
7.2
)
.
Furthermore, according to
(
7.3
)
,
(
7.42
)
follows from
(
7.6
)
,
and
(
7.43
)
–
(
7.44
)
follow immediately by taking

\overline{u}_{r}=\underline{u}_{r}
and
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{l}
into Lemma
7.2
.

2.
If
\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
, according to Theorem
6.12
(ii),
entropy solution
u(x,t)
possesses no divides and turns into a single shock asymptotically in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
Thus, any forward generalized characteristic will turn into a shock in a finite time.
Furthermore,
(
7.45
)
–
(
7.46
)
follow immediately by taking

\overline{u}_{r}=\underline{u}_{r}
and
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{l}
into Lemma
7.2
.
∎

In order to obtain the decay rates of the entropy solution to its asymptotic profile,
we need the growth rates of the initial data functions
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})

as
x\rightarrow\pm\infty
.
More precisely,
when
\overline{u}_{r}=\underline{u}_{r}
and
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{l}
,
according to
(
7.39
)
–
(
7.40
)
,
we assume that,
for some
\gamma_{r},\gamma_{l}\in[0,1)
,
as
x\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.47)

\int^{y+x}_{y}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi=\underline{u}_{r}x+O(1)\,x^{\gamma_{r}}\qquad\quad\,\;{\rm uniformly\ in}\ y\geq 0;

and, as
x\rightarrow{-}\infty
,

(7.48)

\int^{y+x}_{y}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi=\overline{u}_{l}x+O(1)\,({-}x)^{\gamma_{l}}\qquad\,{\rm uniformly\ in}\ y\leq 0.

Theorem 7.4

(Decay rates in the
L^{\infty}
–norm)
.

Let
u=u(x,t)
and
\tilde{u}=\tilde{u}(x,t)
be the entropy solutions
of the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)

and the Cauchy problem
(
7.1
)
, respectively.
Assume that

(
7.47
)
and

(
7.48
)
hold
for some
\gamma_{r}\in[0,1)
and
\gamma_{l}\in[0,1)
, respectively. Then

(i)

When
\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
.
For
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}=\mathcal{K}\cup\big(\cup_{n}\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}\big)

as in
(
6.35
)
,

(a)

In
\mathcal{K}
,

(
7.41
)
holds
;
especially,
u(x,t)-\tilde{u}(x,t)\equiv 0
.

(b)

If there exists
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
with finite
e_{n}
and
h_{n}
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.49)

|u(x,t)-\tilde{u}(x,t)|=|u(x,t)-c_{n}|\lessapprox C_{n}\Big(\frac{h_{n}-e_{n}}{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{n}}}\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{n}}}

holds uniformly in
x
with
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
.

(c)

If there exists
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
with finite
e_{n}
and
h_{n}=\infty
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.50)

|u(x,t)-\tilde{u}(x,t)|=|u(x,t)-\underline{u}_{r}|\lesssim t^{-\frac{1-\gamma_{r}}{2-\gamma_{r}+\alpha(1-\gamma_{r})}}

holds uniformly in
x
with
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
.

(d)

If there exists
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
with finite
h_{n}
and
e_{n}={-}\infty
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.51)

|u(x,t)-\tilde{u}(x,t)|=|u(x,t)-\overline{u}_{l}|\lesssim t^{-\frac{1-\gamma_{l}}{2-\gamma_{l}+\beta(1-\gamma_{l})}}

holds uniformly in
x
with
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{{-}\infty}
.

(ii)

When
\overline{u}_{l}>\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}=\varnothing
.

(a)

If
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.52)

\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,|u(x,t)-\underline{u}_{r}|\lesssim t^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2-\gamma+\alpha(1-\gamma)}}\qquad{\rm uniformly\ in}\ x\ {\rm for\ }(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+},

where
\gamma:=\max\{\gamma_{r},\gamma_{l}\}
.

(b)

If
\overline{u}_{l}>\underline{u}_{r}
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.53)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\begin{cases}\displaystyle|u(x,t)-\overline{u}_{l}|\lesssim t^{-\frac{1-\gamma_{l}}{2-\gamma_{l}+\beta(1-\gamma_{l})}}\quad&{\rm uniformly\ for\ }\ x<x(t),\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle|u(x,t)-\underline{u}_{r}|\lesssim t^{-\frac{1-\gamma_{r}}{2-\gamma_{r}+\alpha(1-\gamma_{r})}}\quad&{\rm uniformly\ for\ }\ x>x(t),\end{cases}

where
x=x(t)
for
t\in[0,\infty)
is a forward generalized characteristic
emitting from any point on the
x
–axis.

In the above,
\overline{u}_{l}
and
\underline{u}_{r}
are given by
(
6.1
)
;

\mathcal{K}_{0}
and
\mathcal{K}
are given by
(
6.11
)
and
(
6.34
)
, respectively
;

\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
with
c_{n}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\pm\infty}
are given by
(
6.30
)
–
(
6.33
)
;

and parameters
\alpha\geq 0
and
\beta\geq 0
satisfy

\lim_{u\rightarrow\underline{u}_{r}}\frac{f^{\prime\prime}(u)}{|u-\underline{u}_{r}|^{\alpha}}=N_{r}>0,\qquad\lim_{u\rightarrow\overline{u}_{l}}\frac{f^{\prime\prime}(u)}{|u-\overline{u}_{l}|^{\beta}}=N_{l}>0.

Proof.

By Theorem
7.3
, it suffices to check the decay rates in different cases.

1
. Suppose that
\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
.

(
7.49
)
follows from
(
7.8
)
directly.

(a).
If
x_{0}^{+}=\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}<\infty
,
then
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
exists and
(
7.43
)
holds.
For any
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
, we let

(7.54)

y_{\pm}(x,t)=x-tf^{\prime}(u(x\pm,t)),\qquad y_{r}(x,t)=x-tf^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r}).

Then

y_{\pm}(x,t)\geq x_{0}^{+}
and
y_{r}(x,t)>x_{0}^{+}
for any
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}.

Since
u(x\pm,t)\in\mathcal{U}(x,t)
, then

E(u(x\pm,t);x,t)-E(\underline{u}_{r};x,t)\geq 0
.
By taking
\underline{u}_{r}
into
(
7.27
)
,

(7.55)

0\leq\int^{u(x\pm,t)}_{\underline{u}_{r}}(s-\underline{u}_{r})f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s\leq\frac{1}{t}\int_{y_{\pm}(x,t)}^{y_{r}(x,t)}(\varphi(\xi)-\underline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi.

Denote

l(x,t):=\big|y_{r}(x,t)-y_{\pm}(x,t)\big|=t\big|f^{\prime}(u(x\pm,t))-f^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})\big|.

Then

(
7.47
)
implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t}\int_{y_{\pm}(x,t)}^{y_{r}(x,t)}(\varphi(\xi)-\underline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi\leq\begin{cases}L\|\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}\|_{L^{\infty}}\,t^{-1}=O(1)\,t^{-1}\quad&{\rm if}\ l(x,t)\leq L,\\[2.84526pt]
O(1)\,\big(t|f^{\prime}(u(x\pm,t))-f^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})|\big)^{\gamma_{r}}\,t^{-1}\quad&{\rm if}\ l(x,t)>L.\end{cases}

Therefore, by using
(
7.54
)

and
(
A.5
)
–
(
A.6
)
,
it is direct to check from
(
7.55
)
that

(7.56)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle|u(x\pm,t)-\underline{u}_{r}|\leq O(1)\,t^{-\frac{1}{2+\alpha}}\quad&{\rm if}\ l(x,t)\leq L,\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle|u(x\pm,t)-\underline{u}_{r}|\leq O(1)\,t^{-\frac{1-{\gamma_{r}}}{2-{\gamma_{r}}+\alpha(1-{\gamma_{r}})}}\quad&{\rm if}\ l(x,t)>L.\end{cases}

Since
\frac{1-{\gamma_{r}}}{2-{\gamma_{r}}+\alpha(1-{\gamma_{r}})}\leq\frac{1}{2+\alpha}

for
\alpha\geq 0
and
{\gamma_{r}}\in[0,1)
,

(
7.56
)
implies
(
7.50
)
.

(b).
Similar to
(a)
,
it can be checked from
(
7.40
)
,
(
7.44
)
, and
(
7.48
)
that
(
7.51
)
holds.

2
. Suppose that
\overline{u}_{l}>\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}=\varnothing
.

(a).
If
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
,
it is direct to check from
(
7.45
)
and
(
7.47
)
–
(
7.48
)
that,
as
x\rightarrow\pm\infty
,

\Big|\int^{y+x}_{y}(\varphi(\xi)-\underline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi\Big|\leq O(1)\,|x|^{\gamma}\qquad{\rm uniformly\ in}\ y\in({-}\infty,\infty).

By the same arguments as those for
(
7.54
)
–
(
7.56
)
,
it can be checked that
(
7.52
)
holds.

(b).
If
\overline{u}_{l}>\underline{u}_{r}
.
Denote
\xi(t):=x_{0}+\lambda t
with

\lambda=\frac{f(\overline{u}_{l})-f(\underline{u}_{r})}{\overline{u}_{l}-\underline{u}_{r}}
.
There are two cases:

(b1).
For the case that
x>x(t)
, by Lemma
7.2
, there are two subcases:

If
x>\max\{x(t),\xi(t)\}
, then
y_{\pm}(x,t)\geq x_{0}
and
y_{r}(x,t)>x_{0}
.
Since
(
7.47
)
holds for
\gamma_{r}\in[0,1)
,
similar to
(
7.54
)
–
(
7.56
)
,
it can be checked that
(
7.53
)
holds for
x>\max\{x(t),\xi(t)\}
.

If
x(t)<x\leq\xi(t)
,
by the same argument as for the case of
(b2)
in the proof of Lemma
7.2
,
from
(
7.37
)
and
(
7.54
)
,

y_{\pm}(\xi(\kappa),\kappa)\geq x_{0}
and
y_{r}(\xi(\kappa),\kappa)>x_{0}
so that
(
7.38
)
holds.
Applying the same argument as that for
(
7.54
)
–
(
7.56
)
to
(
7.38
)
,
we obtain that, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

|u(x\pm,t)-\underline{u}_{r}|\leq O(1)\,\kappa^{-\frac{1-\gamma_{r}}{2-\gamma_{r}+\alpha(1-\gamma_{r})}}\qquad{\rm uniformly\ in}\ x\ {\rm with}\ x(t)<x\leq\xi(t).

Thus, to prove
(
7.53
)
,
it suffices to show that, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.57)

\frac{\kappa}{t}=1+o(1)\qquad{\rm uniformly\ in\ }x\ {\rm for}\ x(t)<x\leq\xi(t).

In fact, if
x=\xi(t)
, then
\kappa=t
.
If
x(t)<x<\xi(t)
, from
(
7.36
)
,

(7.58)

0>(1-\frac{\kappa}{t})\big(f^{\prime}(u(x\pm,t))-\lambda\big)=\frac{x-\xi(t)}{t}>\frac{x(t)-\xi(t)}{t}=\frac{x(t)-x_{0}}{t}-\lambda.

From
(
7.46
)
, we obtain

\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow\infty}\Big(\frac{x(t)}{t}-\lambda\Big)=\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{t}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\Big(\frac{f(u^{-}(\tau))-f(u^{+}(\tau))}{u^{-}(\tau)-u^{+}(\tau)}-\frac{f(\overline{u}_{l})-f(\underline{u}_{r})}{\overline{u}_{l}-\underline{u}_{r}}\Big)\,{\rm d}\tau=0,

so that, by
(
7.58
)
, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

\frac{x-\xi(t)}{t}\longrightarrow 0
uniformly in
x
for
x(t)<x\leq\xi(t),

which, by combining with
(
7.46
)
and
(
7.58
)
, yields
(
7.57
)
.

(b2).
Similar to
(b1)
,
it can be checked that
(
7.53
)
holds for the case that
x<x(t)
.

Up to now, we have completed the proof of Theorem
7.4
.
∎

If
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfies that

\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}\in L^{p}([0,\infty))
with
p\in[1,\infty)
,
then
\overline{u}_{r}=\underline{u}_{r}
.
By the Hölder inequality,

(7.59)

\big|\int_{y}^{y+x}(\varphi(\xi)-\underline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi\big|\leq\|\varphi-\underline{u}_{r}\|_{L^{p}}\,|x|^{1-\frac{1}{p}},

which implies that
(
7.47
)
holds for
\gamma_{r}=1-\frac{1}{p}
.
Similarly, if
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfies that

\varphi(x)-\overline{u}_{l}\in L^{q}(({-}\infty,0])
with
q\in[1,\infty)
,
then
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{l}
,
and
(
7.48
)
holds for
\gamma_{l}=1-\frac{1}{q}
.

Applying Theorem
7.4
to the
L^{\infty}
initial data function
\varphi(x)

satisfying that

\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}\in L^{p}([0,\infty))

and
\varphi(x)-\overline{u}_{l}\in L^{q}(({-}\infty,0])
with
p,q\in[1,\infty)
,
we obtain the following theorem.

Theorem 7.5

(Decay rates in the
L^{\infty}
–norm)
.

Let
u=u(x,t)
be the entropy solution of the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)

with initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying that

\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}\in L^{p}([0,\infty))

and
\varphi(x)-\overline{u}_{l}\in L^{q}(({-}\infty,0])
with
p,q\in[1,\infty)
,
and let
\tilde{u}=\tilde{u}(x,t)
be the entropy solutions
of the Cauchy problem
(
7.1
)
as in (
7.2
)–(
7.3
).
Then

(i)

When
\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
.
For
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}=\mathcal{K}\cup\big(\cup_{n}\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}\big)

as in
(
6.35
)
,

(a)

In
\mathcal{K}
,

(
7.41
)
holds
;
especially,
u(x,t)-\tilde{u}(x,t)\equiv 0
.

(b)

If there exists
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
with finite
e_{n}
and
h_{n}
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.60)

|u(x,t)-\tilde{u}(x,t)|=|u(x,t)-c_{n}|\lessapprox C_{n}\Big(\frac{h_{n}-e_{n}}{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{n}}}\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{n}}}

holds uniformly in
x
with
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
.

(c)

If there exists
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
with finite
e_{n}
and
h_{n}=\infty
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.61)

|u(x,t)-\tilde{u}(x,t)|=|u(x,t)-\underline{u}_{r}|\lessapprox C_{r}\|\varphi-\underline{u}_{r}\|_{L^{p}([x_{0}^{+},\infty))}^{\frac{p}{p+1+\alpha}}\,t^{-\frac{1}{p+1+\alpha}}

holds uniformly in
x
with
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
.

(d)

If there exists
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
with finite
h_{n}
and
e_{n}={-}\infty
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.62)

|u(x,t)-\tilde{u}(x,t)|=|u(x,t)-\overline{u}_{l}|\lessapprox C_{l}\|\varphi-\overline{u}_{l}\|_{L^{q}(({-}\infty,x_{0}^{-}])}^{\frac{q}{q+1+\beta}}\,t^{-\frac{1}{q+1+\beta}}

holds uniformly in
x
with
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
.

(ii)

When
\overline{u}_{l}>\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}=\varnothing
.

(a)

If
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
, then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.63)

|u(x,t)-\underline{u}_{r}|\lessapprox C_{r}\|\varphi-\underline{u}_{r}\|_{L^{s}(\Omega)}^{\frac{s}{s+1+\alpha}}\,t^{-\frac{1}{s+1+\alpha}}

holds uniformly in
x
with
(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
,
where
s:=\max\{p,q\}
,
\Omega=[0,\infty)
if
p>q
,
