\Omega=({-}\infty,0]
if
p<q
, and
\Omega=\mathbb{R}
if
p=q
.

(b)

If
\overline{u}_{l}>\underline{u}_{r}
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.64)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\begin{cases}|u(x,t)-\overline{u}_{l}|\lessapprox C_{l}\|\varphi-\overline{u}_{l}\|_{L^{q}(({-}\infty,x_{0}])}^{\frac{q}{q+1+\beta}}\,t^{-\frac{1}{q+1+\beta}}\ &{\rm uniformly\ for\ }x<x(t),\\[8.53581pt]
|u(x,t)-\underline{u}_{r}|\lessapprox C_{r}\|\varphi-\underline{u}_{r}\|_{L^{p}([x_{0},\infty))}^{\frac{p}{p+1+\alpha}}\,t^{-\frac{1}{p+1+\alpha}}\ &{\rm uniformly\ for\ }x>x(t).\end{cases}

where
x=x(t)
for
t\in[0,\infty)
is a forward generalized characteristic
emitting from any point
x_{0}
on the
x
–axis.

In the above,

\overline{u}_{l}
and
\underline{u}_{r}
are given by
(
6.1
)
;

\mathcal{K}_{0}
and
\mathcal{K}
are given by
(
6.11
)
and
(
6.34
)
, respectively
;

\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
with
c_{n}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\pm\infty}
are given by
(
6.30
)
–
(
6.33
)
;

and constants
C_{n},C_{r},C_{l}
and parameters
\alpha_{n},\alpha,\beta
are given by

{\rm(a)}

\,C_{n}=\big(\frac{1+\alpha_{n}}{N_{n}}\big)^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{n}}}

with
\alpha_{n}\geq 0
satisfying that

\lim_{u\rightarrow c_{n}}\frac{f^{\prime\prime}(u)}{|u-c_{n}|^{\alpha_{n}}}=N_{n}>0
.

{\rm(b)}

\,C_{r}=\big(\frac{2+\alpha}{1+\alpha}\big)^{\frac{p}{p+1+\alpha}}\big(\frac{1+\alpha}{N_{r}}\big)^{\frac{1}{p+1+\alpha}}

and
C_{l}=\big(\frac{2+\beta}{1+\beta}\big)^{\frac{q}{q+1+\beta}}\big(\frac{1+\beta}{N_{l}}\big)^{\frac{1}{q+1+\beta}}

with
\alpha\geq 0
and
\beta\geq 0
satisfying that

\lim\limits_{u\rightarrow\underline{u}_{r}}\frac{f^{\prime\prime}(u)}{|u-\underline{u}_{r}|^{\alpha}}=N_{r}>0
and

\lim\limits_{u\rightarrow\overline{u}_{l}}\frac{f^{\prime\prime}(u)}{|u-\overline{u}_{l}|^{\beta}}=N_{l}>0
, respectively.

Proof.

By
(
7.59
)
,
Theorem
7.5
follows from Theorem
7.4
by
taking
\gamma_{r}=1-\frac{1}{p}
and
\gamma_{l}=1-\frac{1}{q}
,
except to check the constants in
(
7.61
)
–
(
7.64
)
.

We now check constant
C_{r}
in
(
7.61
)
as an example.
All the other constants in
(
7.62
)
–
(
7.64
)
can be checked similarly.
Suppose that
x_{0}^{+}=\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}<\infty
.
For any
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
,
since
u(x\pm,t)\in\mathcal{U}(x,t)
, then

E(u(x\pm,t);x,t)-E(\underline{u}_{r};x,t)\geq 0

so that, by taking
\underline{u}_{r}
into
(
7.27
)
,

(7.65)

0\leq\int^{u(x\pm,t)}_{\underline{u}_{r}}(s-\underline{u}_{r})f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s\leq\frac{1}{t}\int_{x-tf^{\prime}(u(x\pm,t))}^{x-tf^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})}(\varphi(\xi)-\underline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi.

From
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
in
(
6.32
)
,

L_{\underline{u}_{r}}(x_{0}^{+}):x=x_{0}^{+}+tf^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})
is a divide so that,

x-tf^{\prime}(u(x\pm,t))\geq x_{0}^{+},\quad x-tf^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})>x_{0}^{+}\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ (x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}.

Since
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying that

\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}\in L^{p}([0,\infty))
with
p\in[1,\infty)
,
then
\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}\in L^{p}([x_{0}^{+},\infty))
with
p\in[1,\infty)
.
Thus, by the Hölder inequality,

\int_{x-tf^{\prime}(u(x\pm,t))}^{x-tf^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})}(\varphi(\xi)-\underline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi\leq\|\varphi-\underline{u}_{r}\|_{L^{p}([x_{0}^{+},\infty))}\,\big(t|f^{\prime}(u(x\pm,t))-f^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})|\big)^{1-\frac{1}{p}},

which, by
(
7.65
)
and
(
A.5
)
–
(
A.6
)
, implies that,
for
u(x\pm,t)\neq\underline{u}_{r}
,

0\leq\dfrac{\frac{N_{r}}{2+\alpha}+o(1)}{\big(\frac{N_{r}}{1+\alpha}\big)^{1-\frac{1}{p}}+o(1)}\,\big|u(x\pm,t)-\underline{u}_{r}\big|^{\frac{p+1+\alpha}{p}}\leq\|\varphi-\underline{u}_{r}\|_{L^{p}([x_{0}^{+},\infty))}\,t^{-\frac{1}{p}}.

This, by a simple calculation, yields constant
C_{r}
in
(
7.61
)
.

Up to now, we have proved Theorem
7.5
.
∎

If
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
, define
L(\mathcal{K}_{0})
as follows:
If there exists a bounded interval
(e_{n},h_{n})\subset\mathcal{K}^{c}_{0}
in
(
6.29
)
,

(7.66)

L(\mathcal{K}_{0}):=\sup_{n}\big\{h_{n}-e_{n}\,:\,(e_{n},h_{n})\subset\mathcal{K}^{c}_{0}\ {\rm is\ a\ bounded\ interval}\big\};

and, if there exists no bounded interval
(e_{n},h_{n})\subset\mathcal{K}^{c}_{0}
,
we set
L(\mathcal{K}_{0})=0
.

Corollary 7.6

(Asymptotic behaviors in the
L^{\infty}
–norm for the case of flux functions of uniform convexity)
.

Suppose that
f^{\prime\prime}(u)\geq c_{0}>0
on
u\in\mathbb{R}
.
Let
u=u(x,t)
be the entropy solution of the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)

with initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying that

\varphi(x)-\overline{u}_{l}\in L^{p}(({-}\infty,0])

and
\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}\in L^{p}([0,\infty))
with
p\in[1,\infty)
.
Then

(i)

If
\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
.
Then
(
7.4
)
holds,
(
7.60
)
holds with
C_{n}=c_{0}^{-1}
and
\alpha_{n}=0
,
and
(
7.61
)
–
(
7.62
)
hold with

C_{r}=C_{l}=\big(2^{p}c_{0}^{-1}\big)^{\frac{1}{p+1}}
and
\alpha=\beta=0.

Furthermore, if
L(\mathcal{K}_{0})
in
(
7.66
)
satisfies that
L(\mathcal{K}_{0})<\infty
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.67)

|u(x,t)-\tilde{u}(x,t)|\lessapprox\tilde{C}\,t^{-\frac{1}{p+1}}\qquad{\rm uniformly\ in}\ x\ {\rm with}\ (x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+},

where
\tilde{C}
is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{C}=\begin{cases}\big(2^{p}c_{0}^{-1}P_{r}\big)^{\frac{1}{p+1}}\quad&{\rm if}\ \sup\mathcal{K}_{0}<\infty,\ \inf\mathcal{K}_{0}=-\infty,\\[2.84526pt]
\big(2^{p}c_{0}^{-1}P_{l}\big)^{\frac{1}{p+1}}\quad&{\rm if}\ \sup\mathcal{K}_{0}=\infty,\ \inf\mathcal{K}_{0}>-\infty,\\[2.84526pt]
\big(2^{p}c_{0}^{-1}\max\{P_{r},P_{l}\}\big)^{\frac{1}{p+1}}\quad&{\rm if}\ \sup\mathcal{K}_{0}<\infty,\ \inf\mathcal{K}_{0}>-\infty\end{cases}

for
P_{r}=\|\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}\|^{p}_{L^{p}([x_{0}^{+},\infty))}

and
P_{l}=\|\varphi(x)-\overline{u}_{l}\|^{p}_{L^{p}(({-}\infty,x_{0}^{-}])}
.

In particular, if
L(\mathcal{K}_{0})<\infty
with
\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}=\infty
and
\inf\mathcal{K}_{0}=-\infty
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.68)

\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,|u(x,t)-\tilde{u}(x,t)|\lessapprox(2c_{0})^{-1}L(\mathcal{K}_{0})\,t^{-1}\quad{\rm uniformly\ in}\ x\ {\rm with}\ (x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}.

(ii)

If
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}=\varnothing
,
then, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.69)

|u(x,t)-\underline{u}_{r}|\lessapprox\tilde{C}_{r}\,t^{-\frac{1}{p+1}}\qquad{\rm uniformly\ in}\ x\ {\rm with}\ (x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+},

where
\tilde{C}_{r}=\big(2^{p}f^{\prime\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})^{-1}\|\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}\|^{p}_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R})}\big)^{\frac{1}{p+1}}
.

(iii)

If
\overline{u}_{l}>\underline{u}_{r}
, then,
for any forward generalized characteristic
X(x_{0},0):x=x(t)
for
t\geq 0
emitting from
x_{0}
on the
x
–axis,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.70)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle|u(x,t)-\overline{u}_{l}|\lessapprox\bar{C}_{l}\,t^{-\frac{1}{p+1}}\quad&{\rm uniformly\ for\ }\ x<x(t),\\[2.84526pt]
\displaystyle|u(x,t)-\underline{u}_{r}|\lessapprox\bar{C}_{r}\,t^{-\frac{1}{p+1}}\quad&{\rm uniformly\ for\ }\ x>x(t),\end{cases}

where
\bar{C}_{l}
and
\bar{C}_{r}
are given by

\begin{cases}\bar{C}_{l}=\big(2^{p}f^{\prime\prime}(\overline{u}_{l})^{-1}\|\varphi(x)-\overline{u}_{l}\|^{p}_{L^{p}(({-}\infty,x_{0}])}\big)^{\frac{1}{p+1}},\\[5.69054pt]
\bar{C}_{r}=\big(2^{p}f^{\prime\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})^{-1}\|\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}\|^{p}_{L^{p}([x_{0},\infty))}\big)^{\frac{1}{p+1}}.\end{cases}

In the above,

\overline{u}_{r}
,
\underline{u}_{r}
,
\overline{u}_{l}
, and
\underline{u}_{l}

are given by
(
6.1
)
;

and
\mathcal{K}_{0}
is given by
(
6.11
)
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps accordingly.

1
. Since
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
,
from Lemma
6.8
and the definition of
\tilde{u}=\tilde{u}(x,t)
in
(
7.1
)
,

\tilde{u}(x,t)\equiv{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(e_{n})\quad{\rm on}\ \mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})},\qquad\tilde{u}(x,t)\equiv{\rm D}_{\pm}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}^{\pm})\quad{\rm on}\ \mathcal{H}_{\pm\infty},

if the related case appears.
From Theorem
7.1
,
u(x,t)=\tilde{u}(x,t)
on
\mathcal{K}
.

Since
f^{\prime\prime}(u)\geq c_{0}>0
on
\mathbb{R}
,
it is direct to check from Theorem
7.5
that

(
7.60
)
holds with
C_{n}=c_{0}^{-1}
and
\alpha_{n}=0
,
and
(
7.61
)
–
(
7.62
)
hold
with
C_{r}=C_{l}=(2^{p}c_{0}^{-1})^{\frac{1}{p+1}}
and
\alpha=\beta=0
.

For the case that
L(\mathcal{K}_{0})<\infty
,
since
\varphi(x)-\overline{u}_{l}\in L^{p}(({-}\infty,0])

and
\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}\in L^{p}([0,\infty))
,
it follows from
(
7.60
)
–
(
7.62
)
that
(
7.67
)
holds.

In particular, for the case that
L(\mathcal{K}_{0})<\infty
with
\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}=\infty

and
\inf\mathcal{K}_{0}=-\infty
,
both
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
and
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
do not appear.
Since
f^{\prime\prime}(u)\geq c_{0}>0
on
\mathbb{R}
, then
\alpha_{n}\equiv 0

and
C_{n}=f^{\prime\prime}(c_{n})^{-1}\leq c_{0}^{-1}
in
(
7.60
)
,
which, by
L(\mathcal{K}_{0})<\infty
,
yields
(
7.68
)
.

2
.
(
7.69
)
follows by taking
q=p
and
\alpha=0
in
(
7.63
)
.

3
.
(
7.70
)
follows by taking
q=p
and
\alpha=\beta=0
in
(
7.64
)
.
∎

Remark 7.2
.

Suppose that
f^{\prime\prime}(u)\geq c_{0}>0
as in Corollary
7.6
. Then

(i)

For Example
6.1
,
the initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfies that

\varphi(x)-m
has compact support,
i.e.
,

{\rm spt}(\varphi(x)-m)\subset[-R,R]
for some
R>0
.
Then
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
.

If, for any
\,x\in[-R,R]
,

\int_{0}^{x}(\varphi(\xi)-m)\,{\rm d}\xi\geq\int_{0}^{R}(\varphi(\xi)-m)\,{\rm d}\xi=\int_{0}^{-R}(\varphi(\xi)-m)\,{\rm d}\xi,

then
\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}=\infty
and
\inf\mathcal{K}_{0}=-\infty
with
L(\mathcal{K}_{0})\leq 2R
so that,
from
(
7.68
)
, the entropy solution
u=u(x,t)
decays to

\tilde{u}(x,t)\equiv m
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
with decay rate

(c_{0})^{-1}R\,t^{-1}
.

Otherwise,

\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}<\infty
and/or
\inf\mathcal{K}_{0}<-\infty
with
L(\mathcal{K}_{0})\leq 2R
so that,
from
(
7.67
)
, the entropy solution
u=u(x,t)
decays to

\tilde{u}(x,t)\equiv m
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
with decay rate
t^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.

(ii)

For Example
6.2
,
the initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is a periodic function
with the minimum positive period
p>0
and its average
m
over the period.
Thus,

\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}=\infty
and
\inf\mathcal{K}_{0}=-\infty
with
L(\mathcal{K}_{0})\leq p
so that,
from
(
7.68
)
, the entropy solution
u=u(x,t)
decays to

\tilde{u}(x,t)\equiv m
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
with decay rate
(2c_{0})^{-1}p\,t^{-1}
.

(iii)

For Example
6.3
,
initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfies that

\varphi(x)-m\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
, then
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}=m
.
In general, from
(
7.67
)
–
(
7.69
)
,
the entropy solution
u=u(x,t)
decays to
\tilde{u}(x,t)\equiv m
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})

with decay rate
t^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.
For the special case that
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
and
L(\mathcal{K}_{0})<\infty
with
\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}=\infty
and
\inf\mathcal{K}_{0}=-\infty
,
it follows from
(
7.68
)
that the entropy solution
u=u(x,t)
decays to

\tilde{u}(x,t)\equiv m
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
with the decay rate
t^{-1}
.

7.2.
Generalized
N
–waves and decay of entropy solutions in the
L^{p}_{{\rm loc}}
–norm

We now consider the decay of entropy solutions in
L^{p}_{{\rm loc}}
–norm.
We first introduce the notion of generalized
N
–waves,
and then prove that entropy solutions decay to the generalized
N
–waves in the
L^{p}_{{\rm loc}}
–norm,
when the entropy solutions possess at least one divide.

Definition 7.7

(Generalized
N
–waves)
.

Let the initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfies that

\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
for
\mathcal{K}_{0}
given by
(
6.11
)
.
The generalized
N
–wave
w=w(x,t)
is defined in forms by
(
see
Fig.

7.2
)

(7.71)

w(x,t)=\big(f^{\prime}\big)^{-1}\big(\frac{x-\xi}{t}\big)\qquad{\rm for}\ \xi\in\mathcal{K}_{0},

expressed by

(i)

For the case that
(x,t)\in\mathcal{K}
as in
(
6.34
)
,

(7.72)

w(x,t)=\big(f^{\prime}\big)^{-1}\big(\frac{x-x_{0}}{t}\big)\qquad{\rm if}\ (x,t)\in L_{c}(x_{0})

for all divides
L_{c}(x_{0})\subset\mathcal{K}
,
i.e.
,

x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
and
c\in\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
.

(ii)

If there exists
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
as in
(
6.31
)

with
c_{n}
given by
(
6.30
)
,
and
e_{n}(t):=e_{n}+tf^{\prime}(c_{n})
and
h_{n}(t):=h_{n}+tf^{\prime}(c_{n})
,
then, for any chosen forward generalized characteristic
x_{n}(t)
for
t\geq 0
in
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
,

(7.73)

w(x,t)=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\big(f^{\prime}\big)^{-1}\big(\frac{x-e_{n}}{t}\big)\quad&{\rm if}\ e_{n}(t)<x<x_{n}(t),\\[11.38109pt]
\displaystyle\big(f^{\prime}\big)^{-1}\big(\frac{x-h_{n}}{t}\big)\quad&{\rm if}\ x_{n}(t)<x<h_{n}(t).\end{cases}

(iii)

If
x_{0}^{+}=\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}<\infty
,
then
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
as in
(
6.32
)
exists
with
\underline{u}_{r}={\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}^{+})
and

x_{0}^{+}(t)=x_{0}^{+}+tf^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})
.
For any chosen forward generalized characteristic
x_{+}(t)
for
t\geq 0
in
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
,

(7.74)

w(x,t)=\big(f^{\prime}\big)^{-1}\big(\frac{x-x_{0}^{+}}{t}\big)\qquad{\rm if}\ x_{0}^{+}(t)<x<x_{+}(t).

(iv)

If
x_{0}^{-}=\inf\mathcal{K}_{0}>-\infty
,
then
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
as in
(
6.33
)
exists
with
\overline{u}_{l}={\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}^{-})
and

x_{0}^{-}(t)=x_{0}^{-}+tf^{\prime}(\overline{u}_{l})
.
For any chosen forward generalized characteristic
x_{-}(t)
for
t\geq 0
in
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
,

(7.75)

w(x,t)=\big(f^{\prime}\big)^{-1}\big(\frac{x-x_{0}^{-}}{t}\big)\qquad{\rm if}\ x_{-}(t)<x<x_{0}^{-}(t).

In the above,

\mathcal{D}(x_{0})
and
{\rm D}_{\pm}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})

are given by
(
6.5
)
and
(
6.16
)
, respectively.

Figure 7.2.
The four types of generalized
N
-waves:

\mathcal{SD}
,
\mathcal{R}
,
\mathcal{UD}
, and
\mathcal{DS}
.

From Definition
7.7
,
the generalized
N
–wave
w=w(x,t)
is the union of the four types of waves

\mathcal{SD}
,
\mathcal{R}
,
\mathcal{UD}
, and
\mathcal{DS}

as showed in Fig.
7.2
,
all of which are bounded by two divides, or by a shock curve and a divide.
From
(
6.6
)
, for any
x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
,

\mathcal{D}(x_{0})=[{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}),{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})]\subset\mathcal{C}(x_{0})\subset[\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(x_{0}),\underline{{\rm D}}_{+}\Phi(x_{0})].

Remark 7.3

(Detailed description of generalized
N
–waves)
.

From
(
7.71
)
, the form of the generalized
N
–wave
w(x,t)
looks like
a “generalized rarefaction wave” emitting from the point set
\mathcal{K}_{0}
,
instead of a single point for the case of rarefaction waves.
For the cases of
\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{UD}
,
from
(
7.41
)
and
(
7.72
)
,

(7.76)

w(x,t)=\tilde{u}(x,t)=u(x,t)\qquad\ {\rm on}\ \mathcal{K}.

For the cases of
\mathcal{SD}
and
\mathcal{DS}
,
we have

(i)

For the case that
\mathcal{SD}\subset\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
,
bounded by a shock
x_{n}(t)
in
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
from the left
and a divide
L_{c_{n}}(h_{n})
from the right,
by Theorem
3.9
, there are three subcases:

(a)

If
\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(h_{n})<{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(h_{n})

with
\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(h_{n})\notin\mathcal{C}(h_{n})
,
there exists a shock
\mathcal{S}
emitting from
h_{n}
and lying on the left of divide
h_{n}(t)
.
Since any two forward generalized characteristics in
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}

coincide with each other on
t>t_{n}

for sufficiently large
t_{n}>0
, then

(7.77)

w(x,t)=u(x,t)\qquad\ \mbox{\rm if $x_{n}(t)<x<h_{n}(t)$ and $t\geq t_{n}$}.

(b)

If
\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(h_{n})<{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(h_{n})

with
\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(h_{n})\in\mathcal{C}(h_{n})
,
then the forward generalized characteristic of points on the characteristic segment
emitting from
h_{n}

with speed
f^{\prime}(\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(h_{n}))

coincides with
x_{n}(t)
for large time so that
(
7.77
)
holds.

(c)

If
\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(h_{n})={\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(h_{n})\in\mathcal{C}(h_{n})
,
then, for any
x\in(x_{n}(t),h_{n}(t))
,

x-tf^{\prime}(u(x{\pm},t))<h_{n}=x-tf^{\prime}(w(x,t)),

which, by the strictly increasing property of
f^{\prime}(u)
, implies

w(x,t)<u(x,t)\qquad\ {\rm if}\ x_{n}(t)<x<h_{n}(t).

The case of
\mathcal{SD}\subset\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
is the same
via replacing
h_{n}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
by
x_{0}^{-}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
,
etc.
.

(ii)

Similar to
\mathcal{SD}
,
for
\mathcal{DS}
with
e_{n}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}

(
or
x_{0}^{+}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
)
,
there are three subcases:

(d)

If
\underline{{\rm D}}_{+}\Phi(e_{n})>{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(e_{n})

with
\underline{{\rm D}}_{+}\Phi(e_{n})\notin\mathcal{C}(e_{n})
,
for sufficiently large
t^{\prime}_{n}>0
,

(7.78)

w(x,t)=u(x,t)\qquad\ {\rm if}\ e_{n}(t)<x<x_{n}(t),\ t\geq t^{\prime}_{n}.

(e)

If
\underline{{\rm D}}_{+}\Phi(e_{n})>{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(e_{n})

with
\underline{{\rm D}}_{+}\Phi(e_{n})\in\mathcal{C}(e_{n})
, then
(
7.78
)
holds.

(f)

If
\underline{{\rm D}}_{+}\Phi(e_{n})={\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(e_{n})\in\mathcal{C}(h_{n})
,
then

w(x,t)>u(x,t)\qquad\ {\rm if}\ e_{n}(t)<x<x_{n}(t).

See also Fig.
7.3
.

Figure 7.3.
The detailed description of generalized
N
–waves as in Remark
7.3
.

According to Lemma
6.11
,

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}y^{-}(\infty,0)=e_{n},&y^{+}(\infty,0)=h_{n}&{\rm for}\ x_{n}(t)\in\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})},\\[2.84526pt]
y^{-}(\infty,0)=x_{0}^{+},&y^{+}(\infty,0)=\infty&{\rm for}\ x_{+}(t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty},\\[2.84526pt]
y^{-}(\infty,0)=-\infty,&y^{+}(\infty,0)=x_{0}^{-}&{\rm for}\ x_{-}(t)\in\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}.\end{array}\right.

For the case that
x_{n}(t)\in\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
,

f^{\prime}(v^{\pm}(t))-f^{\prime}(u^{\pm}(t))=o(1)\,t^{-1}
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,
where
v^{+}(t)
and
v^{-}(t)
are given by
(
6.39
)
and
(
6.42
)
, respectively.
Then, from
(
7.8
)
and
(
A.5
)
,
if
(
A.4
)
holds with
c=c_{n}
and
\alpha=\alpha_{n}
, then

\displaystyle|v^{\pm}(t)-c_{n}|

\displaystyle\leq|v^{\pm}(t)-u^{\pm}(t)|+|u^{\pm}(t)-c_{n}|

(7.79)

\displaystyle\leq o(1)t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{n}}}+O(1)t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{n}}}=O(1)\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{n}}},

which, by
(
6.39
)
and
(
6.42
)
, implies that,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\int_{e_{n}}^{y^{-}(t,0)}(\varphi(\xi)-c_{n})\,{\rm d}\xi\leq O(1)\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{n}}}\big(y^{-}(t,0)-e_{n}\big),\\[11.38109pt]
\displaystyle\int_{h_{n}}^{y^{+}(t,0)}(\varphi(\xi)-c_{n})\,{\rm d}\xi\leq O(1)\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{n}}}\big(h_{n}-y^{+}(t,0)\big).\end{cases}

Since
\Phi(x)>\bar{\Phi}(x)
on
(e_{n},h_{n})
with

\Phi(e_{n})=\bar{\Phi}(e_{n})
and
\Phi(h_{n})=\bar{\Phi}(h_{n})
,
then, from
(
6.19
)
,

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\int_{e_{n}}^{x}(\varphi(\xi)-c_{n})\,{\rm d}\xi=\Phi(x)-\bar{\Phi}(x)>0\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ x\in(e_{n},h_{n}),\\[11.38109pt]
\displaystyle\int_{h_{n}}^{x}(\varphi(\xi)-c_{n})\,{\rm d}\xi=\Phi(x)-\bar{\Phi}(x)>0\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ x\in(e_{n},h_{n}).\end{cases}

Therefore, we can assume that, for some
\sigma_{n}>0
,

(7.80)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\int_{e_{n}}^{x}(\varphi(\xi)-c_{n})\,{\rm d}\xi\geq O(1)\,|x-e_{n}|^{1+\sigma_{n}}\qquad{\rm as}\ x\rightarrow e_{n}{+},\\[11.38109pt]
\displaystyle\int_{h_{n}}^{x}(\varphi(\xi)-c_{n})\,{\rm d}\xi\geq O(1)\,|x-h_{n}|^{1+\sigma_{n}}\qquad{\rm as}\ x\rightarrow h_{n}{-}.\end{cases}

Similarly, for the case that
x_{+}(t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
,
if
(
A.4
)
holds with
c=\underline{u}_{r}
and
\alpha=\alpha_{r}
,
and
\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}\in L^{p}([0,\infty))
, then

0\leq\int_{x_{0}^{+}}^{y^{-}(t,0)}(\varphi(\xi)-\underline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi\leq O(1)\,t^{-\frac{1}{p+1+\alpha_{r}}}\big(y^{-}(t,0)-x_{0}^{+}\big),

so that we can assume that, for some
\sigma_{r}>0
,

(7.81)

\int_{x_{0}^{+}}^{x}(\varphi(\xi)-\underline{u}_{r})\,{\rm d}\xi\geq O(1)\,|x-x_{0}^{+}|^{1+\sigma_{r}}\qquad{\rm as}\ x\rightarrow x_{0}^{+}{+};

and, for the case that
x_{-}(t)\in\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
,
if
(
A.4
)
holds with
c=\overline{u}_{l}
and
\alpha=\alpha_{l}
, and
\varphi(x)-\overline{u}_{l}\in L^{p}(({-}\infty,0])
,
then

0\leq\int_{x_{0}^{-}}^{y^{+}(t,0)}(\varphi(\xi)-\overline{u}_{l})\,{\rm d}\xi\leq O(1)\,t^{-\frac{1}{p+1+\alpha_{l}}}\big(x_{0}^{-}-y^{+}(t,0)\big),

so that we can assume that, for some
\sigma_{l}>0
,

(7.82)

\int_{x_{0}^{-}}^{x}(\varphi(\xi)-\overline{u}_{l})\,{\rm d}\xi\geq O(1)\,|x-x_{0}^{-}|^{1+\sigma_{l}}\qquad{\rm as}\ x\rightarrow x_{0}^{-}{-}.

From
(
7.76
)
,
w(x,t)=u(x,t)
for the case of
\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{UD}
.
We now give the
L^{p}_{{\rm loc}}
–norm decay of entropy solutions
to the generalized
N
–waves for the cases of
\mathcal{SD}
and
\mathcal{DS}
.

Lemma 7.8

(Generalized
N
–waves as asymptotic profiles in the
L^{p}_{\rm loc}
–norm)
.

Suppose that
\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
.
Let
u=u(x,t)
be the entropy solution of the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)

with initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying that

\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}\in L^{p}([0,\infty))

and
\varphi(x)-\overline{u}_{l}\in L^{p}(({-}\infty,0])
for
p\in[1,\infty)
,
and let the generalized
N
–wave
w=w(x,t)
be given by
Definition

7.7
.
Then
u(x,t)\equiv w(x,t)
on
\mathcal{K}
,
and the following statements hold
:

(i)

If there exists
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
as in
(
6.31
)
,
then, for any
q>0
,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.83)

\|f^{\prime}(u(\cdot,t))-f^{\prime}(w(\cdot,t))\|_{L^{q}([e_{n}(t),\,h_{n}(t)])}\lessapprox\Big(\frac{h_{n}-e_{n}}{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}\,\varepsilon_{n,q}(t)\,t^{-1}.

Furthermore, if
(
7.80
)
holds for some
\sigma_{n}>0
, then,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.84)

\|f^{\prime}(u(\cdot,t))-f^{\prime}(w(\cdot,t))\|_{L^{q}([e_{n}(t),\,h_{n}(t)])}\lesssim t^{-1-\frac{1}{\sigma_{n}(1+\alpha_{n})}}.

(ii)

If
x_{0}^{+}=\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}<\infty
,
for any forward generalized characteristic
x_{+}(t)
in
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
as in
(
6.32
)
and for any
q>\frac{p}{p+1+\alpha_{r}}
,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.85)

\|f^{\prime}(u(\cdot,t))-f^{\prime}(w(\cdot,t))\|_{L^{q}([x_{0}^{+}(t),\,x_{+}(t)])}\lesssim\varepsilon_{r}(t)\,t^{-1+\frac{p/q}{p+1+\alpha_{r}}}.

Furthermore, if
(
7.81
)
holds for some
\sigma_{r}>0
, then, for any
q>\frac{p}{p+1+\alpha_{r}}
,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.86)

\|f^{\prime}(u(\cdot,t))-f^{\prime}(w(\cdot,t))\|_{L^{q}([x_{0}^{+}(t),\,x_{+}(t)])}\lesssim t^{-1+\frac{p/q}{p+1+\alpha_{r}}-\frac{1/\sigma_{r}}{p+1+\alpha_{r}}}.

(iii)

If
x_{0}^{-}=\inf\mathcal{K}_{0}>-\infty
,
for any forward generalized characteristic
x_{-}(t)
in
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}

as in
(
6.33
)
and for any
q>\frac{p}{p+1+\alpha_{l}}
,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.87)

\|f^{\prime}(u(\cdot,t))-f^{\prime}(w(\cdot,t))\|_{L^{q}([x_{-}(t),\,x_{0}^{-}(t)])}\lesssim\varepsilon_{l}(t)\,t^{-1+\frac{p/q}{p+1+\alpha_{l}}}.

Furthermore, if
(
7.82
)
holds for some
\sigma_{l}>0
, then, for any
q>\frac{p}{p+1+\alpha_{l}}
,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.88)

\|f^{\prime}(u(\cdot,t))-f^{\prime}(w(\cdot,t))\|_{L^{q}([x_{-}(t),\,x_{0}^{-}(t)])}\lesssim t^{-1+\frac{p/q}{p+1+\alpha_{l}}-\frac{1/\sigma_{l}}{p+1+\alpha_{l}}}.

In the above,

\overline{u}_{r}
,
\underline{u}_{r}
,
\overline{u}_{l}
, and
\underline{u}_{l}

are given by
(
6.1
)
;

\mathcal{K}_{0}
is given by
(
6.11
)
;

\alpha_{n}
,
\alpha_{r}
, and
\alpha_{l}
are given by
(
A.4
)

with
c=c_{n}
,
c=\underline{u}_{r}
, and
c=\overline{u}_{l}
, respectively
;

and

\varepsilon_{n,q}(t):=\big((\varepsilon^{-}_{n}(t))^{q}+(\varepsilon^{+}_{n}(t))^{q}\big)^{\frac{1}{q}}
with

\varepsilon^{-}_{n}(t):=y^{-}_{n}(t,0)-e_{n}
and
\varepsilon^{+}_{n}(t):=h_{n}-y^{+}_{n}(t,0)
,

\varepsilon_{r}(t):=y^{-}_{r}(t,0)-x_{0}^{+}
, and
\varepsilon_{l}(t):=x_{0}^{-}-y^{+}_{l}(t,0)
,
for
y^{\pm}_{n}(t,0)
,
y^{-}_{r}(t,0)
, and
y^{+}_{l}(t,0)
given by
(
6.36
)
with
the forward generalized characteristics
x_{n}(t)
in
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
,

x_{+}(t)
in
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
,
and
x_{-}(t)
in
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
, respectively.

Proof.

Denote
y(x,t):=x-tf^{\prime}(u(x,t))
for any
(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
From
(
7.41
)
and
(
7.72
)
,

u(x,t)\equiv w(x,t)
on
\mathcal{K}
.
The remaining proof is divided into three steps accordingly.

1
. By Lemma
6.11
,
for any forward generalized characteristic
x_{n}(t)
in
\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

\varepsilon^{-}_{n}(t)=y^{-}_{n}(t,0)-e_{n}\ \longrightarrow 0,\qquad\varepsilon^{+}_{n}(t)=h_{n}-y^{+}_{n}(t,0)\ \longrightarrow 0.

From Lemma
2.8
,
by
(
7.73
)
,
for any
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
with
e_{n}(t)<x<x_{n}(t)
,

|f^{\prime}(u(x,t))-f^{\prime}(w(x,t))|=\Big|\frac{y(x,t)-e_{n}}{t}\Big|\leq\Big|\frac{y^{-}_{n}(t,0)-e_{n}}{t}\Big|=\frac{\varepsilon^{-}_{n}(t)}{t};

and, for any
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},h_{n})}
with
x_{n}(t)<x<h_{n}(t)
,

|f^{\prime}(u(x,t))-f^{\prime}(w(x,t))|=\Big|\frac{y(x,t)-h_{n}}{t}\Big|\leq\Big|\frac{y^{+}_{n}(t,0)-h_{n}}{t}\Big|=\frac{\varepsilon^{+}_{n}(t)}{t}.

From
(
7.9
)
,
x_{n}(t)-tf^{\prime}(c_{n})=\frac{h_{n}+e_{n}}{2}+o(1)
as
t\rightarrow\infty
.
Then

\displaystyle\ \|f^{\prime}(u(\cdot,t))-f^{\prime}(w(\cdot,t))\|^{q}_{L^{q}([e_{n}(t),h_{n}(t)])}

\displaystyle=\int_{e_{n}(t)}^{x_{n}(t)}|f^{\prime}(u(x,t))-f^{\prime}(w(x,t))|^{q}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{x_{n}(t)}^{h_{n}(t)}|f^{\prime}(u(x,t))-f^{\prime}(w(x,t))|^{q}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{\varepsilon^{-}_{n}(t)}{t}\Big)^{q}\,\Big(\frac{h_{n}-e_{n}}{2}+o(1)\Big)+\Big(\frac{\varepsilon^{+}_{n}(t)}{t}\Big)^{q}\,\Big(\frac{h_{n}-e_{n}}{2}+o(1)\Big)

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\frac{h_{n}-e_{n}}{2}\big((\varepsilon^{-}_{n}(t))^{q}+(\varepsilon^{+}_{n}(t))^{q}\big)\,t^{-q}

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\frac{h_{n}-e_{n}}{2}(\varepsilon_{n,q}(t))^{q}\,t^{-q},

which yields
(
7.83
)
.

Furthermore, if
(
7.80
)
holds for some
\sigma_{n}>0
,
it follows from
(
6.42
)
and
(
7.79
)
that

(7.89)

\displaystyle O(1)(\varepsilon^{-}_{n}(t))^{1+\sigma_{n}}\leq\int_{e_{n}}^{y^{-}_{n}(t,0)}(\varphi(\xi)-c_{n})\,{\rm d}\xi\leq\big(v^{-}(t)-c_{n}\big)\,\varepsilon^{-}_{n}(t)\leq O(1)\,t^{-\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{n}}}\,\varepsilon^{-}_{n}(t),

which implies

0\leq\varepsilon^{-}_{n}(t)=y^{-}_{n}(t,0)-e_{n}\leq O(1)\,t^{-\frac{1}{\sigma_{n}(1+\alpha_{n})}}.

Similarly, we have

0\leq\varepsilon^{+}_{n}(t)=h_{n}-y^{+}_{n}(t,0)\leq O(1)\,t^{-\frac{1}{\sigma_{n}(1+\alpha_{n})}}.

Therefore,
(
7.84
)
follows from
(
7.83
)
.

2
. Suppose
x_{0}^{+}=\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}<\infty
.
By Lemma
6.11
, for any forward generalized characteristic
x_{+}(t)
in
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
,

\varepsilon_{r}(t)=y^{-}_{r}(t,0)-x_{0}^{+}\ \rightarrow 0\qquad\,\mbox{as $t\rightarrow\infty$}.

From Lemma
2.8
, by
(
7.74
)
,
for any
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
with
x_{0}^{+}(t)<x<x_{+}(t)
,

|f^{\prime}(u(x,t))-f^{\prime}(w(x,t))|=\Big|\frac{y(x,t)-x_{0}^{+}}{t}\Big|\leq\Big|\frac{y^{-}_{r}(t,0)-x_{0}^{+}}{t}\Big|=\frac{\varepsilon_{r}(t)}{t}.

Since
\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}\in L^{p}([0,\infty))
for
p\in[1,\infty)
,
then, from
(
7.61
)
and
(
A.5
)
,

0<x_{+}(t)-x_{0}^{+}(t)\leq y^{-}_{r}(t,0)-x_{0}^{+}+t\big|f^{\prime}(u^{-}(t))-f^{\prime}(\underline{u}_{r})\big|\leq O(1)\,t^{\frac{p}{p+1+\alpha_{r}}},

so that,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\|f^{\prime}(u(\cdot,t))-f^{\prime}(w(\cdot,t))\|_{L^{q}([x_{0}^{+}(t),x_{+}(t)])}

\displaystyle=\Big(\int_{x_{0}^{+}(t)}^{x_{+}(t)}|f^{\prime}(u(x,t))-f^{\prime}(w(x,t))|^{q}\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{r}(t)}{t}\,\big(x_{+}(t)-x_{0}^{+}(t)\big)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq O(1)\,\varepsilon_{r}(t)\,t^{-1+\frac{p/q}{p+1+\alpha_{r}}},

which yields
(
7.85
)
.
Furthermore, if
(
7.81
)
holds for some
\sigma_{r}>0
,
similar to
(
7.89
)
,

\varepsilon_{r}(t)=y^{-}_{r}(t,0)-x_{0}^{+}\leq O(1)\,t^{-\frac{1/\sigma_{r}}{p+1+\alpha_{r}}},

which, by taking into
(
7.85
)
, implies
(
7.86
)
.

3
.
Suppose
x_{0}^{-}=\inf\mathcal{K}_{0}>-\infty
.
By Lemma
6.11
,
for any forward generalized characteristic
x_{-}(t)
in
\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
,

(7.90)

\varepsilon_{l}(t)=x_{0}^{-}-y^{+}_{l}(t,0)\ \rightarrow 0\qquad\,\mbox{as $t\rightarrow\infty$}.

From Lemma
2.8
, by
(
7.75
)
,
for any
(x,t)\in\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}
with
x_{-}(t)<x<x_{0}^{-}(t)
,

(7.91)

|f^{\prime}(u(x,t))-f^{\prime}(w(x,t))|=\Big|\frac{y(x,t)-x_{0}^{-}}{t}\Big|\leq\Big|\frac{y^{+}_{l}(t,0)-x_{0}^{-}}{t}\Big|=\frac{\varepsilon_{l}(t)}{t}.

Since
\varphi(x)-\overline{u}_{l}\in L^{p}(({-}\infty,0])
for
p\in[1,\infty)
,
from
(
7.62
)
,
(
7.90
)
, and
(
A.5
)
,

0<x_{0}^{-}(t)-x_{-}(t)\leq x_{0}^{-}-y^{+}_{l}(t,0)+t\big|f^{\prime}(u^{+}(t))-f^{\prime}(\overline{u}_{l})\big|\leq O(1)\,t^{\frac{p}{p+1+\alpha_{l}}},

so that, by
(
7.91
)
, as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\|f^{\prime}(u(\cdot,t))-f^{\prime}(w(\cdot,t))\|_{L^{q}([x_{-}(t),x_{0}^{-}(t)])}

\displaystyle=\Big(\int_{x_{-}(t)}^{x_{0}^{-}(t)}|f^{\prime}(u(x,t))-f^{\prime}(w(x,t))|^{q}\,{\rm d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{l}(t)}{t}\,\big(x_{0}^{-}(t)-x_{-}(t)\big)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq O(1)\,\varepsilon_{l}(t)\,t^{-1+\frac{p/q}{p+1+\alpha_{l}}},

which yields
(
7.87
)
.
Furthermore, if
(
7.82
)
holds for some
\sigma_{r}>0
,
similar to
(
7.89
)
,

\varepsilon_{l}(t)=x_{0}^{-}-y^{+}_{l}(t,0)\leq O(1)\,t^{-\frac{1/\sigma_{l}}{p+1+\alpha_{l}}},

which, by taking into
(
7.87
)
, implies
(
7.88
)
.
∎

Theorem 7.9

(Decay rates in the
L^{p}_{\rm loc}
–norm)
.

Suppose that

\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
,
or
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
.
Let
u=u(x,t)
be the entropy solution of the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)

with initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying that

\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}\in L^{p}([0,\infty))

and
\varphi(x)-\overline{u}_{l}\in L^{p}(({-}\infty,0])
for
p\in[1,\infty)
,
and let the generalized
N
–wave

w=w(x,t)
be given by
Definition

7.7
.
Then the following statements hold
:

(i)

For any two divides
e(t)
and
h(t)
with
e(t)<h(t)
and for any
q>0
,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.92)

\|f^{\prime}(u(\cdot,t))-f^{\prime}(w(\cdot,t))\|_{L^{q}([e(t),\,h(t)])}\leq o(1)\,t^{-1}.

(ii)

If
\inf\mathcal{K}_{0}>-\infty
and/or
\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}<\infty
,
for any two forward generalized characteristics
x_{1}(t)\in\mathcal{H}_{-\infty}

and/or
x_{2}(t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}
with
x_{1}(t)<x_{2}(t)

and for any
q>\frac{p}{p+1+\alpha}
,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.93)

\|f^{\prime}(u(\cdot,t))-f^{\prime}(w(\cdot,t))\|_{L^{q}([x_{1}(t),\,x_{2}(t)])}\leq o(1)\,t^{-1+\frac{p/q}{p+1+\alpha}},

In particular, if
x_{0}^{+}=\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}<\infty

with
{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}^{+})<\underline{{\rm D}}_{+}\Phi(x_{0}^{+})

and/or
x_{0}^{-}=\inf\mathcal{K}_{0}>-\infty

with
\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(x_{0}^{-})<{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}^{-})
,
then, for any
q>0
,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.94)

\|f^{\prime}(u(\cdot,t))-f^{\prime}(w(\cdot,t))\|_{L^{q}([x_{1}(t),\,x_{2}(t)])}\leq o(1)\,t^{-1}.

In the above,

\overline{u}_{r}
,
\underline{u}_{r}
,
\overline{u}_{l}
, and
\underline{u}_{l}

are given by
(
6.1
)
;

\mathcal{K}_{0}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\pm\infty}

are given by
(
6.11
)
and
(
6.32
)
–
(
6.33
)
, respectively
;

\underline{{\rm D}}_{+}\Phi(x)
and
\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(x)
,
and
{\rm D}_{\pm}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})

are given by
(
3.4
)
and
(
6.16
)
, respectively
;

and
\alpha
is given by

\alpha=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\alpha_{l}&{\rm if}\ x_{1}(t)\in\mathcal{H}_{-\infty},&x_{2}(t)\in\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},\,h_{n})},\\[2.84526pt]
\alpha_{r}&{\rm if}\ x_{1}(t)\in\mathcal{H}_{(e_{n},\,h_{n})},&x_{2}(t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty},\\[2.84526pt]
\min\{\alpha_{l},\alpha_{r}\}&{\rm if}\ x_{1}(t)\in\mathcal{H}_{-\infty},&x_{2}(t)\in\mathcal{H}_{\infty}\end{array}\right.

for
\alpha_{r}
and
\alpha_{l}
given by
(
A.4
)
with
c=\underline{u}_{r}
and
c=\overline{u}_{l}
, respectively.
See also
Fig.

2.7
.

Proof.

It is direct to see that
(
7.92
)
follows from
(
7.83
)
and
u(x,t)=w(x,t)
on
\mathcal{K}
in
(
7.76
)
.

Furthermore,

(
7.93
)
follows by combining
(
7.83
)
and
(
7.85
)
with
(
7.87
)
.

By Remark
7.3
, if
x_{0}^{+}=\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}<\infty

with
{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}^{+})<\underline{{\rm D}}_{+}\Phi(x_{0}^{+})
,
there exists sufficiently large
t_{+}>0
such that

u(x,t)=w(x,t)
for
x_{0}^{+}(t)<x<x_{2}(t)
with
t>t_{+}
.
If
x_{0}^{-}=\inf\mathcal{K}_{0}>-\infty

with
\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(x_{0}^{-})<{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}^{-})
,
there exists sufficiently large
t_{-}>0
such that

u(x,t)=w(x,t)
for
x_{1}(t)<x<x_{0}^{-}(t)
with
t>t_{-}.

Therefore, it follows from
(
7.92
)
that
(
7.94
)
holds.
∎

For the initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying that

(7.95)

\varphi(x)\equiv u_{l}\quad{\rm if}\ x<s_{-},\qquad\,\,\varphi(x)\equiv u_{r}\quad{\rm if}\ x>s_{+}

with
s_{-}<s_{+}
, we have

\Phi(x)-\Phi(s_{-})\equiv u_{l}(x-s_{-})\quad{\rm if}\ x\leq s_{-},\qquad\,\,\Phi(x)-\Phi(s_{+})\equiv u_{r}(x-s_{+})\quad{\rm if}\ x\geq s_{+}.

Thus, it follows from Lemmas
6.6
–
6.7
that,

\bar{\Phi}(x)>-\infty
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
if
u_{l}\leq u_{r}
,
and
\bar{\Phi}(x)=-\infty
with
\mathcal{K}_{0}=\varnothing
if
u_{l}>u_{r}
.

Furthermore, from Lemma
6.8
, if
u_{l}\leq u_{r}
,

(7.96)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}({-}\infty,s_{-}]\subset\mathcal{K}_{0}&{\rm if}\ s_{-}\in\mathcal{K}_{0},&({-}\infty,s_{-}]\cap\mathcal{K}_{0}=\varnothing&{\rm if}\ s_{-}\notin\mathcal{K}_{0},\\[5.69054pt]
[s_{+},\infty)\subset\mathcal{K}_{0}&{\rm if}\ s_{+}\in\mathcal{K}_{0},&[s_{+},\infty)\cap\mathcal{K}_{0}=\varnothing&{\rm if}\ s_{+}\notin\mathcal{K}_{0}.\end{array}\right.

Corollary 7.10

(Decay in the
L^{p}
–norm for some special initial data)
.

Let
u=u(x,t)
be the entropy solution of the Cauchy problem
(
1.1
)
–
(
1.2
)

for the initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying

(
7.95
)
with
u_{l}\leq u_{r}
.

(i)

If
s_{+}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
and
s_{-}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
,
then, for any
q>0
,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.97)

\|f^{\prime}(u(\cdot,t))-f^{\prime}(w(\cdot,t))\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R})}\leq o(1)\,t^{-1}.

(ii)

If
s_{-}\notin\mathcal{K}_{0}
and/or
s_{+}\notin\mathcal{K}_{0}
,
for any chosen forward generalized characteristics
x_{\pm}(t)
emitting from
s_{\pm}
,
define

\bar{w}(x,t):=\begin{cases}u_{l}\quad&{\rm if}\ x<x_{-}(t),\\
w(x,t)\quad&{\rm if}\ x_{-}(t)<x<x_{+}(t),\\
u_{r}\quad&{\rm if}\ x>x_{+}(t).\end{cases}

Then, for any
q>\frac{p}{p+1+\alpha}
,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.98)

\|f^{\prime}(u(\cdot,t))-f^{\prime}(\bar{w}(\cdot,t))\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R})}\leq o(1)\,t^{-1+\frac{p/q}{p+1+\alpha}}.

In particular, if
s_{+}\notin\mathcal{K}_{0}

with
{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}^{+})<\underline{{\rm D}}_{+}\Phi(x_{0}^{+})

and/or
s_{-}\notin\mathcal{K}_{0}

with
\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(x_{0}^{-})<{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}^{-})
,
then, for any
q>0
,
as
t\rightarrow\infty
,

(7.99)

\|f^{\prime}(u(\cdot,t))-f^{\prime}(\bar{w}(\cdot,t))\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R})}\leq o(1)\,t^{-1}.

In the above,

\mathcal{K}_{0}
is given by
(
6.11
)
;

\underline{{\rm D}}_{+}\Phi(x)
and
\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(x)
,
and
{\rm D}_{\pm}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})

are given by
(
3.4
)
and
(
6.16
)
, respectively
;

and
\alpha
is given by

\alpha=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\alpha_{l}&{\rm if}\ s_{-}\notin\mathcal{K}_{0},&s_{+}\in\mathcal{K}_{0},\\
\alpha_{r}&{\rm if}\ s_{-}\in\mathcal{K}_{0},&s_{+}\notin\mathcal{K}_{0},\\
\min\{\alpha_{l},\alpha_{r}\}&{\rm if}\ s_{-}\notin\mathcal{K}_{0},&s_{+}\notin\mathcal{K}_{0}\end{array}\right.

for
\alpha_{r}
and
\alpha_{l}
given by
(
A.4
)
with
c=\underline{u}_{r}
and
c=\overline{u}_{l}
, respectively.

Proof.

If
s_{+}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
and
s_{-}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
,
by
(
7.96
)
,
u(x,t)=w(x,t)\equiv u_{r}
on
\{x>s_{+}+tf^{\prime}(u_{r})\}\subset\mathcal{K}
and

u(x,t)=w(x,t)\equiv u_{l}
on
\{x<s_{-}+tf^{\prime}(u_{l})\}\subset\mathcal{K}
so that

(
7.97
)
follows by
(
7.92
)
.

If
s_{+}\notin\mathcal{K}_{0}
, from
(
7.96
)
, we see that
x_{0}^{+}<s_{+}
so that,
by Lemma
6.11
,

y^{+}(t,0)=x_{+}(t)-tf^{\prime}(u(x_{+}(t){+},t))\ \rightarrow\infty\qquad\,\,{\rm as}\ t\rightarrow\infty,

which means that there exists sufficiently large
t_{+}
such that
y^{+}(t,0)>s_{+}
for
t>t_{+}
.
From
(
7.95
)
, by Lemmas
2.8
–
2.9
,

u(x,t)\equiv u_{r}
for
x>x_{+}(t)
with
t>t_{+}.

Similarly, if
s_{-}\notin\mathcal{K}_{0}
, then

u(x,t)\equiv u_{l}
for
x<x_{-}(t)
with
t>t_{-}.

Thus, we obtain that, when
t>\max\{t_{+},t_{-}\}
,

u(x,t)=\bar{w}(x,t)
for
x>x_{+}(t)\ {\rm or}\ x<x_{-}(t),

which, by combining with
(
7.93
)
, yields
(
7.98
)
.

Finally, it follows from
(
7.94
)
that
(
7.99
)
holds.
∎

Remark 7.4
.

The generalized
N
–waves provide a
good approximation of entropy solutions
which possess at least one divide.
In detail,
the generalized
N
–wave
w(x,t)
exactly equals to the entropy solution
u(x,t)
for large time
in five of the seven cases in Remark
7.3
.
For the remaining two cases,
according to Lemma
7.8
and Theorem
7.9
,

the entropy solutions can decay to generalized
N
–waves with rates

(
even far more
)
greater than
t^{-1}
.
In particular, from Remark
7.3
,
for the period initial data function satisfying that
there emits a rarefaction wave initially at any point of
\mathcal{K}_{0}
,
the generalized
N
–wave exactly equals to the entropy solution for large enough time.
As an example, considering the Burgers’ equation
u_{t}+(\frac{1}{2}u^{2})_{x}=0

with the initial data function
\varphi(x)=(|\sin x|)^{\prime}
,
it is direct to see that, when
t>\frac{\pi}{2}
,
the entropy solution equals to the generalized
N
–wave,
for which the forward generalized characteristics
x_{n}(t)
are chosen to emit from points
x_{0}=n\pi
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
with the same initial speed
1
.

8.
Extension of the New Formula
to More General
Hyperbolic Conservation Laws

The new formula for entropy solutions can be generalized to the Cauchy problem
for more general scalar hyperbolic conservation law:

(8.1)

\displaystyle U(u)_{t}+F(u)_{x}=0\qquad\,\,{\rm for}\ (x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+},

(8.2)

\displaystyle u|_{t=0}=\varphi(x),

where the flux pair
(U(u),F(u))\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
satisfies

(8.3)

U^{\prime}(u)>0\,\,\,\,\,{\rm on}\ u\in\mathbb{R},\qquad\,\,H(u):=\frac{F^{\prime}(u)}{U^{\prime}(u)}\,\,{\rm is\ strictly\ increasing},

and the initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L_{{\rm loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
satisfies

(8.4)

\mathop{\overline{\lim}}\limits_{s\rightarrow\pm\infty}\frac{\varphi(x-tH(s))}{s}<1\qquad{\rm locally\ uniform\ in\ }(x,t).

Condition (
8.3
) for
H(u)
is equivalent to

(8.5)

H^{\prime}(u)\geq 0\,\,\,\,\mbox{on $u\in\mathbb{R}$},\qquad\,\,\,\mathcal{L}\{u\,:\,H^{\prime}(u)=0\}=0,

where
\mathcal{L}
is the Lebesgue measure, which means that
H(u)
is not uniformly increasing.

The new formula for entropy solutions of the Cauchy problem (
8.1
)–(
8.2
)
can be introduced as follows:
For any fixed
(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
,

(8.6)

E(u;x,t)=t\int_{0}^{u}H^{\prime}(s)\big(U(\varphi(x-tH(s)))-U(s)\big)\,{\rm d}s.

From
(
8.4
)
, for any
(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
,
there exist
s^{\pm}(x,t)
such that

\varphi(x-tH(u))<u\quad{\rm if}\ u>s^{+}(x,t),\qquad\,\,\,\varphi(x-tH(u))>u\quad{\rm if}\ u<s^{-}(x,t).

This, by
(
8.3
)
, implies that, if
u>s^{+}(x,t)
, then

H^{\prime}(u)\big(U(\varphi(x-tH(u)))-U(u)\big)\leq 0

so that
E(\cdot\,;x,t)
is strictly decreasing for
u>s^{+}(x,t)
;
and if
u<s^{-}(x,t)
, then

H^{\prime}(u)\big(U(\varphi(x-tH(u)))-U(u)\big)\geq 0

so that
E(\cdot\,;x,t)
is strictly increasing for
u<s^{-}(x,t)
.
This means that
E(\cdot\,;x,t)
attains its maximum on
[s^{-}(x,t),s^{+}(x,t)]
.

Let
\mathcal{U}(x,t)
be the set of points
at which
E(u;x,t)
attains its maximum in
u\in\mathbb{R}
,
i.e.
,

(8.7)

\mathcal{U}(x,t)=\big\{w\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,E(w;x,t)=\max\limits_{v\in\mathbb{R}}E(v;x,t)\big\}.

The definition of
u^{\pm}(x,t)
is given by

(8.8)

u^{-}(x,t)=\sup\,\mathcal{U}(x,t),\qquad u^{+}(x,t)=\inf\,\mathcal{U}(x,t).

Similar to
(
2.7
)
, for any
(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
, we define

(8.9)

\hat{E}(x,t):=\max_{v\in\mathbb{R}}E(v;x,t)-\int_{0}^{x-tH(0)}U(\varphi(\xi))\,{\rm d}\xi.

Similar to
(
2.15
)
–
(
2.17
)
,
for any fixed
\tau>0
, consider the following the Cauchy problem:

(8.10)

\begin{cases}U(v)_{t}+F(v)_{x}=0\qquad&{\rm for}\ x\in\mathbb{R},\ t>\tau,\\[5.69054pt]
v(x,t)|_{t=\tau}=u(x,\tau)\qquad&{\rm for}\ x\in\mathbb{R},\ t=\tau,\end{cases}

where
u(x,\tau)
is obtained by restricting solution
u(x,t)

of the Cauchy problem (
8.1
)–(
8.2
) to the line:
t=\tau
.
Similar to
(
8.6
)
–
(
8.9
)
,
we define that, for any
(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
with
t>\tau
,

(8.11)

E(v;x,t;\tau):=(t-\tau)\int_{0}^{v}H^{\prime}(s)\big(U(u(x-(t-\tau)H(s),\tau))-U(s)\big)\,{\rm d}s,

and

(8.12)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathcal{U}(x,t;\tau):=\big\{v\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,E(v;x,t;\tau)=\max_{w\in\mathbb{R}}E(w;x,t;\tau)\big\},\\[8.53581pt]
\displaystyle v^{-}(x,t):=\sup\,\mathcal{U}(x,t;\tau),\qquad v^{+}(x,t):=\inf\,\mathcal{U}(x,t;\tau),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\hat{E}(x,t;\tau):=\max_{w\in\mathbb{R}}E(w;x,t;\tau)+\hat{E}(x-(t-\tau)H(0),\tau).\end{cases}

With these, we can obtain the main theorem for the Cauchy problem (
8.1
)–(
8.2
)
similar to Theorem
2.4
and Corollaries
2.5
–
2.6
.
From
(
8.4
)
, the intervals:

[s^{-}(x,t),s^{+}(x,t)]
are contained in the same bounded interval locally in
(x,t)
.
Then
u^{\pm}(x,t)
defined by
(
8.8
)
are locally uniformly bounded,
which, instead of
(
2.25
)
, can be used to prove
the local properties similar to Lemmas
2.7
–
2.9
as follows:

(i)

For any
t>0
and
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\int^{x_{2}}_{x_{1}}U(u^{\pm}(x,t))\,{\rm d}x=\hat{E}(x_{1},t)-\hat{E}(x_{2},t).

(ii)

For any
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
t_{2}>t_{1}>0
,

\int^{t_{2}}_{t_{1}}F(u^{\pm}(x,t))\,{\rm d}t=\hat{E}(x,t_{2})-\hat{E}(x,t_{1}).

(iii)

For any
t>0
and
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
with
x<x^{\prime}
,

x-tH(u^{+}(x,t))\leq x^{\prime}-tH(u^{-}(x^{\prime},t)).

(iv)

Lemma
2.9
holds via replacing
f^{\prime}(c)
by
H(c)
in
(
2.43
)
.

By the same arguments as the proofs of Theorem
2.4
and Corollaries
2.5
–
2.6
,
these local properties of
u^{\pm}(x,t)
and
\hat{E}(x,t)
validate
the following main theorem for the Cauchy problem (
8.1
)–(
8.2
).

Theorem 8.1

(Formula for entropy solutions of the Cauchy problem
(
8.1
)
–
(
8.2
)
)
.

Suppose that the flux pair
(U(u),F(u))
satisfies
(
8.3
)

and the initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L_{{\rm loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R})
satisfies
(
8.4
)
.
Let
u=u(x,t)
on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
be defined by

(8.13)

u(x,t):=u^{+}(x,t)=\inf\big\{w\in\mathbb{R}\,:\,E(w;x,t)=\max_{v\in\mathbb{R}}E(v;x,t)\big\}

with
E(u;x,t)
given by
(
8.6
)
.
Then
u=u(x,t)
,
defined by the solution formula
(
8.13
)
,
is the unique entropy solution of the Cauchy problem
(
8.1
)
–
(
8.2
)
in the sense that

H(u(x_{2},t))-H(u(x_{1},t))\leq\frac{x_{2}-x_{1}}{t}\qquad{\rm for\ any\ }x_{2}>x_{1}\ {\rm and}\ t>0.

Furthermore, the entropy solution
u=u(x,t)
possesses both the left- and right-traces pointwise
and satisfies

u(x{-},t)=u^{-}(x,t),\qquad u(x{+},t)=u^{+}(x,t)\qquad\,{\rm on}\ (x,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{+};

and, for each
t>0
,
u(x,t)=u(x{-},t)=u(x{+},t)
holds except
at most a countable set of
(x,t)
at which
u(x{-},t)>u(x{+},t)
and satisfies

H(u(\cdot,t))\in{\rm BV}_{{\rm loc}}(\mathbb{R})\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ t>0.

Moreover, the results in
Corollary

2.5
still hold
if
\varphi_{i}(x)
,
u_{i}(x,t)
, and
f^{\prime}(u)
in
(
2.11
)

are replaced by
U(\varphi_{i}(x))
,
U(u_{i}(x,t))
, and
H(u)
, respectively.
Also, the results in
Corollary

2.6
still hold.

To elucidate the fine properties of entropy solutions of the Cauchy problem (
8.1
)–(
8.2
) as in §3–§7,
we restrict to the case that
the initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
.
For this purpose,
we present the following adjustments to the corresponding symbols and notions:

(a)

Replace
f^{\prime}(u)
and
f^{\prime\prime}(u)
by
H(u)
and
H^{\prime}(u)
, respectively.

(b)

Replace all the integrals of
\varphi(x)
and
u(x,t)
by
the corresponding integrals of
U(\varphi(x))
and
U(u(x,t))
,
especially for replacing
\Phi(x)=\int^{x}_{0}\varphi(\xi)\,{\rm d}\xi

by
\Phi(x)=\int^{x}_{0}U(\varphi(\xi))\,{\rm d}\xi

along with its Dini derivatives and convex hull
\bar{\Phi}(x)
over
(-\infty,\infty)
.

(c)

Replace
\Phi(l;x_{0},c)
and
F(l;t,c)
defined in
(
3.6
)
by

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\ \Phi(l;x_{0},c)=\!\int^{x_{0}+l}_{x_{0}}(U(\varphi(\xi))-U(c))\,{\rm d}\xi,\quad F(l;t,c)={-}t\!\int^{u(l;t,c)}_{c}(U(s)-U(c))H^{\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s.

(d)

The four invariants

\overline{u}_{l}
,
\underline{u}_{l}
,
\overline{u}_{r}
, and
\underline{u}_{r}
,
defined by
(
6.1
)
, are replaced by

\qquad\quad\begin{cases}U(\overline{u}_{l}):=\displaystyle\mathop{\overline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow-\infty}\frac{1}{x}\int^{x}_{0}U(\varphi(\xi))\,{\rm d}\xi,\quad&U(\underline{u}_{l}):=\displaystyle\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow-\infty}\frac{1}{x}\int^{x}_{0}U(\varphi(\xi))\,{\rm d}\xi,\\[11.38109pt]
U(\overline{u}_{r}):=\displaystyle\mathop{\overline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{x}\int^{x}_{0}U(\varphi(\xi))\,{\rm d}\xi,\quad&U(\underline{u}_{r}):=\displaystyle\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{x}\int^{x}_{0}U(\varphi(\xi))\,{\rm d}\xi.\end{cases}

(e)

Similar to
(
7.1
)
,
let
\tilde{u}=\tilde{u}(x,t)
be the rarefaction-constant solution
of the Cauchy problem for (
8.1
) with the initial data function
\bar{\Phi}^{\prime}(x)
.

By the same arguments as §3–§7,
these adjustments can validate the following main theorem
on the fine properties of entropy solutions of the Cauchy problem (
8.1
)–(
8.2
).

Theorem 8.2

(Fine properties for entropy solutions of the Cauchy problem
(
8.1
)
–
(
8.2
)
)
.

Suppose that the flux pair
(U(u),F(u))
satisfies
(
8.3
)

and the initial data function
\varphi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
.
Then the unique entropy solution
u=u(x,t)
,
defined by
(
8.13
)
,
of the Cauchy problem (
8.1
)–(
8.2
) possesses the following fine properties
:

(i)

The results in
Theorems

5.3
–
5.4

with
Proposition

5.1
and
Lemma

5.2
still hold
;

and the results in
Theorem

5.5
still hold if
the left- and right-derivatives of shock curves as in
(
5.30
)
–
(
5.31
)
are replaced by

\qquad\quad\frac{{\rm d}^{+}x(t)}{{\rm d}t}=\frac{F(u(x(t){-},t))-F(u(x(t){+},t))}{U(u(x(t){-},t))-U(u(x(t){+},t))},\qquad\frac{{\rm d}^{-}x(t)}{{\rm d}t}=\frac{F(d_{n})-F(c_{n})}{U(d_{n})-U(c_{n})}.

(ii)

The results in
Lemma

3.2
still hold
;

the results in
Propositions

3.3
–
3.7
still hold
if
(
3.11
)
is replaced by

\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(x_{0})\leq U(c)\leq\underline{{\rm D}}_{+}\Phi(x_{0})

and

(
3.12
)
is replaced by

(\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(x_{0}),\underline{{\rm D}}_{+}\Phi(x_{0}))\subset U(\mathcal{C}(x_{0}))\subset[\overline{{\rm D}}_{-}\Phi(x_{0}),\underline{{\rm D}}_{+}\Phi(x_{0})];

and
the results in
Theorems

3.8
–
3.9
,
5.6
,
and
3.12
–
3.14
with
Lemma

3.11
also hold.

(iii)

The results in
Theorems

4.1

and
4.4
with
Lemma

4.3
still hold.

(iv)

The results in
Lemma

6.1
still hold
if
tc
and
td
in
(
6.2
)
are replaced by

tU(c)
and
tU(d)
, respectively
;

the results in
Theorem

6.2
still hold
if
\overline{u}_{l}
,
\underline{u}_{l}
,
\overline{u}_{r}
, and
\underline{u}_{r}

in
(
6.4
)
are replaced by

U(\overline{u}_{l})
,
U(\underline{u}_{l})
,
U(\overline{u}_{r})
, and
U(\underline{u}_{r})
, respectively
;

and the results in
Propositions

6.4
–
6.5

and
Theorems

6.9
–
6.10
still hold
if
(
6.20
)
is replaced by

\Phi(x_{0})=\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}),\qquad{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})\leq U(c)\leq{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}).

Moreover,
the results in
Lemma

6.11
still hold
if
v^{+}(t)-c
in
(
6.39
)

and
v^{-}(t)-d
in
(
6.42
)
are replaced by

U(v^{+}(t))-U(c)
and
U(v^{-}(t))-U(d)
respectively,
and
(
6.38
)
and
(
6.41
)
are replaced by

U(c)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}U(u(x(t){+},t))={\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(y^{+}(\infty,0))

and

U(d)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}U(u(x(t){-},t))={\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(y^{-}(\infty,0))

respectively
;

and
the results in
Theorem

6.12
also hold.

(v)

The results in
Lemmas

7.1
–
7.2

and
Theorems

7.3
–
7.4
hold
;

the results in
Theorem

7.5
still hold
if

u(x,t)-\overline{u}_{l}
and
u(x,t)-\underline{u}_{r}
are replaced by

U(u(x,t))-U(\overline{u}_{l})
and
U(u(x,t))-U(\underline{u}_{r})
respectively,
and

\varphi(x)-\overline{u}_{l}
and
\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}
are replaced by

U(\varphi(x))-U(\overline{u}_{l})
and
U(\varphi(x))-U(\underline{u}_{r})
respectively
;

and the results in

Lemma

7.8
and
Theorem

7.9
still hold
for the generalized
N
–waves given by
Definition

7.7

if

\varphi(x)-\overline{u}_{l}
and
\varphi(x)-\underline{u}_{r}
are replaced by

U(\varphi(x))-U(\overline{u}_{l})
and
U(\varphi(x))-U(\underline{u}_{r})
respectively.

In addition, for Appendix A,
\rho(u,v)
in
(
A.1
)
becomes

\rho(u,v)=\frac{\int^{u}_{v}U(s)H^{\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s}{\int^{u}_{v}H^{\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s}\quad{\rm if}\ u\neq v,\qquad\rho(v,v)=U(v)\quad{\rm if}\ u=v.

Then the corresponding results similar to
(
A.2
)
–
(
A.3
)
hold.
Furthermore, for any fixed
c\in\mathbb{R}
,
if

H^{\prime}(u)=(N_{\pm}+o(1))\,|u-c|^{\alpha\pm}\qquad{\rm as}\ u\rightarrow c{\pm}

holds for some
\alpha_{\pm}\geq 0
and
N_{\pm}>0
,
then
(
A.5
)
still holds if
f^{\prime}(u)
is replaced by
H(u)
,
and
(
A.6
)
becomes

\int^{u}_{c}\big(U(s)-U(c)\big)H^{\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s=\frac{N_{\pm}U^{\prime}(c)+o(1)}{2+\alpha_{\pm}}\,|u-c|^{2+\alpha_{\pm}}\qquad\,{\rm as}\ u\rightarrow c{\pm};

moreover, for
(
A.7
)
, it is direct to check that, as
u\rightarrow c{\pm}
,

\rho(u,c)-U(c)=(1+o(1))\,\frac{1+\alpha_{\pm}}{2+\alpha_{\pm}}\,(U(u)-U(c)).

For Appendix B,
Lemmas
6.6
–
6.8
still hold if

\overline{u}_{l}
and
\underline{u}_{r}
are replaced by
U(\overline{u}_{l})
and
U(\underline{u}_{r})
.

Appendix A
Properties of the Flux Functions of Convexity Degeneracy

Suppose that the flux function
f(u)
satisfies
(
1.3
)
.
For
(u,v)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}
, we define

(A.1)

\rho(u,v):=\dfrac{\int^{u}_{v}sf^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s}{\int^{u}_{v}f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s}\quad\mbox{if}\ u\neq v,\qquad\rho(u,v):=v\quad\ \mbox{if}\ u=v.

Then

\rho(u,v)=\rho(v,u)\in C(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R})
satisfies

(A.2)

\lim_{u,v\rightarrow w}\rho(u,v)=\rho(w,w)=w\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ w\in\mathbb{R};

and, for any fixed
v\in\mathbb{R}
,

\rho(u,v)=\rho(v,u)
is strictly increasing in
u\in\mathbb{R}

and satisfies

(A.3)

\partial_{1}\rho(u,v)=\partial_{2}\rho(v,u)=f^{\prime\prime}(u)\frac{u-\rho(u,v)}{f^{\prime}(u)-f^{\prime}(v)}\geq 0\qquad{\rm if}\ u\neq v.

Furthermore, for any fixed
c\in\mathbb{R}
, if

(A.4)

f^{\prime\prime}(u)=(N_{\pm}+o(1))\,|u-c|^{\alpha_{\pm}}\qquad{\rm as}\ u\rightarrow c{\pm}

holds for some
\alpha_{\pm}\geq 0
and
N_{\pm}>0
,
then

(i)

As
u\rightarrow c{\pm}
,

(A.5)

|f^{\prime}(u)-f^{\prime}(c)|=\frac{N_{\pm}+o(1)}{1+\alpha_{\pm}}\,|u-c|^{1+\alpha_{\pm}}.

(ii)

As
u\rightarrow c{\pm}
,

(A.6)

\int^{u}_{c}(s-c)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s=\frac{N_{\pm}+o(1)}{2+\alpha_{\pm}}\,|u-c|^{2+\alpha_{\pm}}.

(iii)

As
u\rightarrow c{\pm}
,

(A.7)

\rho(u,c)-c=(1+o(1))\,\frac{1+\alpha_{\pm}}{2+\alpha_{\pm}}\,(u-c).

Proof.

Clearly,
\rho(u,v)=\rho(v,u)
. Since
f^{\prime}(u)
is strictly increasing in
u\in\mathbb{R}
,
then it is direct to see that
\rho(u,v)
is continuous at any point
(u,v)
with
u\neq v
.
Moreover, for any
w\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle|\rho(u,v)-\rho(w,w)|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{\int^{u}_{v}(s-v)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s}{f^{\prime}(u)-f^{\prime}(v)}+v-w\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\frac{(u-v)f^{\prime}(u)-(f(u)-f(v))}{f^{\prime}(u)-f^{\prime}(v)}\bigg|+|v-w|

\displaystyle=\frac{f^{\prime}(u)-f^{\prime}(\xi)}{f^{\prime}(u)-f^{\prime}(v)}|u-v|+|v-w|\leq|u-v|+|v-w|,

where
\xi
lies between
u
and
v
.
Thus,
(
A.2
)
holds so that

\rho(u,v)
is continuous at
(w,w)
for any
w\in\mathbb{R}
.
Therefore,
\rho(u,v)\in C(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R})
.

To prove
(
A.3
)
, fix
v\in\mathbb{R}
. For any
u\neq v
,
it is direct to check that

\partial_{1}\rho(u,v)=\partial_{2}\rho(v,u)=f^{\prime\prime}(u)\,\frac{u-\rho(u,v)}{f^{\prime}(u)-f^{\prime}(v)}=f^{\prime\prime}(u)\,\frac{\int^{u}_{v}(u-s)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s}{\big(\int^{u}_{v}f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s\big)^{2}}\geq 0,

which, by the strictly increasing property of
f^{\prime}(u)
, implies that

\rho(u,v)
is strictly increasing in
u
.

Furthermore, if
(
A.4
)
holds, then

(i)

Letting
u\rightarrow c{\pm}
,

\displaystyle|f^{\prime}(u)-f^{\prime}(c)|

\displaystyle=\int^{1}_{0}f^{\prime\prime}(\theta u+(1-\theta)c)\,{\rm d}\theta\,|u-c|

\displaystyle=\int^{1}_{0}(N_{\pm}+o(1))\,|\theta(u-c)|^{\alpha_{\pm}}\,{\rm d}\theta\,|u-c|=\frac{N_{\pm}+o(1)}{1+\alpha_{\pm}}\,|u-c|^{1+\alpha_{\pm}}.

(ii)

Letting
u\rightarrow c{\pm}
, if
u>c
,

\displaystyle\int^{u}_{c}(s-c)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s=\int^{u}_{c}(s-c)(N_{\pm}+o(1))(s-c)^{\alpha_{\pm}}\,{\rm d}s=\frac{N_{\pm}+o(1)}{2+\alpha_{\pm}}(u-c)^{2+\alpha_{\pm}};

and, if
u<c
,

\displaystyle\int^{u}_{c}(s-c)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s=\int^{c}_{u}(c-s)(N_{\pm}+o(1))(c-s)^{\alpha_{\pm}}\,{\rm d}s=\frac{N_{\pm}+o(1)}{2+\alpha_{\pm}}(c-u)^{2+\alpha_{\pm}}.

(iii)

From
(
A.5
)
–
(
A.6
)
, as
u\rightarrow c{\pm}
,

\frac{\rho(u,c)-\rho(c,c)}{u-c}=\dfrac{\int^{u}_{c}(s-c)f^{\prime\prime}(s)\,{\rm d}s}{(u-c)(f^{\prime}(u)-f^{\prime}(c))}=\dfrac{\frac{N_{\pm}+o(1)}{2+\alpha_{\pm}}|u-c|^{2+\alpha_{\pm}}}{\frac{N_{\pm}+o(1)}{1+\alpha_{\pm}}|u-c|^{2+\alpha_{\pm}}}=(1+o(1))\frac{1+\alpha_{\pm}}{2+\alpha_{\pm}},

which implies
(
A.7
)
.

In summary, we have proved the properties of the flux functions of convexity degeneracy.
∎

Appendix B
Proofs of Lemmas
6.6
–
6.8

From
(
6.9
)
–
(
6.10
)
,
\Phi(x)
is
continuous on
({-}\infty,\infty)
,
and the convex hull
\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)
of
\Phi(x)
on
[-N,N]
satisfies that

\Phi(x)\geq\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)
on
[-N,N]
,
\bar{\Phi}_{N}(\pm N)=\Phi(\pm N),

and

\Phi(x)\geq\bar{\Phi}_{1}(x)\geq\cdots\geq\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)\geq\bar{\Phi}_{N+1}(x)\geq\cdots\qquad\,\,\mbox{for any $x\in\mathbb{R}$}.

Then
\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)
converges to
\bar{\Phi}(x)
pointwise on
[-\infty,\infty)
.

We now give the proofs of Lemmas
6.6
–
6.8
.

Proof of
Lemma

6.6
.

The proof is divided into two steps.

1.
Suppose that
\bar{\Phi}(x)>-\infty
,
we now prove
(
6.12
)
.
By the definition of
\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)
in
(
6.10
)
,
for any fixed
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, if
N>\max\{|x_{1}|,|x_{2}|\}
,

\bar{\Phi}_{N}(\frac{x_{1}+x_{2}}{2})\leq\frac{\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{1})+\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{2})}{2},

which, by letting
N\rightarrow\infty
, implies that
\bar{\Phi}(x)
is a convex function,
i.e.
,
for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,

(B.1)

\bar{\Phi}(\frac{x_{1}+x_{2}}{2})\leq\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x_{1})+\bar{\Phi}(x_{2})}{2}.

From
(
B.1
)
, the left- and right-derivatives
{\rm D}_{\pm}\bar{\Phi}(x)
of
\bar{\Phi}(x)

exist pointwise on
({-}\infty,\infty)
.
Denote
c:={\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(0)
. Then

\bar{\Phi}(x)\geq\bar{\Phi}(0)+cx
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}.

From
{\Phi}(x)\geq\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)\geq\bar{\Phi}(x)
,

\Phi(x)\geq\bar{\Phi}(0)+cx\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ x\in\mathbb{R},

which implies

(B.2)

\mathop{\underline{\lim}}_{x\rightarrow\pm\infty}(\Phi(x)-cx)\geq\bar{\Phi}(0)>-\infty.

2.

On the other hand, suppose that
(
B.2
)
holds for some
c\in\mathbb{R}
.
Then there exists
b_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\mathop{\underline{\lim}}_{x\rightarrow\pm\infty}(\Phi(x)-cx)\geq b_{0}.

Thus, for
N
sufficiently large,

\Phi(x)-cx\geq b_{0}-1\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ |x|>N.

Denote the minimum of
\Phi(x)-cx
on
[-N,N]
by
b_{1}
and
b:=\min\{b_{0}-1,b_{1}\}
.
Then

(B.3)

\Phi(x)\geq cx+b\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ x\in\mathbb{R},

so that, by
(
6.10
)
,

\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)\geq cx+b
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}.

By letting
N\rightarrow\infty
, this implies that

\bar{\Phi}(x)\geq cx+b>-\infty\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ x\in\mathbb{R},

as desired.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

6.7
.

We divide the proof into three steps accordingly.

1
. If
\overline{u}_{l}
and
\underline{u}_{r}
in
(
6.1
)
satisfy that
\overline{u}_{l}<\underline{u}_{r}
,
then, for any
c\in(\overline{u}_{l},\underline{u}_{r})
,

(B.4)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}(\Phi(x)-cx)=\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}x\,\Big(\frac{\Phi(x)}{x}-c\Big)=\infty,\\[11.38109pt]
\displaystyle\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow-\infty}(\Phi(x)-cx)=\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow-\infty}({-}x)\,\Big(c-\frac{\Phi(x)}{x}\Big)=\infty,\end{cases}

which, by Lemma
6.6
, implies that
\bar{\Phi}(x)>-\infty
on
x\in\mathbb{R}
.

From
(
B.4
)
, there exists
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

{\Phi}(x)-cx\geq{\Phi}(x_{0})-cx_{0}
for any
x\in\mathbb{R},

which, by
(
6.10
)
, implies that

\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)\geq cx+({\Phi}(x_{0})-cx_{0})
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}.

Then, by letting
N\rightarrow\infty
,

(B.5)

\bar{\Phi}(x)\geq cx+({\Phi}(x_{0})-cx_{0})\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ x\in\mathbb{R},

which means that

\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})\geq cx_{0}+({\Phi}(x_{0})-cx_{0})=\Phi(x_{0}).

Since
{\Phi}(x)\geq\bar{\Phi}(x)
on
\mathbb{R}
,
then
{\Phi}(x_{0})=\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})
so that
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
.

Since
{\Phi}(x)\geq\bar{\Phi}(x)
on
\mathbb{R}
, then,
for any fixed
y\in\mathbb{R}
,

(B.6)

\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(y)}{x-y}\leq\frac{{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(y)}{x-y}\ \ {\rm if}\ x>y,\qquad\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(y)}{x-y}\geq\frac{{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(y)}{x-y}\ \ {\rm if}\ x<y.

Then it follows from
(
B.1
)
that

\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(y)}{x-y}
is nondecreasing in
x\in\mathbb{R}

so that, by
(
B.6
)
,

\displaystyle{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(y)

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow y{+}}\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(y)}{x-y}\leq\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(y)}{x-y}\leq\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}\frac{{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(y)}{x-y}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}\frac{{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(y)}{x-y}\,\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}\frac{x-y}{x}+\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\bar{\Phi}(y)}{x}

(B.7)

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}\Big(\frac{{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(y)}{x-y}\,\frac{x-y}{x}+\frac{\bar{\Phi}(y)}{x}\Big)=\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}\frac{{\Phi}(x)}{x}=\underline{u}_{r}.

Similarly, it follows from
(
B.6
)
that

{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(y)\geq\overline{u}_{l}
for any
y\in\mathbb{R}
.
This implies
(
6.13
)
.

To prove
(
6.14
)
, from
(
6.13
)
, we first have

(B.8)

\inf_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}}{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})\geq\overline{u}_{l},\qquad\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}}{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})\leq\underline{u}_{r}.

On the other hand, similar to the arguments as those for
(
B.4
)
–
(
B.5
)
,
for any
c\in(\overline{u}_{l},\underline{u}_{r})
,
there exists
x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
such that

\bar{\Phi}(x)\geq cx+\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})-cx_{0}
for any
x\in\mathbb{R},

which implies

{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})=\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow x_{0}{-}}\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})}{x-x_{0}}\leq c,\qquad{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})=\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow x_{0}{+}}\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})}{x-x_{0}}\geq c.

By the arbitrariness of
c\in(\overline{u}_{l},\underline{u}_{r})
, we obtain

(B.9)

\inf_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}}{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})\leq\overline{u}_{l},\qquad\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}}{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})\geq\underline{u}_{r}.

Combining
(
B.8
)
with
(
B.9
)
,
(
6.14
)
holds.

2
. If
(
6.15
)
holds, it follows from Lemma
6.6
that

\bar{\Phi}(x)>-\infty
on
x\in\mathbb{R}
.

On the other hand, suppose that
\bar{\Phi}(x)>-\infty
on
x\in\mathbb{R}
.
From Lemma
6.6
,
there exists
c\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\mathop{\underline{\lim}}_{x\rightarrow\pm\infty}(\Phi(x)-cx)>-\infty
,
and hence
(
B.3
)
holds.
Then, to prove
(
6.15
)
, it suffices to show that
c=\underline{u}_{r}
.
In fact, from
(
B.3
)
,

\frac{\Phi(x)}{x}\geq\frac{b}{x}+c\quad{\rm if}\ x>0,\qquad\frac{\Phi(x)}{x}\leq\frac{b}{x}+c\quad{\rm if}\ x<0,

which implies

\overline{u}_{l}=\mathop{\overline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow-\infty}\frac{\Phi(x)}{x}\leq c\leq\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\Phi(x)}{x}=\underline{u}_{r}.

This, by
\overline{u}_{l}=\underline{u}_{r}
, yields that
c=\underline{u}_{r}
.

By the same arguments as those for
(
B.6
)
–
(
B.7
)
,
it can be checked that

{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(y)={\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(y)=\underline{u}_{r}
for any
y\in\mathbb{R},

which implies that

\bar{\Phi}(x)=\underline{u}_{r}\,x+b_{0}
for some
b_{0}\in\mathbb{R}.

Thus, if there exists
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\Phi(x_{0})=\underline{u}_{r}x_{0}+b_{0}
,
then
\mathcal{K}_{0}\neq\varnothing
;
and, if
\Phi(x)>\underline{u}_{r}x+b_{0}
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
then
\mathcal{K}_{0}=\varnothing
.

3
. By the definition of
\overline{u}_{l}
and
\underline{u}_{r}
,
for any
c>\underline{u}_{r}
,

\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}(\Phi(x)-cx)=\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}x\Big(\frac{\Phi(x)}{x}-c\Big)=-\infty,

and, for any
c<\overline{u}_{l}
,

\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow-\infty}(\Phi(x)-cx)=\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow-\infty}({-}x)\Big(c-\frac{\Phi(x)}{x}\Big)=-\infty.

Then it follows from
\overline{u}_{l}>\underline{u}_{r}
that, for any
c\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow\infty}(\Phi(x)-cx)=-\infty\qquad{\rm or}\qquad\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{x\rightarrow-\infty}(\Phi(x)-cx)=-\infty,

which, by Lemma
6.6
, implies that
\bar{\Phi}(x)\equiv-\infty
on
\mathbb{R}
.

Up to now, we have proved Lemma
6.7
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

6.8
.

Suppose that
\bar{\Phi}(x)>-\infty
on
x\in\mathbb{R}
.
From
(
B.1
)
,
\bar{\Phi}(x)
is convex.

1
. For any fixed
x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
,
\Phi(x_{0})=\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})
.
Since
\Phi(x)\geq\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)\geq\bar{\Phi}(x)
, then

(B.10)

{\Phi}(x_{0})=\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})=\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ N.

(a).

We first prove that,

(B.11)

\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})=\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}{\Phi}(x_{0}),\qquad\sup_{l<0}\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})=\sup_{l<0}\Delta_{l}{\Phi}(x_{0}).

In fact, since
\Phi(x)\geq\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)
, by
(
B.10
)
,
for any fixed
N
,

\frac{{\Phi}(x_{0}+l)-{\Phi}(x_{0})}{l}\geq\frac{\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0}+l)-\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})}{l}\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ l>0,

which implies

(B.12)

\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}{\Phi}(x_{0})\geq\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0}).

Furthermore, for any
l>0
, if
x_{0}+l\geq N
,
by
(
6.10
)
,
\Phi(x_{0}+l)=\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0}+l)
so that, by
(
B.10
)
,

(B.13)

\frac{\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0}+l)-\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})}{l}=\frac{{\Phi}(x_{0}+l)-{\Phi}(x_{0})}{l}\geq\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}{\Phi}(x_{0});

and, if
x_{0}+l<N
,
(
B.13
)
holds when
\Phi(x_{0}+l)=\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0}+l)
.
For the case that
\Phi(x_{0}+l)>\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0}+l)
with
x_{0}+l<N
,
by the definition of convex hull and
(
B.10
)
,
there exists
\bar{l}>0
with
x_{0}+\bar{l}\leq N
such that

(B.14)

\frac{\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0}+l)-\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})}{l}=\frac{{\Phi}(x_{0}+\bar{l})-{\Phi}(x_{0})}{\bar{l}}\geq\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}{\Phi}(x_{0}).

Then it follows from
(
B.13
)
–
(
B.14
)
that

(B.15)

\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})\geq\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}{\Phi}(x_{0}).

Combining
(
B.12
)
with
(
B.15
)
, it follows that
\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})=\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}{\Phi}(x_{0})
in
(
B.11
)
.
Similarly, it can be checked that

\sup_{l<0}\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})=\sup_{l<0}\Delta_{l}{\Phi}(x_{0})
.
Hence,
(
B.11
)
holds.

(b).
We now prove
(
6.16
)
.
Since
\bar{\Phi}(x)
is convex on
({-}\infty,\infty)
,
then
\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})
is nondecreasing so that

{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})=\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}),\qquad\,\,{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})=\sup_{l<0}\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}).

Suppose that
x_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
.
Since
\Phi(x)\geq\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)\geq\bar{\Phi}(x)
on
\mathbb{R}
,
it is direct to check from
(
B.10
)
that

\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})\leq\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})\quad{\rm for}\ l>0,\qquad\,\,\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})\geq\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})\quad{\rm for}\ l<0,

which, by
(
B.11
)
, implies

(B.16)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})=\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})\leq\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})=\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}{\Phi}(x_{0}),\\[11.38109pt]
\displaystyle{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})=\sup_{l<0}\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})\geq\sup_{l<0}\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})=\sup_{l<0}\Delta_{l}{\Phi}(x_{0}).\end{cases}

Then, to prove
(
6.16
)
,
it suffices to prove the inverse inequalities in
(
B.16
)
.
In fact, for any given
R>|x_{0}|
,

\{\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)\}_{N}
is a nonincreasing sequence of continuous functions on
[-R,R]

and converges to
\bar{\Phi}(x)
pointwise so that

\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)
converges to
\bar{\Phi}(x)
uniformly on
[-R,R]
.
Thus, for any fixed
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
N_{0}>0
such that,
for any
N>N_{0}
,

0\leq\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(x)<\varepsilon\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ x\in[-R,R],

which, by
(
B.11
)
, implies that, if
x\in(x_{0},R)
, then

\frac{\varepsilon+\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})}{x-x_{0}}>\frac{\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)-\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})}{x-x_{0}}\geq\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})=\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}{\Phi}(x_{0}),

and, if
x\in({-}R,x_{0})
, then

\frac{\varepsilon+\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})}{x-x_{0}}<\frac{\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)-\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})}{x-x_{0}}\leq\sup_{l<0}\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x_{0})=\sup_{l<0}\Delta_{l}{\Phi}(x_{0}).

By the arbitrariness of
\varepsilon>0
, we obtain

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})}{x-x_{0}}\geq\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}{\Phi}(x_{0})\quad&{\rm if}\ x\in(x_{0},R),\\[11.38109pt]
\displaystyle\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})}{x-x_{0}}\leq\sup_{l<0}\Delta_{l}{\Phi}(x_{0})\quad&{\rm if}\ x\in({-}R,x_{0}),\end{cases}

which, by letting
x\rightarrow x_{0}{+}
and
x\rightarrow x_{0}{-}
respectively, implies

(B.17)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})=\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow x_{0}{+}}\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})}{x-x_{0}}\geq\inf_{l>0}\Delta_{l}{\Phi}(x_{0}),\\[11.38109pt]
\displaystyle{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})=\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow x_{0}{-}}\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(x_{0})}{x-x_{0}}\leq\sup_{l<0}\Delta_{l}{\Phi}(x_{0}).\end{cases}

We combine
(
B.16
)
with
(
B.17
)
to conclude
(
6.16
)
.

2
.
Since
\Phi(x)
and
\bar{\Phi}(x)
are both continuous functions,

\mathcal{K}_{0}
in
(
6.11
)
is a closed set so that

\mathcal{K}^{c}_{0}=\mathbb{R}-\mathcal{K}_{0}
is an open set.

(a).
Suppose that
x^{+}_{0}=\sup\mathcal{K}_{0}<\infty
.
Since
\mathcal{K}_{0}
is a closed set, then
x^{+}_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
.
Since
{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x)
is nondecreasing,
it follows from
(
6.14
)
that
{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x^{+}_{0})=\underline{u}_{r}
.

By the nondecreasing of

\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}(x^{+}_{0})=\frac{1}{l}\big(\bar{\Phi}(x^{+}_{0}+l)-{\Phi}(x^{+}_{0})\big)
in
l\neq 0
,
for
l>0
,

\displaystyle\underline{u}_{r}

\displaystyle={\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(x^{+}_{0})\leq\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}(x^{+}_{0})\leq\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}(x^{+}_{0})

\displaystyle=\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}\Big(\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x^{+}_{0}+l)}{x^{+}_{0}+l}\,\frac{x^{+}_{0}+l}{l}-\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x^{+}_{0})}{l}\Big)=\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x^{+}_{0}+l)}{x^{+}_{0}+l}\leq\mathop{\underline{\lim}}\limits_{l\rightarrow\infty}\frac{{\Phi}(x^{+}_{0}+l)}{x^{+}_{0}+l}=\underline{u}_{r},

which implies that, for any
x=x_{0}^{+}+l>x_{0}^{+}
,

\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\Phi(x_{0}^{+})}{x-x_{0}^{+}}=\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}^{+})}{x-x_{0}^{+}}=\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}(x^{+}_{0})=\underline{u}_{r}.

This implies
(
6.17
)
.

(b).
Suppose that
x^{-}_{0}=\inf\mathcal{K}_{0}>-\infty
.
Similar to
(a)
,
then
x^{-}_{0}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
and
{\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x^{-}_{0})=\overline{u}_{l}
.

By the nondecreasing of

\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}(x^{-}_{0})=\frac{1}{l}\big(\bar{\Phi}(x^{-}_{0}+l)-{\Phi}(x^{-}_{0})\big)
in
l\neq 0
,
for
l<0
,

\displaystyle\overline{u}_{l}

\displaystyle={\rm D}_{-}\bar{\Phi}(x^{-}_{0})\geq\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}(x^{-}_{0})\geq\lim_{l\rightarrow-\infty}\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}(x^{-}_{0})

\displaystyle=\lim_{l\rightarrow-\infty}\Big(\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x^{-}_{0}+l)}{x^{-}_{0}+l}\,\frac{x^{-}_{0}+l}{l}-\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x^{-}_{0})}{l}\Big)=\lim_{l\rightarrow-\infty}\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x^{-}_{0}+l)}{x^{-}_{0}+l}

\displaystyle\geq\mathop{\overline{\lim}}_{l\rightarrow-\infty}\frac{{\Phi}(x^{-}_{0}+l)}{x^{-}_{0}+l}=\overline{u}_{l},

which implies that, for any
x=x_{0}^{-}+l<x_{0}^{-}
,

\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\Phi(x_{0}^{-})}{x-x_{0}^{-}}=\frac{\bar{\Phi}(x)-\bar{\Phi}(x_{0}^{-})}{x-x_{0}^{-}}=\Delta_{l}\bar{\Phi}(x^{-}_{0})=\overline{u}_{l}.

This implies
(
6.18
)
.

(c).
Since the bounded interval
(e_{n},h_{n})
contains in

\mathcal{K}^{c}_{0}
with
e_{n},h_{n}\in\mathcal{K}_{0}
, then

(B.18)

\begin{cases}{\Phi}(x)>\bar{\Phi}(x)\qquad\quad{\rm for\ any}\ x\in(e_{n},h_{n}),\\[2.84526pt]
{\Phi}(e_{n})=\bar{\Phi}(e_{n}),\quad{\Phi}(h_{n})=\bar{\Phi}(h_{n}),\end{cases}

which, by
{\Phi}(x)\geq\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)\geq\bar{\Phi}(x)
,
implies that, for any
N>0
,

{\Phi}(e_{n})=\bar{\Phi}_{N}(e_{n})=\bar{\Phi}(e_{n}),\qquad{\Phi}(h_{n})=\bar{\Phi}_{N}(h_{n})=\bar{\Phi}(h_{n}).

Then it follows from
(
6.10
)
that,
if
N>\max\{|e_{n}|,|h_{n}|\}
,

\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)\big|_{[e_{n},\,h_{n}]}=\mathop{\rm cov}_{[e_{n},\,h_{n}]}\Phi(x)\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ x\in[e_{n},h_{n}],

which, by letting
N\rightarrow\infty
, implies

(B.19)

\bar{\Phi}(x)\big|_{[e_{n},\,h_{n}]}=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\bar{\Phi}_{N}(x)\big|_{[e_{n},\,h_{n}]}=\mathop{\rm cov}_{[e_{n},\,h_{n}]}\Phi(x)\qquad{\rm for\ any}\ x\in[e_{n},h_{n}].

Combining
(
B.18
)
with
(
B.19
)
, we obtain

\begin{cases}\displaystyle{\Phi}(x)>\mathop{\rm cov}\limits_{[e_{n},\,h_{n}]}\Phi(x)\qquad\quad{\rm for\ any}\ x\in(e_{n},\,h_{n}),\\[11.38109pt]
\displaystyle{\Phi}(e_{n})=\mathop{\rm cov}\limits_{[e_{n},\,h_{n}]}\Phi(e_{n}),\quad{\Phi}(h_{n})=\mathop{\rm cov}\limits_{[e_{n},\,h_{n}]}\Phi(h_{n}),\end{cases}

which, by the definition of the convex hull and
(
B.19
)
, implies that,
for any
x\in[e_{n},h_{n}]
,

\displaystyle\bar{\Phi}(x)=\mathop{\rm cov}\limits_{[e_{n},\,h_{n}]}\Phi(x)

\displaystyle={\Phi}(e_{n})+\frac{{\Phi}(h_{n})-{\Phi}(e_{n})}{h_{n}-e_{n}}(x-e_{n})={\Phi}(e_{n})+{\rm D}_{+}\bar{\Phi}(e_{n})(x-e_{n}).

This means that
(
6.19
)
holds.

To sum up, we have completed the proof of Lemma
6.8
.
∎
